# Stratum³ outreach shortlist

466 companies that passed the 7-gate eligibility check and were scored against the 200-signal library with coverage >= 40%.

`fit` is a weighted blend of per-category fit, counting only signals that were actually resolved. `cov` is the share of assessed signals resolved — read them together: a high fit at low coverage is a thin scan, not a good company.

## Meet (25)

Strongest evidence of thesis fit. Worth an intro conversation.

**Finologee** — Finologee has unusually credible founder-market history, a verified production customer base and strong regulated SaaS infrastructure. The meeting should focus on investability because stage, ownership and total capital are opaque, and on whether the product can expand beyond Luxembourg-centric TradFi workflows.
  - Licences: Luxembourg Support PFS licence
  - Found via: LHoFT Finologee profile
  - Check before the call: Export the entity-specific Finologee row from the CSSF JavaScript register and archive it; licence 06/19 is corroborated, but the official register does not expose a static entity URL.; Obtain a cap table, full financing history and confirmation of whether the company is seeking institutional venture capital.

**Eunice (Reasoon Limited)** — Strong live institutional traction, confirmed FCA sandbox engagement, credible seed leads and unusually strong founder execution history offset a closed product surface and limited public security/compliance proof.
  - Licences: FCA Regulatory Sandbox accepted firm, 2025
  - Found via: FCA Regulatory Sandbox accepted firms
  - Check before the call: Obtain current ARR, net retention, ACV and customer concentration.; Request security audit, penetration-test, model-governance and data-residency evidence.

**Evrotrust** — Evrotrust combines unusually strong regulatory proof, live bank deployments and a current core-sized Series A with direct EUDI Wallet relevance. Founder pedigree misses the fund's Tier-1 FI requirement, so the meeting should test domain access and DACH execution rather than licence credibility.
  - Licences: EU qualified trust service provider; EU-notified electronic identification scheme
  - Found via: Romania and Bulgaria fintech report
  - Check before the call: Reconcile the user's €13.7m total with €12.4m found across disclosed €2.5m, €3.3m and €6.6m rounds.; Verify the full founder and executive employment histories from primary profiles.

**spektr** — spektr has crossed from promising tooling into live bank infrastructure, with Santander Leasing production evidence, fast releases and credible security attestations. The founder exit and investor quality offset weak Tier-1-bank pedigree, but the €23m total already puts cheque fit in stretch territory. Meet promptly and diligence retention, AI-control governance and the economics of bespoke implementations.
  - Found via: Copenhagen Fintech current members
  - Check before the call: Verify founder employment histories and whether any founder held a qualifying Tier-1 financial-institution role.; Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate and SOC 2 Type II report directly and confirm current scope and expiry.

**Deon Digital** — Deon is an unusually direct thesis match with a Tier-1 financial-infrastructure founder, Danish FSA sandbox validation and a live Bursa Malaysia deployment. The score is pulled down by stale public product/funding cadence, a highly fragmented cap table and limited enterprise-security and contract disclosure. It claims no licence and appears to act as a software vendor rather than the regulated operator, so the absence of a FINMA/FCA/BaFin/CASP entry is not a veto.
  - Found via: IFZ FinTech Study 2026 company directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm current ARR, gross margin, contracted backlog and the number of paying production financial-institution customers.; Obtain the current cap table, identify the 2023 lead investor and quantify founder ownership after more than 120 shareholders.

**Kayna** — Kayna combines a repeat-founder exit, live multinational insurance distribution, recent seed financing and unusually strong regulatory engagement for its size. The score is capped by the absence of a Tier-1 FI founder and thin public security assurance, both diligence priorities rather than licence vetoes.
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab Cohort 9
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current ISO 27001/SOC 2 roadmap, penetration-test summary and subprocessor list.; Verify production transaction volume, recurring revenue, gross retention and revenue concentration across WTW-linked programmes.

**360X AG** — The live BaFin/ESMA-regulated venue has excellent thesis alignment, credible founder history and real institutional production trades. Its score is held back by thin public security assurance and founder-team depth evidence. The undisclosed sizes of two 2024 rounds prevent confirmation of the €30m capital ceiling, so capital diligence is mandatory before a meeting.
  - Licences: BaFin DLT multilateral trading facility permission, 29 Apr 2025; LEI 529900M42L36KXZ1JD69
  - Found via: ESMA list of authorised DLT market infrastructures
  - Check before the call: Obtain the amounts of the September and October 2024 Seed financings and current fully diluted cap table.; Verify shareholder voting control and confirm 360X is not consolidated as an incumbent subsidiary.

**21X AG** — The first-in-EU DLT TSS permission is verified in ESMA's register, the venue is live and institutional partners are named. The score is constrained by thin founder pedigree, customer-originated proof and public technical diligence material.
  - Licences: EU DLT Pilot Regime DLT TSS permission, BaFin
  - Found via: ESMA Authorised DLT Market Infrastructures
  - Check before the call: Confirm current cap table, cumulative funding and ownership after Bank Frick's dilution.; Obtain participant volumes, recurring revenue and customer-originated production references.

**7Analytics** — 7Analytics has strong insurer traction, strategic CVC backing and rare central-bank validation of its data. However, the team has no financial-services pedigree and the product is adjacent risk data rather than core regulated rails, while technical transparency is limited. Monitor as a thesis-edge insurtech data asset rather than a priority infrastructure deal.
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab Cohort 14
  - Check before the call: Verify ARR, net retention, insurer production revenue and revenue concentration.; Confirm information-security certification, data governance and model-risk controls.

**RULEMATCH AG** — The verified MiCA licence, Tier-1-bank founder pedigree and live institutional access network make RULEMATCH highly thesis-relevant. Evidence of production use exists, but public customer outcomes, company security certification and fresh financing are weak. Monitor closely and request diligence materials before a meeting decision.
  - Licences: Liechtenstein FMA MiCA authorisation via RULEMATCH Europe AG
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: IFZ FinTech Study 2026 company directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm cumulative capital raised and whether a 2025–2026 round closed privately.; Obtain the CASP passporting map and clarify regulated responsibilities between the Swiss and Liechtenstein entities.

**21 Analytics AG** — Excellent Travel Rule specialisation, real regulated-custodian deployments and standards participation are offset by weak founder/investor evidence, narrow disclosed geography and no independently verifiable ISO certificate details.
  - Licences: ISO 27001:2022 certified; states DORA and NIS2 readiness
  - Found via: INATBA members directory (medium tier)
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate, certificate number, certification body and current validity date.; Confirm cumulative external capital raised and the present financing stage.

**Quantoz Payments** — Quantoz has a register-verified EMI licence, broad EEA passports, current MiCA whitepapers and credible live distribution through regulated venues. It lacks the fund's original-founder FI pedigree, disclosed stage and disclosed capital total, while public enterprise security evidence is incomplete. The 0.4211 fit includes a 0.12 anti-signal deduction.
  - Licences: De Nederlandsche Bank Electronic Money Institution (EEA passport); MiCA EMT issuer
  - Found via: Fabric Ventures portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain total group and Payments-entity capital raised, ownership allocation and current round stage.; Request the ISO 27001 certificate, ISAE 3402 report, DORA control map and latest penetration-test summary.

**Skribble AG** — Skribble has real enterprise deployments, regulated-partner integrations and strong DACH commercial proof, but it is horizontal e-signature infrastructure rather than a finance-native rail. The founder evidence does not confirm the library's two-year Tier-1-bank threshold, and the last disclosed financing was in 2022. Monitor for a finance-specific wedge, EUDIW roadmap and a new round.
  - Licences: Qualified electronic signatures under Swiss ZertES and EU eIDAS through regulated trust-service partners
  - Found via: IFZ FinTech Study Switzerland
  - Check before the call: Confirm Philipp Dick's exact UBS employment dates to resolve the Tier-1-bank alumni threshold.; Obtain the current ISO 27001 certificate, certification body, scope and expiry.

**fundcraft** — Regulatory substance, quantified Moonfare deployment and strong specialist investors make this a credible institutional operator. The founder category is materially below thesis because no founder has verified Tier-1 FI, regulator, payment-network or qualifying crypto-infrastructure pedigree. Monitor for fresh financing, post-founder-CTO technical continuity and evidence that the product can scale beyond regulated fund administration services.
  - Licences: Luxembourg authorised AIFM, CSSF code A00003222; Luxembourg specialised PSF, CSSF code P00000501
  - Found via: SIX FinTech Ventures current portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the underlying OCA Global ISO certificate number, validity dates and complete scope rather than relying only on the company announcement.; Confirm current CSSF entries P00000501 and A00003222 directly in Search Entities; the accessible national passport registers independently corroborate AIFM status.

**Finqware** — Finqware combines a real PI licence, cross-border passports, public APIs, enterprise pricing and production bank deployments. The main thesis gap is that it remains conventional PSD2/open-banking infrastructure rather than digital-asset infrastructure, and founder regulatory pedigree is limited.
  - Licences: Payment institution authorised by the National Bank of Romania
  - Found via: Romania and Bulgaria fintech report
  - Check before the call: Reconcile total capital and valuation across the reported €2m prior and €3m 2026 financing.; Retrieve a direct current EBA central-register extract and the NBR licence number.

**Fipto** — Fipto has excellent thesis alignment, verified dual regulation and fresh production evidence through Kyriba, Ledger, Mantu and Melanion Digital. The weighted score is held back by founder-threshold strictness, dated financing and incomplete security-certificate evidence; monitor pending proof of current fundraise plans, certificate scope and named compliance leadership.
  - Licences: AMF MiCA PSCA A2026-009; ACPR payment institution
  - Found via: AMF PSCA white list
  - Check before the call: Reconcile the €15m company disclosure with FT Partners' separate 2022 $2m and 2023 approximately $11m financing entries.; Obtain the current ISO/IEC 27001 certificate, accredited certification body, number, scope and surveillance status.

**NoFrixion** — The live, passported EMI and production-grade ledger/API stack are rare Seed-stage regulated infrastructure assets, reinforced by founder exit experience and quantified enterprise deployments. Founder pedigree misses the fund's Tier-1 FI criterion and funding is stale, so the meeting should focus on runway, current ARR and compliance leadership.
  - Licences: Central Bank of Ireland electronic money institution, reference C458163, authorised 25 July 2023
  - Found via: Central Bank of Ireland EMI register
  - Check before the call: Confirm current ARR, payment volume, runway and whether any financing occurred after October 2022.; Verify the current MiCAR/CASP status of the 2023 VASP registration and whether crypto services remain offered.

**AxonIQ** — AxonIQ has excellent open-source adoption, fresh financing and credible production use in banks and payments. It misses the thesis's founder-background requirement, and the public funding total lacks an upper bound needed to classify cheque fit. Monitor pending cap-table and exact total-capital diligence.
  - Found via: AVP current portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the exact cumulative capital raised and fully diluted cap table.; Verify current ISO 27001/SOC 2 evidence and any external penetration-test report.

**Enable Banking** — Live regulated infrastructure, strong product transparency and credible production usage are offset by a founding team outside the fund's Tier-1 FI/digital-asset alumni thesis. The company is capital-efficient and cash-positive, but its last disclosed round is old.
  - Licences: Finnish AISP/PISP permissions under PSD2, passported in the EEA
  - Found via: Helsinki Fintech Farm company directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the actual ISO 27001 certificate, certification body and certificate number.; Verify current cap table and whether any post-2022 capital was raised.

**Suade Labs** — Suade has unusually strong founder pedigree and official BIS, ECB and Bank of England validation, plus live bank deployments and a durable open data standard. The score is held back by anonymous case studies, old and undisclosed funding, thin public API/security verification, and no digital-asset rail. Monitor pending capital disclosure and product-level diligence.
  - Found via: Innovate Finance member directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the 2021 Series A amount and current fully diluted cap table.; Review the actual ISO 27001 certificate and SOC 2 Type II report under NDA.

**Ctrl Alt** — Ctrl Alt has converted a modest seed base into unusual regulatory and production momentum: a register-verified active VARA licence, BoE/FCA DSS entry, UK and Irish authorisation claims, $1.7bn-plus tokenised and a live government land-registry deployment. The diligence case is held back by thin public API/security evidence, no Tier-1 bank production reference and an undisclosed amount for the 2025 strategic investment. Meet to verify the new FCA/Irish permissions directly, current revenue quality and financing history.
  - Licences: UK Digital Securities Sandbox, Gate 1 passed 4 Dec 2025 as Ctrl Alt Ltd (originally Alt Ltd, Gate 1 26 Mar 2025)
  - Found via: Bank of England Digital Securities Sandbox Dashboard (Gate 1 SANs)
  - Check before the call: Export the FCA Register record for FRN 1045023 and the Central Bank of Ireland record for C00571300, including permissions, restrictions and effective dates.; Reconcile the company's current 'over $6m raised' statement with the only quantified £2.2m equity round and obtain the fully diluted cap table.

**Solvimon** — Solvimon has exceptional founder-market fit from Adyen, strong API and integration infrastructure, and quantified production adoption across European fintech customers. The score is held below moderate after the anti-signal deduction because the most recent disclosed financing is over two years old and no current revenue or growth figures are public. It merits a monitored relationship and an update call focused on 2025–2026 ARR, retention, capital needs and payment-regulatory perimeter.
  - Found via: Northzone portfolio
  - Check before the call: Current ARR, net revenue retention, gross margin and 2025–2026 growth are undisclosed.; No financing after November 2023 is public; runway and current fundraising status need confirmation.

**Applied Blockchain** — Applied Blockchain has deep institutional delivery evidence, current security certification, patents, open-source privacy infrastructure and a founder with Lloyds experience. Its strongest thesis fit is Silent Data, now supporting regulated tokenised-fund infrastructure, while its broader business remains partly project-development-led. The stale financing history and lack of quantified bank case studies warrant monitoring rather than an immediate meeting.
  - Found via: Level39 member directory (Blockchain)
  - Check before the call: Confirm current financing stage, profitability, recurring Silent Data ARR and whether the company is actively fundraising.; Verify the exact duration and role of Adi Ben-Ari's Lloyds Banking Group tenure.

**Komgo** — Komgo has exceptional institutional adoption, bank-originated references, production connectivity and quantified workflow impact. Founder thesis fit is real, but stage and cumulative-capital ambiguity plus an unusually broad shareholder base weaken investability confidence. Verify cap table, total paid-in capital and current round status before a meeting.
  - Found via: Rabo Investments corporate-venturing portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the complete cap table and cumulative paid-in capital across all rounds, including undisclosed 2018-2020 and 2023 investments.; Resolve the conflicting Seed, Series A and Series B labels with Komgo.

**Strise** — Strise has unusually strong Nordic production traction and a technically credible API/data platform, but none of the founders has the required Tier-1-FI or digital-asset-infrastructure pedigree. The stale Series A, headcount contraction, 59-holder cap table and 2026 enforcement lien warrant financial-health diligence before engagement. Monitor rather than meet until runway, lien resolution and growth reacceleration are confirmed.
  - Found via: Finance Innovation Norway KYC/AML group
  - Check before the call: Ask management to explain the March 2026 enforcement lien and provide evidence it has been discharged.; Reconcile official 30-person headcount with prior claims of nearly 50 employees.

**FinanceKey** — Strong product evidence, named multinational customers, bank integrations and quantified ROI offset a founder set rooted in corporate treasury rather than a named Tier-1 FI. The licence claim remains unconfirmed at record level and public compliance leadership is immature. Monitor pending direct FIN-FSA verification and compliance diligence.
  - Licences: Finnish registered Account Information Service Provider under PSD2
  - ⚠️ Review flags: AISP status is claimed and links to FIN-FSA, but the dynamic public register did not yield a record-level company result; verify licence status and scope manually before investment review.; No named MLRO or substantive public AML policy was found for this regulated AISP.
  - Found via: Helsinki Fintech Farm company directory
  - Check before the call: Retrieve FinanceKey Oy's exact active FIN-FSA/EBA AISP register row, registration reference, services and passporting scope.; Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate, certification body, certificate number, issue and expiry dates.

**OpenTrade** — OpenTrade is a rare high-thesis-fit infrastructure company with Tier-1 bank and USDC standards pedigree, quantified multi-country production traction and strong recurring-investor support. It is already in the stretch cheque tier and regulatory/security diligence must verify the Five Sigma/Capricorn perimeter and obtain audit/certification evidence before investment.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: REGULATORY-PERIMETER REVIEW: Capricorn Fund Managers is FCA-authorised (FRN 505252), but Five Sigma's claimed appointed-representative status was not independently found in the public register.
  - Found via: CMCC Global portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the FCA register extract confirming Five Sigma Finance Ltd as Capricorn's appointed representative and map permissions to every product.; Confirm exact cumulative funding in EUR and whether the May 2026 strategic round changes formal stage.

**Rhinestone** — Rhinestone is a technically strong, standards-shaping wallet infrastructure company with excellent specialist investors and a core-sized capital profile. The score is held down by founder-thesis mismatch, limited enterprise assurance and no named FI production customer. Monitor for institutional conversion, certification and clearer separation of infrastructure revenue from the emerging B2C wallet.
  - Found via: 1kx portfolio
  - Check before the call: Confirm the exact UK operating legal entity and current Companies House filing record.; Obtain named production users, transaction volume, API revenue and customer concentration.

**Futurae** — Excellent technical depth, multi-country bank deployments and strategic investors are held back by no current public ISO proof and limited regulatory/standards evidence.
  - Found via: AXA Venture Partners Futurae portfolio materials
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current ISO 27001 certificate and recent penetration-test executive summary.; Reconcile CHF15m total funding with the CHF7m itemised disclosed rounds and identify any undisclosed later financing.

**Aktionariat** — Aktionariat is a direct tokenisation-rails fit with an exceptional policy-connected founder, real transaction volume and unusually transparent open-source infrastructure. Its commercial footprint remains Switzerland-heavy, institutional customers are scarce and the April 2026 exploit is a serious diligence issue despite full reimbursement. Monitor pending an independent post-incident audit and a clear regulatory-perimeter opinion.
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: Capital Markets and Technology Association members
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current FINMA/SRO perimeter opinion covering Brokerbot, stablecoin reserve flows, order matching and secondary trading.; Review both historical smart-contract audits plus the post-April-2026 re-audit, incident timeline and continuous-monitoring controls.

**Axiology** — The regulatory moat is real and unusually strong for a €7m seed company, and the founder brings exceptional central-bank credibility. However, the score is held back by thin public developer/security proof and geographically concentrated early commercial evidence. Reassess when a named Tier-1 institution deploys in production or the company publishes stronger technical assurance.
  - Licences: Bank of Lithuania B-category investment firm authorisation LB002300; EU DLT Pilot Regime trading and settlement system permission
  - Found via: Bank of Lithuania financial market participants
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate, certification body, scope and expiry directly from management.; Verify ARR, fee model, active institutional counterparties and production transaction volumes.

**CryptoNext Security** — The cryptography research pedigree, NIST validation, central-bank references and investor syndicate are unusually strong. However, the founding team lacks the thesis-required FI or digital-asset-infrastructure background, commercial bank evidence remains pilot-heavy, and the product is broader cybersecurity rather than a clear TradFi-digital-asset rail.
  - Found via: AXA Venture Partners CryptoNext investment
  - Check before the call: Confirm whether Crédit Agricole, Allianz or another bank has converted a proof of concept into paid production.; Obtain current ARR, 2024-2026 funding details, customer concentration and sales pipeline.

**WealthArc AG** — WealthArc is live, independent and has unusually concrete production traction, quantified ROI, global custodian coverage and scoped ISO 27001 certification. The fit is held back by missing public developer infrastructure, weak institutional VC validation and no explicit tokenisation or regulated-rails bridge. Monitor for a fresh financing, independently verified bank deployments and deeper digital-asset connectivity.
  - Found via: IFZ FinTech Study Switzerland
  - Check before the call: Resolve conflicting total-funding estimates from the current cap table and bank statements.; Verify Gogol's exact Vontobel tenure and whether it satisfies the two-year Tier-1 threshold.

**enclaive** — Exceptional cryptography pedigree, current ISO claim, working open-source artefacts and a major public tender establish technical credibility. The narrow fit concern is structural: enclaive is horizontal cybersecurity, with no Tier-1 FI founder or named financial-institution deployment. Monitor until financial-sector production evidence or a finance-specific go-to-market becomes public.
  - Licences: ISO/IEC 27001
  - Found via: Dealroom confidential-computing company directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the actual ISO/IEC 27001 certificate, scope, certificate number and expiry from Proks.; Confirm enclaive's economics and exact workshare in the €30.8m GovTech framework.

**REGnosys** — REGnosys has elite Goldman founder pedigree, real production use, strong standards leverage and unusually credible regulatory engagement. It is nevertheless adjacent to rather than centred on tokenisation or digital assets, and the undisclosed minority investment prevents a clean capital-ceiling confirmation. Monitor as a high-quality regulated-rails exception and clarify ownership before a meeting decision.
  - Found via: FINOS Common Domain Model community
  - Check before the call: Identify the minority investor, investment date, amount, ownership percentage and control rights.; Obtain named customer references and isolate Rosetta production use from generic ISDA DRR adoption.

**Dfns** — Dfns has the strongest product and enterprise evidence in the set: current public API/OpenAPI/Postman assets, multi-model key control, credible security assurance, live compliance integrations, more than 400 customers and IBM/Standard Chartered-grade references. The founder category is less externally evidenced and the cumulative capital is already near the strategy ceiling. Meet, but treat the cheque as stretch and verify ARR, margin and ownership before advancing.
  - Licences: Former AMF PSAN E2023-108 via DFNS Custody SAS; current MiCA status unknown
  - Found via: AMF PSAN white-list record
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001/27017/27018 certificates, SOC 2 Type II report and current penetration-test summaries under NDA.; Confirm fully diluted cap table and exact cumulative cash raised, including whether the $1m pre-seed is additive.

**ClearToken** — ClearToken combines an official BoE DSS Gate-1 record, a live $225m first settlement cycle and unusually deep market-infrastructure leadership. The FCA permissions are documented by the company but still need a firm-specific register export; DSS Gate 1 does not permit live CSD/FMI activity and the clearing house remains subject to future authorisation. Meet to verify permissions, revenue conversion, regulatory capital and the path into CSD/CCP economics.
  - Licences: FCA Authorised Payment Institution, FRN 1017352; FCA cryptoasset registration; Bank of England/FCA Digital Securities Sandbox Gate 1
  - Found via: Bank of England Digital Securities Sandbox dashboard
  - Check before the call: Obtain direct FCA Register exports for FRNs 1016940 and 1017352, including permission restrictions and effective dates.; Clarify the ownership and purpose of ClearToken Holdings Limited (BVI) versus the UK holding and regulated operating entities.

**Banfico** — Banfico has genuine standards influence, strong founder payments experience, public API documentation and verified production use by a financial institution. EPC qualification is real but is a scheme qualification, not a regulatory licence; no licence veto applies because Banfico supplies technology rather than executing regulated payments. The dated, undisclosed financing record and lack of quantified Tier-1 cases justify monitoring pending ownership and revenue diligence.
  - Licences: EPC Verification of Payee Routing and Verification Mechanism qualification
  - Found via: Level39 member directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm total capital raised, profitability status and whether the 2018 Seed label remains economically meaningful.; Obtain a named-customer schedule by country, production volumes, net revenue retention and independently quantified outcomes.

**Aisot Technologies AG** — Aisot has unusually strong research depth, credible institutional production use and efficient validation from Tenity, Haute, Finovate and regulated fund managers. It nevertheless misses the thesis-critical founder-employer pedigree and publishes limited security/compliance assurance for an institutional platform. Monitor for a financing event, a Tier-1 bank deployment and stronger assurance artefacts.
  - Found via: IFZ FinTech Study Switzerland
  - Check before the call: Confirm whether the December 2025 capital increase was a new external round and update total funding.; Obtain current ARR, retention, customer count and named bank/private-bank production references.

**AUTHADA** — AUTHADA is eligible, independent and commercially live at meaningful German bank-network scale. It benefits from a Tier-1 bank alumnus co-founder, BSI/eID positioning, privacy-by-design architecture, production references and strategic investors. The score remains weak because current ISO/BSI certificate identifiers are not independently exposed, the developer/security surface is mostly gated, geographic traction is concentrated in Germany and funding recency is poor.
  - Licences: BSI-certified
  - Found via: Germany Bundesnetzagentur trust service provider register
  - Check before the call: Obtain current ISO 27001 and BSI TR-03128/TR-03124 certificates directly from TÜV PROFICERT/BSI, including number, scope and expiry; the public BSI index did not surface a current AUTHADA entry.; Confirm cumulative equity raised and the current cap table, including InfoCert/Tinexta's present ownership.

**Cleverbase** — Cleverbase has unusually credible regulated identity infrastructure, active EUDIW work and strong public technical evidence. However, neither founder has verified Tier-1 FI or digital-asset-infrastructure employment, and public institutional traction is weakly evidenced. Monitor until round economics, B2B revenue mix and named financial-institution production deployments are verified.
  - Licences: eIDAS Qualified Trust Service Provider since Feb 2018, supervised by RDI; PKIoverheid; ISO 27001
  - Found via: EU Trusted List / Netherlands RDI QTSP register
  - Check before the call: Exact cumulative capital raised and 2025 post-money valuation; Whether the 2025 growth-capital transaction is compatible with Seed/Series A mandate

**Finshark** — Finshark has an unusually strong regulatory and technical base: an active Swedish PI licence, verified EEA passporting, public API documentation, a sandbox and live FI deployments. However, the founding team does not meet the Tier-1-FI or digital-asset pedigree signal, and the last disclosed financing is from 2022. Pass for thesis fit while retaining as market infrastructure intelligence.
  - Licences: Swedish Payment Institution, FI institution number 55241; PISP and AISP permissions passported across the EEA
  - Found via: Swedish FI company register
  - Check before the call: Reconcile the complete founder roster and verify prior employers for Adnan Sporo, Alen Hasicevic, Kristian Sternros and other claimed co-founders.; Confirm current ARR, profitability, customer concentration and whether undisclosed financing occurred after March 2022.

**3rd-eyes analytics** — The company has credible bank deployments, a Tier-1-bank-alumnus founder, recurring SaaS and recent Nordic traction. It is nevertheless outside the sharpest thesis edge: no tokenisation or regulated-rail bridge is evidenced, security disclosure is limited, and a B2C subsidiary muddies the institutional-only profile.
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: IFZ FinTech Study 2026 company directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm the current priced-round stage, total capital including Quirin's stake, valuation and ownership.; Review the current SOC 2 Type II report, auditor identity, scope and exceptions.

**CheckSig S.r.l.** — CheckSig combines an unusually strong banking founder, a verified first-in-Italy MiCAR CASP authorization, SOC attestations and a live Banca Profilo/Tinaba deployment. The score is held down by limited public institutional customer breadth, closed developer access and a still-material private-client business, so institutional revenue mix must be proven before a meeting.
  - Licences: Italian MiCAR CASP authorisation; SOC 1 and SOC 2 Type II attestations
  - Found via: Crypto Valley Association corporate directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain audited B2B versus B2C revenue, gross-margin and retention splits for CheckSig Clear.; Review CONSOB authorization scope, EU passport notifications, DORA controls, Travel Rule implementation and the named MLRO's credentials.

**finperks** — Exceptional repeat-founder proof and a real July 2026 fintech deployment are offset by no institutional-bank case, no public security assurance and limited operating history.
  - Found via: Tech.eu funding coverage
  - Check before the call: Obtain current penetration-test, ISO/SOC and uptime evidence.; Confirm the PSD2 perimeter for funding and settlement flows, including any agent, limited-network or commercial-agent reliance.

**ZTL Payment Solution** — ZTL has unusually strong regulatory proof, founder-bank pedigree, Nordic ERP distribution and fresh strategic capital. The signal score is pulled down by limited institutional-bank deployment evidence and a severely fragmented, founder-diluted cap table. Monitor pending cap-table diligence, governance review and confirmation of the compliance leadership structure.
  - Licences: Norwegian FSA payment institution; payment initiation and account information permissions
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: EBA PSD2 central register
  - Check before the call: Obtain the fully diluted cap table and shareholder-agreement governance rights.; Verify the named MLRO/CCO, AML programme ownership and current information-security certifications.

**Devengo** — The live Spanish payment-institution entry, EPC participation, recent bank-backed financing and named quantified customer references are positive. Founder depth, security assurance, public enterprise readiness and international expansion evidence remain below the meeting threshold after anti-signal deductions.
  - Licences: Spanish payment institution, Bank of Spain entity 6940
  - Found via: EBAday Fintech Zone 2026
  - Check before the call: Primary cap-table and complete funding history; Current ISO certificate and audit evidence

**Ockto** — Ockto has an unusually strong regulatory and commercial base for this cohort: a live DNB AISP authorisation with new EEA passports, an AFM licence, current production use at Lloyds Bank and ABN AMRO/Florius, nearly 100 organisational users and public APIs. The result remains weak because founder-fit evidence is thin, the stage and total capital are unresolved, international customer conversion is unproven and its published ISO 27001 certificate is expired.
  - Licences: Dutch Central Bank AISP licence, granted 2020
  - Found via: Dutch Central Bank public register
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current LRQA ISO 27001 renewal certificate and verify certification continuity since 15 March 2026.; Confirm total capital raised, round/stage classification, current shareholders, founder ownership and ABN AMRO's stake.

**Qomply** — Qomply has excellent institutional founder-market fit, named production usage, credible channel partnerships and direct FCA/Bank of England taskforce engagement. Its stage and total capital are undisclosed, security assurance is not public, and the product has no explicit digital-asset/tokenisation bridge; validate these before a meeting decision.
  - Found via: RegTech100 2024
  - Check before the call: Establish current financing stage, all capital raised and investor identities.; Obtain audited ARR, customer concentration, retention and named customers outside the UK.

**Compellio SA** — Compellio is technically and strategically close to the thesis, with verified EU sandbox participation, IETF standards work, patents and a credible Clearstream-linked founder. The evidence for financial-institution production adoption, security certification and a true Seed/Series A stage is materially weaker. Monitor pending customer references and stage/round clarification.
  - Licences: Selected for the 1st EU Blockchain Regulatory Sandbox; states eIDAS 2.0 ARF conformance and MiCA compliance
  - Found via: INATBA members directory (micro tier)
  - Check before the call: Current share register and whether Uni Systems has follow-on or control rights; Exact cumulative funding and valuation

**Validation Cloud** — Validation Cloud has unusually strong infrastructure, security, live integrations, funding quality and a founder with real bank experience. It lacks a public Tier-1 bank production case, has thin regulatory/compliance disclosure for an institutional staking vendor, and sits just inside core cheque fit after reconciling conflicting database totals. Fit includes a 0.06 anti-signal deduction.
  - Found via: Blockwall portfolio
  - Check before the call: Reconcile company/Crypto Valley all-time funding of $20.8m with Fortune/Crunchbase's $30.8m and CB Insights' $37.1m before confirming cheque fit.; Obtain the current SOC 2 Type II report, staking audit and penetration-test summaries and test incident-history completeness.

**Authologic** — Authologic combines a Tier-1 bank founder, prior fintech exit, broad national-eID coverage and credible production deployments. The strict score is held back by limited public security assurance, sparse Tier-1-bank case-study evidence and a round now outside the 18-month momentum window. Validate enterprise pipeline and certification before prioritising a meeting.
  - Found via: Warsaw fintech industry map
  - Check before the call: Confirm current ISO 27001/SOC 2 status and obtain penetration-test evidence.; Verify named customers and production deployments outside Poland.

**Yields NV** — Yields is the strongest institutional-finance product in this cohort: it has production deployments at named banks, public reference customers, ISO 27001 certification, IBM financial-services validation, Google Cloud Marketplace distribution and founders with direct quantitative-finance experience. Under the strict 200-signal contract it still lands just below the weak band because founder/regulator pedigree, public developer tooling and recent financing carry disproportionate weight, while several otherwise impressive controls are not exact matches for the library's licence- or standards-specific tests. Pass on the numeric rule, but this is the highest-priority diligence exception if Stratum3 permits judgment alongside the mechanical score.
  - Check before the call: Obtain audited ARR, growth, net retention, gross margin, average contract value, customer concentration and current runway.; Verify the complete cap table, 2023 post-money valuation and whether any financing occurred after June 2023.

**Ondorse** — Ondorse has real production traction, a Tier-1-bank alumnus founder, strong compliance expertise and unusually transparent enterprise pricing and product operations. The fit is pulled down by the narrow founder-team signal set, no licensable regulated activity of its own, limited public security proof, and stale disclosed funding. Monitor for a fresh round, independently verified ISO/SOC artefacts and deeper Tier-1-bank deployments.
  - Found via: ISAI portfolio news
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate, certification body, certificate number, scope and SOC 2 Type II report.; Confirm current ARR, retention, gross margin, and whether the Careline managed-service launch changes software gross margin.

**Adhara** — Adhara is unusually on-thesis: experienced bank founders, central-bank validation, open standards work, major institutional partners and a now-profitable merged platform. The score remains weak because the strict library requires production bank references, current certifiable security artefacts and public developer evidence that are not available; the stretch cheque and post-merger integration also warrant measured diligence rather than an immediate meeting.
  - Found via: EBAday Fintech Zone 2025
  - Check before the call: Confirm merger completion and regulatory approvals, the surviving legal/holding structure and Stratum3's investable security.; Obtain named production bank references, contracted ARR, revenue mix, renewal rates, pipeline conversion and customer concentration.

**ID-Pal** — ID-Pal has independently verified UK DIATF certification, global enterprise traction, bank-originated deployment proof and strong current awards. The score is held back by limited public developer/security evidence, no explicit digital-asset thesis bridge and stale capital formation.
  - Licences: UK DIATF certified; ISO 9001; ISO 27001
  - Found via: UK DSIT DIATF certified digital identity provider register
  - Check before the call: Retrieve the current ISO 27001/9001 certificates with certificate numbers, scope and certification body.; Retrieve ID-Pal's iBeta confirmation letters; the public iBeta index did not surface them in search.

**GenTwo AG** — GenTwo is highly thesis-relevant, globally deployed infrastructure with exceptional reported scale and a founder who built bank product infrastructure. Strict signal scoring penalises limited public technical assurance, developer tooling and founder-pattern evidence. Monitor pending security diligence and a clear regulatory-perimeter memo for GenTwo versus its issuing SPVs.
  - Found via: Crypto Valley Association ecosystem events
  - Check before the call: Obtain current ISO 27001/SOC 2, penetration-test and business-continuity evidence under NDA.; Map each Swiss, Guernsey and Cayman issuing vehicle, licence perimeter, paying agent and investor-protection allocation.

**InsurX** — InsurX has powerful commercial validation: Lloyd's verifies its Coverholder status, more than 40 brokers and insurers are connected, and named institutions confirm live facilities with over $100m GWP. The signal-library score is depressed by the lack of direct FCA authorisation, limited public security and developer evidence, no visible compliance leader and weak disclosed investor provenance. Monitor as high-quality institutional insurance infrastructure that sits slightly outside Stratum3's core digital-finance rails thesis.
  - Licences: Lloyd's Coverholder
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab Cohort 8
  - Check before the call: Retrieve the record-level FCA entries for InsurX Limited and InsurX Europe GmbH UK Branch and reconcile the full scope and effective dates of their appointed-representative permissions.; Obtain the Lloyd's ATLAS Coverholder PIN, current binding authorities, sponsoring managing agents and permitted territories/classes.

**Next Gate Tech** — Next Gate is a genuine institutional infrastructure company with Tier-1 founder pedigree, named production customers, €500bn+ platform scale and unusually strong strategic investors. The exact signal contract still scores it weak because public regulatory assurance is partly gated and the product has no digital-asset or tokenisation bridge, while disclosed capital is already about €19.48m. No financial licence is claimed and CSSF/EBA/FCA checks found none; this is not a veto for a software vendor, but meeting should wait for the regulated-rails roadmap and gated diligence pack.
  - Found via: LHoFT Innovators interview
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate to verify certification body, certificate number, scope and expiry; review the gated pentest and vulnerability reports.; Reconcile the conservative €17m equity total against CB Insights' $18.06m figure and confirm whether any post-2023 capital or debt is undisclosed.

**Konsentus** — Konsentus has unusually strong founder-market fit, credible production bank deployments and genuinely institutional open-finance infrastructure. Its current company is independent and live, but the 2024 administration/newco transfer and stale funding history dominate the risk assessment. Re-engage only after verifying asset ownership, customer continuity, revenue retention and newco liquidity.
  - Found via: Open Banking technical service providers directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the administrators' sale agreement and map which IP, contracts, liabilities and employees transferred to company 15493844.; Confirm current ARR, gross retention and how many of the claimed 250 financial institutions are directly contracted paying customers.

**ThreatFabric** — ThreatFabric is live, independent and squarely in institutional fraud infrastructure, with two named Dutch bank deployments, bank CVC validation, a prior founder exit and unusually strong original threat research. The score is held back by closed public technical assurance, no published security certification or audit, little geographic customer disclosure and no quantified bank case study. Monitor pending security diligence and evidence of repeatable non-Dutch enterprise expansion.
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: Holland FinTech network directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain current ARR, net retention, fraud-loss reduction outcomes, customer count by country and customer concentration.; Confirm aggregate funding and ownership after the undisclosed Rabo Investments and OneSpan transactions.

**IdentYum (Identity Consortium d.o.o.)** — The active AISP registration, broad EEA passport, eIDAS assurance certification and KentBank production use are real institutional signals. Fit is held back by unverified Tier-1 founder pedigree, limited target-market traction, sales-gated developer infrastructure and a continuing B2C wallet line. Confirm stage, ownership and enterprise revenue mix before prioritising a meeting.
  - Found via: EUDI Wallet Consortium implementation roster
  - Check before the call: Name and verify Robert Ilijaš's former bank, role dates and whether it meets the Tier-1 threshold.; Obtain the cap table, complete funding history and Identyum's allocation under current EU grants.

**Scorechain SA** — Scorechain is a live EU-native compliance vendor with ISO 27001 assurance, a production deployment at DZ BANK and more than 200 organisations supported. Exact-threshold scoring is constrained by thin founder institutional pedigree, stale financing and unquantified case studies. Monitor and request current ARR, retention and fundraising plans before deciding whether the durable niche position merits a meeting.
  - Licences: LEI 8945004V82JGFJZJ4B61
  - Found via: EU Digital Finance Platform fintech map
  - Check before the call: Confirm current stage and whether any financing closed after the October 2021 pre-Series A.; Obtain ARR, growth, gross retention, net retention and enterprise/customer concentration.

**Atfinity** — Atfinity is live, capital-efficient bank software with credible production deployments and strong B2B discipline. However, exact stage and cumulative capital remain undisclosed, founder pedigree only partly clears strict thresholds, and the product lacks the digital-asset or regulated-rail bridge central to Stratum3's thesis.
  - Found via: IFZ FinTech Study 2026 company directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm cumulative external capital and current financing stage directly with management.; Obtain Alexander Balzer's exact Credit Suisse and HSBC roles and dates.

**Cashlink Technologies** — Cashlink has unusually strong regulatory proof and live institutional deployment, including active BaFin permissions and real KfW/Bundesbank transactions. The score is held down by the thesis-critical absence of founder Tier-1 financial-institution pedigree and weak public security/developer transparency. Monitor and meet only if partner judgment can waive the founder-background requirement.
  - Licences: German eWpG section 16 crypto securities registrar licence; Eurosystem exploratory work, wave 2 (2024), market DLT operator
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: ECB Eurosystem exploratory work participant list (wave 2)
  - Check before the call: Confirm cumulative capital, valuation and ownership after the undisclosed April 2026 Stellar investment.; Request the current security certifications, smart-contract audit report, penetration-test summary and operational resilience metrics.

**IZNES** — IZNES has unusually strong, quantified production traction and a verified ACPR investment-firm licence, plus a completed Eurosystem experiment. Its weighted fit is 0.4145 before two anti-signal deductions; founder pedigree misses the strict Tier-1 threshold, technical assurance is opaque and total capital/stage remain unresolved.
  - Licences: Eurosystem exploratory work, wave 2 (2024), market DLT operator
  - Found via: ECB Eurosystem exploratory work participant list (wave 2)
  - Check before the call: Pull a current REGAFI extract for ACPR authorisation 87120 and confirm all active passport permissions.; Confirm Euroclear's exact ownership percentage and shareholder-control rights.

**A-Cube** — A-Cube has verified Peppol authority status, a current ISO certificate, strong API accessibility, cross-border customer evidence and institutional investors. Its score is constrained by founders without thesis-required financial-infrastructure pedigree and no public financial-institution production customer. The notified/certified role is accurately characterised as Peppol service-provider status, not a financial-services licence.
  - Licences: Certified Peppol Access Point
  - Found via: P101 A-Cube investment announcement
  - Check before the call: Confirm whether any bank, insurer or payment institution uses A-Cube in production and will reference.; Obtain revenue, ARR growth, gross margin and net retention behind the 70m-invoice and 600-client claims.

**Securosys** — Securosys has unusually credible cryptographic product validation and live production use in Swiss market infrastructure. However, the founders lack the required prior Tier-1 FI or digital-asset-infrastructure pedigree, and the funding/compliance evidence is stale or thin. NIST now marks FIPS certificate 4583 historical, although current Common Criteria EAL4+ certification mitigates product-certification risk.
  - Licences: FIPS 140-2 Level 3; Common Criteria EAL 4+
  - Found via: IFZ FinTech Study 2025 company directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm current cap table and whether any undisclosed financing occurred after 2015.; Obtain current company-level ISO 27001/SOC 2 evidence and security-testing reports.

**Algoan** — Algoan has a verified AISP passport, public developer infrastructure and an unusually strong quantified production case with Sofinco. Founder pedigree is useful but misses the contract’s Tier-1-bank and regulatory thresholds, while funding visibility is poor. Monitor pending capital and security-certificate diligence rather than rejecting the commercially validated product.
  - Licences: ACPR-registered Account Information Service Provider no. 17728
  - Found via: EBA PSD2 central register
  - Check before the call: Confirm total capital raised, current stage and ownership from the cap table.; Obtain the Bureau Veritas ISO 27001 certificate number, scope and expiry date.

**Monthio** — Monthio has a verified passported AISP permission, an exceptional bank-alumni founder, named production use at Danske Bank and strong Nordic penetration. It misses the digital-asset/DeFi edge of the thesis and public technical diligence is thin, but the 2024 Series A, institutional ownership and multi-market credit infrastructure justify monitoring rather than a pass.
  - Licences: Supervised by the Danish FSA; permission details undisclosed
  - Found via: Copenhagen Fintech current members
  - Check before the call: Capture Monthio's exact Danish FSA register detail and EBA register row, including home authorisation date, FT-ID and full passport list.; Obtain the current ISO 27001 certificate, certificate number, scope, validity dates and latest penetration-test executive summary.

**Cachet** — Cachet is live, regulated, passported and commercially credible, with strong cross-border platform traction and growing brokerage share. However, the founding-team fit is only partial, public technology assurance is thin, and the continuing B2C channel plus stale equity financing create four confirmed anti-signals.
  - Licences: Estonian insurance broker registration
  - Found via: Icebreaker.vc portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current Estonian intermediary registration decision and confirm every active passport directly with host regulators.; Clarify whether 2026 revenue is predominantly B2B platform fees, brokerage commissions, or individual-policy revenue.

**Subaio** — Subaio has excellent bank-originated validation, live multinational deployments, a Mastercard channel and transparent production operations. It misses Stratum3's founder threshold because no founder is a Tier-1 FI or digital-asset infrastructure alumnus, and it has no tokenisation or regulated-rail angle. The strong commercial business is therefore a thesis mismatch rather than a weak company.
  - Found via: Copenhagen Fintech current members
  - Check before the call: Confirm the amounts and terms of all pre-Series A bank investments.; Obtain current ARR, growth, gross margin and bank-logo retention.

**Apiax AG** — Apiax has exceptional thesis evidence in founder-bank pedigree, regulation-as-code infrastructure and production use at Credit Suisse. Strict evidence gaps around current funding momentum, public security certification and developer self-service temper the score. Reconfirm revenue growth, cap table and current round plans before outreach.
  - Found via: Swiss FinTech Awards company directory
  - Check before the call: Verify the precise total raised and any undisclosed post-2021 financing from the Swiss commercial register.; Obtain current ISO 27001/SOC 2 evidence and the private API documentation.

**Ozone API** — Rare founder-standard pedigree and current bank production evidence are attractive, but public product documentation, assurance proof, funding recency and multi-country named-customer evidence remain weak.
  - Found via: Mastercard Start Path New Networks 2025
  - Check before the call: Obtain current ISO 27001 and SOC 2 Type II evidence, penetration-test summary and uptime history.; Verify ARR growth, retention, customer concentration and national-market contract economics.

**TransactionLink** — TransactionLink has the best verified commercial signal set in this batch: regulated-FI announcements, production references, quantified ROI and a strong seed syndicate. Its Visa founder credential fits the infrastructure thesis, but no digital-asset rail is visible and public security/case-study hygiene needs diligence.
  - Found via: Warsaw fintech industry map
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate and validate scope, body, expiry, penetration tests and subprocessor controls.; Reconcile the public Fiserv/Viva Wallet case-study placeholders and reference-check each named deployment.

**Lissi** — Lissi is exceptionally relevant to mandated European identity infrastructure and brings genuine Commerzbank, standards and EU-pilot credibility. The public record is still pilot-heavy, with no quantified production bank case study or verifiable ISO 27001 certificate. Monitor closely after the fresh round and move to a meeting once production conversion and security assurance are evidenced.
  - Found via: German EUDI Wallet prototype competition (funded challenge)
  - Check before the call: Confirm production versus pilot status, ARR and contract value for Commerzbank, ING, itsme and Post Luxembourg.; Obtain ISO 27001 scope/certificate or security audit, penetration-test and secure-SDLC evidence.

**Mifundo** — Mifundo combines a bank-CEO founder, active EU credit-intermediary passporting and unusually strong EIC backing. Public proof of security certification, developer infrastructure and named multi-country customers remains thin, while the residual consumer line weakens thesis purity. Monitor for a named production bank case study and independent security evidence.
  - Licences: Estonian credit intermediary licence
  - Found via: EU Digital Finance Platform fintech map
  - Check before the call: Reconcile the official register's displayed deletion notice with the still-active status and current 2025 filing.; Obtain ISO 27001 evidence, penetration-test scope, API documentation and uptime/SLA data.

**eXate** — eXate has unusually credible founder-market fit from two former HSBC digital and RegTech leaders, real current production evidence at ING, patent-backed policy enforcement and broad deployment across APIs, event streams, databases and hybrid clouds. The product is relevant compliance middleware rather than a regulated service, so the absence of an FCA licence is not a veto. The main gaps are stale financing, limited independently inspectable security material, a broken public API reference and the absence of a named quantified customer case.
  - Found via: ING Ventures-backed eXate seed announcement
  - Check before the call: Obtain the actual ISO 27001:2022 and ISO 27701 certificates, certificate numbers, scope, expiry, surveillance history, penetration-test summary and any SOC 2 report.; Resolve the discrepancy between the 2015 legal-entity incorporation and the company's stated 2018 founding date.

**Atlar** — Atlar has the strongest commercial evidence in this batch: a licensed-bank production deployment, public reference executives, quantified ROI, broad geographic customers, enterprise-grade certifications, active API docs and Tier-1 venture backing. The strict fit score is pulled down by no Tier-1-bank founder, no public regulatory-perimeter opinion, limited public standards engagement, an unverifiable public ISO certificate, and a financing gap beyond 24 months.
  - Found via: Index Ventures portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain counsel's written PSD2 perimeter analysis confirming that Atlar is a technical-service provider and does not require PI/PISP authorisation in its operating markets.; Review the ISO 27001 certificate, named certification body, scope, expiry and SOC 2 Type II report through the Trust Center.

**STOKR** — STOKR has the strongest operating proof in this cohort so far: independently verified MiCA and PI passports, current ISO certification, a published smart-contract audit, a public OpenAPI-driven product, named Blockstream/Bitfinex production use, $312m of payouts and $1.3bn-plus active digital securities. Its score is nevertheless constrained by the library's 30% founder weighting, no Tier-1-bank/payment-network/regulator alumni, limited public compliance leadership, and a residual individual-investor model. Monitor closely and advance only after customer concentration, recurring revenue and licence-operating controls are diligenced.
  - Licences: CSSF MiCAR crypto-asset service provider authorisation, Jun 2026; CSSF payment institution authorisation, Jun 2026
  - Found via: LHoFT Luxembourg Fintech Map 2025
  - Check before the call: Export the CSSF and EBA records directly to capture STOKR's PI national reference, authorised services and full passport scope.; Obtain the ISO/IEC 27001 certificate PDF with certificate number, scope, certification body accreditation and expiry.

**Scytáles** — Scytáles has unusually strong standards influence, profitable public-sector traction and two independently confirmed European Commission contracts. The score is held down by no Tier-1 FI founder, thin public developer/security evidence and no disclosed financing history; four confirmed anti-signals reduce the pre-deduction 0.3960 fit to 0.2760. Monitor because the EUDI position is strategically valuable despite the poor calibrated score.
  - Found via: OpenID Foundation members / EUDI Wallet reference implementation contributors
  - Check before the call: Establish equity ownership, total external capital and whether any institutional financing round defines a current stage.; Obtain audited security material, ISO 27001 scope, penetration-test reports and EUDI certification roadmap.

**Myver** — Myver is unusually close to the thesis on founder pedigree, payments data and regulated-card-rail adjacency, with defensible patents and PCI DSS claims. It still lacks a named bank production reference and public developer/security evidence, while 2025 filings show negative equity and financing dependence despite a 2026 capital increase.
  - Found via: Visa Innovation Program Europe Nordics and Baltics 2025
  - Check before the call: Obtain the exact EIFO round amount, instrument, valuation and full cap table.; Confirm current PCI DSS Level 1 assessor, attestation date and scope.

**Ownera** — The strongest thesis match in the set has real 2026 production evidence, open institutional standards and strategic bank backing, but limited founder-bank pedigree and incomplete assurance disclosure.
  - Found via: Global Digital Finance collateral sandbox
  - Check before the call: Quantify the 2025 convertible loans and 2026 share issuances to confirm the €30m capital ceiling.; Obtain current ISO certificates, penetration-test evidence, architecture review and uptime history.

**Ryft** — Ryft is a credible, active FCA-authorised payment API with strong merchant adoption, named acquirer usage and clean early-stage financing. It misses the fund's defining founder-pedigree and digital-asset/tokenisation axes, leaving a poor thesis fit despite good execution.
  - Licences: FCA Authorised Payment Institution 972895; PCI DSS Level 1 service provider
  - Found via: Tech.eu funding coverage
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current PCI DSS Attestation of Compliance and any ISO 27001 or SOC 2 report.; Verify Grant Campbell's AML qualifications and prior regulated-institution experience.

**Mazepay A/S** — Mazepay has the clearest institutional-finance fit in the set so far: an active EU payment-institution passport, live bank and issuer distribution, broad named enterprise usage and credible payment-network experience in the founding team. It scores down on thin public technical transparency, lack of an independently verifiable ISO certificate, and limited Tier-1 founder/investor pedigree. Monitor for a fresh round, certificate-room evidence and clearer recurring revenue metrics.
  - Licences: Mastercard ICCP partner
  - Found via: Vendep Capital portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the Danish FSA licence detail or EBA-register export showing the home-state authorisation number and complete passport scope.; Obtain the current ISO 27001 certificate, certification body, scope and expiry date.

**Meo (formerly NewBanking)** — Meo is squarely in thesis and has a genuine long-running production reference at KommuneKredit, public API documentation and status transparency, third-party assurance, a live national-eID integration and unusually strong regulatory-expert-group engagement. However, public employee count has contracted to four, 2025 equity is negative, the latest financing is stale, and the only public ISO 27001 certificate has expired while the website still claims certification. These outweigh the strategic fit at this stage.
  - Found via: Copenhagen Fintech current members
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current cap table, March 2024 financing documents, cash balance, burn, debt covenants and a solvency plan for the reported negative 2025 equity.; Verify whether ISO 27001 was renewed after certificate FI210331-79 expired and reconcile any continued certification claim.

**Inven** — Inven has exceptional commercial momentum, strong investor validation and credible global B2B deployment. It is adjacent to the thesis through institutional private-market data, but the founders lack the required Tier-1 FI/digital-asset background and the product does not touch regulated rails, MiCA or tokenisation.
  - Found via: Vendep Capital portfolio
  - Check before the call: Confirm exact founder employment histories and durations from primary LinkedIn profiles.; Request SOC 2/ISO 27001 evidence and the latest penetration-test attestation.

**Vouchsafe (Interrobang Limited)** — Very early but compelling: a verified current DVS certificate, strong inclusion thesis, reputable lead, live credit-union results and public-sector validation justify a meeting.

  - Found via: UK DSIT DIATF certified digital identity provider register and gov.uk Digital Identity Sectoral Analysis Report 2026
  - Check before the call: Verify the exact £79,800 grant source and grant agreement.; Obtain iBeta report, penetration test, ISO/SOC roadmap and model-bias testing.

**Animo Solutions** — Animo is unusually strong technically for its size: active open source, current APIs, national-eID integration and real EUDIW interoperability work. It remains a poor current fit because cumulative funding and stage are unknown, commercial evidence is mostly logos and public projects rather than paying production customers, and security assurance is not independently published. Monitor for a priced Seed round, a named financial-institution deployment and audited security controls.

  - Check before the call: Confirm cumulative equity, grants, ownership and whether a Seed financing is planned.; Convert the trusted-by logos into references: contract value, production status, geography and renewal evidence.

**Causa Prima** — Exceptional repeat founders, a named top-tier lead and unusually transparent developer surfaces merit attention, but current institutional traction is entirely unnamed and enterprise assurance is immature.
  - Found via: Creandum portfolio
  - Check before the call: Name and reference-check design partners, paying customers and the composition of the claimed 3,000 accounts.; Separate free Scribo/Byll users from enterprise Causa Prima deployments, paid ARR and retained transaction volume.

**Topograph** — Topograph is a very young but unusually well-evidenced KYB infrastructure company: it has a real public API, sandbox, changelog, status/trust centres, 20 customers across several continents, regulated fintech users and a high-quality Seedcamp-led round. The contract score remains weak because the founders narrowly miss the two-year Tier-1-bank threshold, no Tier-1 bank case study is public, security certificates are claimed but not publicly verifiable to certificate-level detail, and the company is too young to show durable multi-market organisation. Monitor closely and seek a reference/security diligence call before moving to meet.
  - Found via: Seedcamp portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate and SOC 2 Type II report with issuer, number, scope, dates and exceptions.; Validate customer-level ARR, net retention, gross margin after register fees, pipeline conversion and customer concentration.

**Dotfile** — Dotfile shows excellent early commercial proof, current product velocity, global KYB coverage and credible enterprise production use. The score is held down by the thesis-critical absence of founder FI/digital-asset pedigree and by missing identity-standard/regulatory-positioning signals; as a vendor it does not itself require an EMI, PI, VASP or CASP licence.

  - Check before the call: Verify ARR, net retention, gross margin and the revenue CAGR underlying the Sifted rank.; Obtain the SOC 2 report and confirm whether it is Type II, audit period and exceptions.

**Copla** — Copla has exceptional early commercial momentum, credible repeat founders and core cheque fit, but it lacks the required Tier-1-FI founder pedigree and scores weakly on public technical assurance. The product is highly relevant to regulated finance yet remains a broad compliance layer rather than tokenisation or regulated-rail infrastructure. Monitor for a Tier-1 bank deployment, verifiable security certification and deeper digital-asset architecture.
  - Found via: Iron Wolf Capital portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the actual ISO 27001 certificate, scope, certification body and expiry date.; Verify founder employment histories directly and test for any qualifying Tier-1 financial-institution tenure.

**Thirdfort** — Excellent UK traction and verified identity/FCA/ICO standing, but the round is a stretch cheque, geographic concentration is high and technical assurance is not publicly verifiable.

  - Found via: UK statutory digital verification services register
  - Check before the call: Obtain FCA Register extract for FRN 806236 and exact permitted activities.; Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate and current penetration-test report.

**iGrant.io** — A technically credible EUDI wallet stack with unusually strong standards participation, open-source activity, certification and public-sector pilots. It misses the thesis's founder-FI requirement and lacks a verifiable non-pilot bank customer or recent institutional financing. Revisit only if a bank converts into paid production and the financing history is reconciled.

**sproof** — sproof has strong evidence in ISO 27001, public API documentation, national-eID breadth and a real bank deployment. It is an orchestrator rather than a QTSP, has no founder-FI pedigree and retains a consumer tier, so the strict thesis score remains conservative.   - Found via: Bitkom Arbeitskreis Digitale Identitaeten member companies
  - Check before the call: Confirm current ARR, enterprise share, renewal rate and financing plans.; Verify the contractual/QTSP architecture for every QES route and EUDI ARF conformance.

**TwoWay** — TwoWay has unusually strong founder-market fit, a clean recent round and credible production signals from BNP Paribas and other Tier-1/2 institutions. Evidence is still light on named quantified case studies and independently certified security controls. Monitor closely and request security diligence plus reference calls before upgrading to meet.

**Tunic Pay** — Tunic has the best direct signal set so far: repeat fintech founders, a live TSB product, FI-originated confirmation, Finovate, Parliament evidence and recent award wins. **Yaspa** — Yaspa has the best operating momentum in this set: payments-native founder, live regulated rails, public APIs and status data, named production customers, fresh lead financing, a returning investor, government grant and strong awards. **Climate X** — High-quality Tier-1 bank alumni, production financial-institution use, strong advisors and excellent investor validation do not offset thin public technical/security proof and a weak digital-asset-infrastructure fit. **Spherity** — Spherity has credible identity standards work, EUDI positioning, enterprise customers and fresh early-stage capital. Its strongest production proof is outside finance, and the founding team lacks the fund's required FI/digital-asset-infrastructure pedigree. **Softloans** — Softloans has the strongest verified commercial signal so far: active Bank of Lithuania registration, a live Luminor product, Baltic distribution, measurable merchant outcomes, and €1m pre-seed funding. **Certificall** — Certificall has strong early commercial momentum: a fresh €1m Seed with Groupama participation, more than 150 customers and 20,000 users at the round, over 3 million certified photos, public Swagger/API documentation, production insurance integration and active AFNOR standards work. Certificall is a relying SaaS provider, not itself a QTSP or regulated insurer, PI, EMI or CASP, so no missing company licence was found. The biggest gaps are institutional founder pedigree, security assurance for Certificall itself, formal compliance leadership and quantified FI customer outcomes.   - Found via: Bank of Lithuania financial market participants
