**Narval** — Narval has rare thesis fit through Jessner's long BNP Paribas markets career and a genuinely live route from BitGo qualified custody into DeFi. Public evidence remains concentrated in one distribution relationship, while certification, external audit, compliance tooling and customer economics are undisclosed. Monitor the BitGo rollout rather than pass outright, but require institutional references and security diligence before a meeting.
  - Found via: Fabric Ventures portfolio
  - Check before the call: Confirm Narval's commercial contract with BitGo, revenues, institutional end-user count and transaction volume since the June 2026 launch.; Obtain SOC 2/ISO 27001, penetration-test, external audit, incident-response and secure-TEE attestation evidence.

**Kore Labs** — Kore has excellent domain founder-market fit, credible production financial-institution customers and verified FCA Pathways and ISO 27001 signals. Its closed technical surface, fragmented angel-led financing and lack of explicit digital-asset or tokenisation infrastructure materially weaken Stratum3 fit. Monitor for a digital-assets use case, FI-originated reference case and institutional lead investor before meeting.
  - Found via: AIFinTech100 2023
  - Check before the call: Confirm the exact cumulative equity, grant and subsidised-loan total after the 2025 and 2026 share allotments.; Obtain the current ISO certificate and scope, penetration-test summary, uptime record, DPA and enterprise SLA.

**Maximum Information** — Maximum Information has a high-quality insurance team, six paying enterprise insurers, multi-year SaaS revenue, a named Convex deployment and strong specialist investors. The low library score is driven by bank-specific and digital-asset signals that do not reward insurance-domain expertise, plus very limited public security and developer evidence. Monitor closely and advance if customer references validate production criticality and expansion economics.
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab Cohort 12
  - Check before the call: Obtain names, countries, ACV, renewal dates and reference calls for the six paying enterprise insurers.; Review SOC 2 or ISO 27001 roadmap, penetration-test results, data architecture, SLA and disaster-recovery controls.

**iDenfy** — iDenfy has real commercial deployment, excellent public references and unusually strong G2 traction. It misses the mandatory founder-background thesis and has a time-sensitive security-certification gap. Commercial quality is higher than its Stratum3 thesis fit.
  - Found via: Fintech in Baltic — Lithuania fintech directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the post-2-June-2026 ISO 27001 renewal certificate or written evidence of certification continuity.; Reconcile the four reported rounds with the US$1.1m/latest and approximately US$1m/total funding disclosures.

**Ariadne Business Analytics** — Ariadne has unusually strong financial-contract IP, a successful prior founder exit and real ACTUS standards credibility. However, it lacks the thesis-required founder pedigree and public named-bank traction, while commercial and security proof remain thin after eleven years.
  - Found via: Tenity European Accelerator 2025
  - Check before the call: Identify current ARR, gross margin and the names of production financial-institution customers.; Verify the depth and request count of the public Postman workspace.

**ReceiptHero** — ReceiptHero has a real Visa channel, public integration documentation and multi-country merchant traction. It lacks a named bank in production, institutional security assurance and founder financial-institution pedigree; the undisclosed 2024 round leaves Gate 4 unresolved.
  - Licences: PCI DSS compliant
  - Found via: Lifeline Ventures companies
  - Check before the call: Obtain the 2024 round amount and post-money cap table to resolve Gate 4 and confirm core cheque fit.; Obtain named bank production references, contract economics and ReceiptHero-attributable transaction volumes.

**Open Payments** — Strong 2026 product evidence, current regulatory status and an excellent Monitor deployment show a credible bank-connectivity platform. Founder pedigree, security attestations and institutional-FI references remain below Stratum3's bar.
  - Licences: Swedish Payment Institution supervised by Finansinspektionen
  - Found via: Swedish FinTech Association member directory
  - Check before the call: Archive the current Finansinspektionen detail result and obtain a dated permissions/passport extract for the investment file.; Request current ARR, growth, gross margin, net retention and customer concentration.

**SettleMint NV** — SettleMint is the strongest thesis match in this set on institutional tokenisation, founder-bank pedigree, production deployment and venture validation. Strict public-evidence thresholds expose material diligence gaps in security certification, compliance leadership and funding total; seven confirmed anti-signals reduce the pre-deduction 0.3603 fit to 0.1503. Monitor urgently because the disclosed €23.6m already makes it stretch-fit and the undisclosed 2024 investment could breach the €30m ceiling.
  - Found via: INATBA members directory (medium tier)
  - Check before the call: Obtain the December 2024 THG investment amount and a fully reconciled capital schedule; independently verify or reject the $31.2m database claim.; Review current SOC 2 Type II and ISO 27001 reports, certificate numbers, scope and accredited issuing body.

**Elucidate** — Elucidate combines a credible institutional founder, named bank production use, strong API and messaging-standard evidence, a differentiated regulated-benchmark position and a solid investor syndicate. The current BaFin/ESMA register row and current ISO certificate still require direct verification, while the four-year funding gap makes operating momentum and runway the central diligence questions.
  - Licences: BaFin-regulated benchmark administrator; ESMA-authorised
  - Found via: Elevator Lab (Raiffeisen Bank International)
  - Check before the call: Obtain a current dated ESMA benchmark-register extract or direct BaFin supervisory record for Elucidate GmbH and confirm there has been no lapse or scope change.; Obtain the current ISO 27001 and ISO 42001 certificates, named accredited certification bodies, scope, expiry dates and recent penetration-test summary.

**Secfix** — Secfix has strong customer and financing momentum, but it is a horizontal SMB/mid-market compliance platform rather than finance-native infrastructure. The decisive thesis gap is the absence of a Tier-1 FI or digital-asset-infrastructure founder, compounded by limited public technical assurance artifacts.
  - Found via: Neosfer portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate to verify certification body, number, scope and expiry.; Identify and reference-check a named licensed bank using Secfix in production.

**LOQR** — LOQR has genuine production bank traction, current ISO 27001 certification and a regulator-verified LINCE product qualification. However, it lacks the required Tier-1-FI/digital-asset-infrastructure founder profile, has no digital-asset angle, and its last disclosed round was in 2021.
  - Licences: LINCE qualification obtained 2024
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: EU Trusted List / national QTSP registers sweep
  - Check before the call: Confirm current ARR, growth and whether any post-2021 capital was raised privately.; Obtain the underlying LINCE certificate and current ISO/IEC 30107-3 test report.

**IdentifAI** — IdentifAI is a credible adjacent identity-security platform with excellent funding momentum, a founder exit, public API evidence and a production insurer integration. It is not yet a high-conviction Stratum3 fit because regulated-finance pedigree, Tier-1 bank customers, security assurance and identity-standards integration are missing. Monitor for a bank onboarding deployment and independently verifiable enterprise security controls.
  - Found via: United Ventures portfolio and releases
  - Check before the call: Verify ARR, API usage revenue, gross retention and the share of revenue from banking/insurance versus media and public-sector customers.; Request ISO 27001/SOC 2 status, penetration-test results, model-security controls and a GDPR DPIA.

**walt.id** — walt.id has the strongest open-source and standards evidence in this cohort and credible government/bank-adjacent production usage. It misses the fund's founder-background requirement and lacks public enterprise security assurance, while commercial revenue and recent financing remain opaque. Monitor closely around EUDI rollout, but require hard ARR, security and named-bank evidence before a meeting.
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: BIS G20 TechSprint 2025
  - Check before the call: Verify audited ARR, enterprise-versus-support revenue mix, net retention and concentration.; Obtain penetration-test reports, software supply-chain controls and any non-public ISO/SOC assurance.

**Flagright** — Flagright combines unusually strong developer documentation, real institutional production references, quantified customer outcomes, current certifications, global deployment and fresh Series A backing. Founder pedigree is not Tier-1 and the conservative funding total makes the cheque a stretch, but the operating evidence is the strongest in this cohort.
  - Found via: Y Combinator company directory
  - Check before the call: Reconcile CB Insights' $26.6m total across seven events with the three headline rounds totalling about $19.6m.; Obtain ISO certificate number/body, SOC 2 auditor and reports, penetration-test summary and uptime history.

**Danube Tech** — Danube Tech is one of the technically strongest identity-infrastructure assets in the set: it helped create widely adopted open standards and code, has real government/EU project selection and remains founder-controlled. It does not yet show the institutional-finance commercial proof, team scale, security certification or priced institutional funding needed for a meeting recommendation. Monitor as a strategic deep-tech asset and revisit on a named production FI deployment or financing process.
  - Found via: Decentralized Identity Foundation members
  - Check before the call: Obtain a complete schedule and amounts for FFG, NGI, CEF and other grants to prove the capital ceiling.; Confirm whether the company is formally raising a Seed or Series A round and at what pre-money valuation.

**Intellibonds** — The institutional product, Tier-1 bank founders and FCA Sandbox history fit the thematic perimeter. However, verified production traction, security assurance, developer transparency and recent institutional funding are all absent, leaving a 0.3503 weighted category fit before four confirmed anti-signal deductions.
  - Licences: FCA Regulatory Sandbox participant
  - Found via: Level39 member directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm current priced-round stage, cash runway and any financing since 2021 directly with management.; Request named production customers, retention metrics and measured ROI.

**SCRYPT Digital Trading AG** — Strong institutional positioning, live integrations and quantified production use merit diligence, but official VQF membership confirmation and technical-assurance evidence are gating items.
  - Licences: VQF SRO member; SCRYPT Digital Investments AG FINMA portfolio manager licence, July 2023
  - ⚠️ Review flags: VQF membership is claimed by SCRYPT and repeated by third parties, but a company entry could not be verified in a public VQF member register; require a current VQF confirmation before investment.
  - Found via: Crypto Valley Association podcast
  - Check before the call: Confirm VQF membership directly in VQF's non-indexed member register.; Obtain audited revenue/ARR, customer concentration and current cap table.

**ConnectPay** — ConnectPay has a current, broad EMI licence, pan-EEA passporting, an experienced payments founder, high payment volume, public APIs and quantified customer deployments. Those strengths are outweighed by a fresh AML/CFT enforcement action, a 32.6% revenue decline in 2025, a €5.2m annual loss, unclear shareholder financing and no institutional validation.
  - Licences: Bank of Lithuania EMI licence; ISO/IEC 27001; ISO 22301
  - ⚠️ Review flags: VETO — Bank of Lithuania enforcement register records a €98,000 AML/CFT fine and administrative arrangement on 20 January 2026.
  - Found via: Bank of Lithuania EMI register / e-Residency marketplace
  - Check before the call: Full text, remediation plan and supervisory follow-up for the January 2026 AML/CFT settlement; Cumulative equity and shareholder-loan financing versus the €30m mandate ceiling

**opago** — A differentiated German agentic-payment and embedded-compliance proposition has early merchant traction, awards and credible industry visibility. However, the public licence record does not substantiate its MiCA-compliance/passporting claims despite terms describing crypto-transfer activity; do not meet until BaFin authorisation or a binding outside-counsel perimeter opinion is produced.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: LICENCE VETO: opago's terms describe channel management, Bitcoin payment intermediation and transfer to merchants, but no BaFin/ESMA CASP authorisation was found.; AML GOVERNANCE VETO: opago sells AML/Travel Rule infrastructure and performs merchant checks, but names no qualified MLRO and publishes no governed AML policy.
  - Found via: German INVEST eligible-company directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the BaFin/MiCA application or authorisation reference and exact licensed service perimeter.; Confirm cumulative funding, latest round size, investors and cap table.

**Obligate (Obligate AG, formerly FQX AG)** — Obligate is a live, core-cheque-fit tokenisation rail with production issuances, institutional integrations and strong strategic alignment. The score is held down mainly by unverified founder pedigree, thin public security/API assurance and limited quantified bank case studies. Resolve the VQF register entry and founder backgrounds before a meeting decision.
  - Licences: VQF SRO member and Swiss AMLA financial intermediary
  - ⚠️ Review flags: VQF/AMLA status is current company copy, but a company-specific FINMA SRO-register result was not retrievable; verify directly in FINMA's SRO member search before investment.
  - Found via: CVA Davos Disruptors startup spotlight
  - Check before the call: Retrieve and archive a current FINMA SRO-member-search result for Obligate AG/FQX AG and confirm the VQF membership number.; Verify each founder's full employment history against the Tier-1 FI/digital-asset-infrastructure thesis requirement.

**Wealth Reader** — The regulated, passported API and founder exit are real positives, with credible product depth and early institutional references. Fit is held down by the absence of a Tier-1 FI founder, thin public compliance leadership, and customer evidence that is largely PoC or unquantified. Monitor for a named production bank deployment, compliance hire and DACH/UK expansion.
  - Licences: Bank of Spain Account Information Service Provider authorisation
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Human review required: the AISP registration is verified, but no public AML framework or named MLRO was evidenced.
  - Found via: AEFI Infraestructura Financiera vertical
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current AENOR ISO 27001 certificate number, scope and expiry.; Confirm ARR, gross retention and how much of the claimed 2m monthly connections is paid production volume.

**ShareID** — ShareID has real strategic signal: a Tier-1-bank-experienced founder, defensible patent, public API docs, France Identité interoperability, quantified RollingFunds production results, a French ministry deployment and a 2026 national-wallet convention. The strict fit remains poor after three anti-signals, and its advertised QEAA issuance lacks visible qualified-provider status. Keep it on monitor pending trust-service-role and security-assurance diligence.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: ShareID advertises EAA/QEAA issuance, but no ShareID qualified trust-service entry is visible in the EU Trusted List. Confirm whether issuance is future, delegated to a QTSP, or an unqualified regulated-service claim before proceeding.
  - Found via: France FinTech BtoFi infrastructure map
  - Check before the call: Reconcile the disclosed €2m seed with Google's later statement that ShareID completed €4m of funding; obtain round dates and cap table.; Determine whether ShareID itself issues QEAA, uses a listed QTSP, or is describing future technical capability.

**Atoa** — Atoa is live, properly UK-authorised and has credible merchant ROI plus a founder exit, but it is conventional UK merchant-payments infrastructure rather than institutional digital-asset infrastructure. The last disclosed financing is stale and commercial evidence is concentrated in small UK businesses, not financial institutions. Public AML leadership and auditable ISO/SOC artefacts require diligence.
  - Licences: FCA authorised payment institution FRN 1007647; AIS and PIS
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Regulated payment activity is confirmed, but no substantive public AML framework or named MLRO was found; verify privately before investment.
  - Found via: Open Banking regulated providers directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm FCA record status and permissions directly in a live register session and obtain the FCA authorisation letter.; Obtain the named MLRO, AML programme summary, SOC 2 Type II report and ISO 27001 certificate with scope and expiry.

**FinchTrade AG** — There is credible production usage and a technically capable team, but the founder/funding profile is opaque and the key Swiss VQF claim is not yet register-verifiable; current EU/UK restrictions also undermine immediate Stratum3 market fit.
  - Licences: VQF SRO financial intermediary; ISO/IEC 27001; ISO/IEC 27701
  - ⚠️ Review flags: REGULATORY VERIFICATION REQUIRED: VQF membership is a company claim; FINMA confirms the SRO framework, but the entity-level public member result could not be reproduced.; EU/UK go-to-market is currently constrained by the company's own not-MiCAR/not-FCA disclaimer.
  - Found via: Crypto Valley Association corporate directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the live VQF membership certificate, member number and direct register extract.; Obtain audited volume, gross margin, counterparty concentration and balance-sheet exposure.

**BondAuction** — BondAuction has the cohort's strongest relevant founder pedigree, an active AFM licence with broad EEA passporting and a well-evidenced £25m production transaction. Those strengths are outweighed by repeated prudential/reporting failures, overdue holding-company filings, a 63-shareholder cap table and no public enterprise-security evidence. Pass unless regulatory remediation, solvency and governance are conclusively demonstrated.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Adverse regulatory action: DNB enforcement order dated 20 March 2025 for late/missing IFREP and FINREP reports.; Adverse regulatory action: DNB fine dated 26 February 2026 for two periods below the €75,000 minimum capital requirement.; The FCA authorisation claim could not be independently resolved to a public FCA register entry; verify FRN and permission scope before investment.
  - Found via: Tenity (formerly F10)
  - Check before the call: Obtain the FCA firm-reference number and verify the exact permission scope directly in the FCA register.; Confirm whether DNB's 2025 order and 2026 fine are final, paid and fully remediated; obtain current regulatory-capital reports.

**Klearly** — Klearly has exceptional merchant traction, credible payments investors and European expansion, but it is hospitality-SMB acceptance rather than financial-institution infrastructure. Founder thesis fit is weak and the public regulatory/security posture is too opaque for a payments company. The Rapyd dependency and Klearly's legal role in merchant acquiring must be verified before any reconsideration.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Regulatory-perimeter review required: no Klearly PI/EMI register entry found; public support materials identify Rapyd as settlement provider but do not establish Klearly's agent status.
  - Found via: Holland FinTech network directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the Rapyd–Klearly programme agreement and confirm whether Klearly is a registered agent, technical service provider or payment facilitator.; Verify full founder employment histories, especially Sam Koekoek's UNIPaaS tenure and any prior bank/network roles.

**Januar** — Januar has unusually strong product evidence for this set: a real public API/UAT environment, fresh MiCA authorisation, PSD2 passporting and explicit fiat-stablecoin rails. The score is held down by no Tier-1 founder, no named production customers or case studies, and the 2024 Danish FSA control orders. Monitor pending independent evidence that every order is closed and that regulated infrastructure is generating durable institutional revenue.
  - Licences: Danish FSA PSD2 Payment Institution, FTID:21230; Danish FSA MiCA CASP, FTID:10908
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Adverse regulatory action: four Danish FSA orders were issued in December 2024. Januar says it later adhered, but the underlying closure letter could not be independently rendered.
  - Found via: byFounders portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the Danish FSA closure or follow-up document and confirm all four December 2024 orders are formally satisfied.; Reconcile disclosed lifetime funding to primary bank statements or cap-table records; public round databases double-count some 2022 and 2024 entries.

**OneID** — OneID has credible production traction with NatWest, a real Adobe integration and unusually accessible API infrastructure. The founding team lacks the thesis-required Tier-1 FI or digital-asset-infrastructure provenance, while the cap table is unusually fragmented. Do not rely on the FCA claim until FRN 928911 is manually confirmed in the live register.
  - Licences: FCA-registered AISP, FRN 928911; UK DIATF certified across Identity, Orchestration and Holder/Wallet
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Licence-verification review required: FRN 928911 is claimed, but the live FCA firm record could not be independently retrieved.
  - Found via: EU-Startups funding coverage - European digital identity rounds
  - Check before the call: Manually retrieve FCA firm record 928911 and confirm current AISP status and permissions.; Confirm whether the February 2025 financing is legally classified as Seed or Series A.

**ReStabilise** — The founder-market fit is unusually strong for a pre-seed company, with Swift, Deutsche Bank and top-tier crypto-regulatory legal experience. FCA sandbox selection validates regulatory relevance, but it is not a licence and there is no public production deployment, API assurance or customer evidence. Monitor for authorisation and a named institutional go-live before meeting.
  - Licences: FCA Regulatory Sandbox (stablecoins cohort)
  - ⚠️ Review flags: No FCA authorisation or cryptoasset registration was located for RESTABILISE LTD; confirm the exact sandbox testing permissions and whether any live custody/issuance is conducted.
  - Found via: FCA Regulatory Sandbox - stablecoins cohort
  - Check before the call: Obtain the FCA sandbox testing plan, restricted permission basis and any firm reference number.; Confirm whether custody or issuance is live with real clients and under which legal entity and permissions.

**Brickken Solutions, S.L.** — Brickken has the best public technical stack in this group and meaningful scale, but founder-FI fit and named institutional-customer evidence are weak. More importantly, its live token/staking activity lacks visible post-transition MiCA register status or a MiCAR whitepaper, forcing regulatory review.
  - Licences: Selected participant, European Blockchain Sandbox
  - ⚠️ Review flags: MiCA perimeter requires counsel: Brickken is absent from the CNMV CASP list while BKN trading, staking and a June–July 2026 token migration remain active.
  - Found via: EU Digital Finance Platform fintech map
  - Check before the call: Obtain counsel's written MiCA perimeter analysis for BKN issuance, staking, migration, P2P transfers and marketplace modules.; Validate ISO certificates against their accredited certification body and obtain current scopes.

**iconicchain** — iconicchain has unusually credible enterprise proof for its size: BayernLB and Helaba independently confirm production use on billion-euro securitisations, and the business is active in Finland and Germany. Its score is held down by a founder profile outside institutional finance, stale disclosed funding, closed technical evidence and an unverifiable ISO certificate. Monitor because the production footprint is strategically relevant despite the low signal-library score.
  - Licences: ISO 27001 certified
  - ⚠️ Review flags: The claimed ISO 27001 certification could not be verified to a public certificate number, certification body, scope and validity period; no credit was awarded.
  - Found via: Helsinki Fintech Farm RegTech landscape
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate, certificate number, accredited body, scope and expiration date.; Confirm the complete founding team and any unpublicised structured-finance or Tier-1 bank operating background.

**BPX Markets (BPX Digital Securities Exchange)** — FCA permissions and an experienced board are credible, but the investable evidence is still pre-live: one announced member, no production case, no public technical diligence estate, stale funding and a DSS scope limitation.
  - Licences: FCA-authorised market operator & AIFM (Jun 2025); Bank of England/FCA Digital Securities Sandbox (Gate 1)
  - ⚠️ Review flags: {'flag': 'DSS permission-scope mismatch requires counsel review', 'evidence': 'Gate 1 permits testing but explicitly does not approve live FMI activity; BPX must reach Gate 2 for live DSD operations.', 'url': 'https://www.bankofengland.co.uk/-/media/boe/files/financial-stability/digital-securities-sandbox/bpx-gate-1-san.pdf'}
  - Found via: Bank of England / FCA Digital Securities Sandbox (Gate 1 SAN)
  - Check before the call: Confirm the exact FCA permission set and any requirements or limitations directly from the full register extract.; Establish timing and conditions for DSS Gate 2 and live digital-securities-depository activity.

**Due** — Due is unusually on-thesis technologically: a live, developer-friendly fiat/stablecoin bridge with verified Spanish MiCA authorisation, EU passporting, credible investors and named production customers. Founder pedigree and Tier-1-bank traction remain weak against the library, while residual B2C positioning dilutes structural fit. The UK contracting/regulatory perimeter is a pre-meeting legal diligence condition.
  - Licences: Spanish MiCA CASP via Due Network, S.L., CIF B16407272
  - ⚠️ Review flags: UK regulatory-perimeter review required: Due Ltd is not FCA-authorised, yet its English-law platform terms describe virtual-asset services; determine the contracting and regulated entity for UK users.
  - Found via: Circle Alliance Program directory
  - Check before the call: Map each customer jurisdiction and service to the exact contracting and licensed group entity, especially in the UK.; Verify the claimed FinCEN, FINTRAC and Bank of Canada registrations in the respective official databases.

**Monerium** — Monerium has unusually credible regulated-token infrastructure, a live developer stack and strong disclosed usage, but the current CASP-licence gap and documented AML remediation are investment-committee vetoes. Its direct consumer line and nearly five-year funding gap also weaken fit for a new Seed/Series A institutional-infrastructure cheque.
  - Licences: Icelandic Electronic Money Institution supervised by the Central Bank of Iceland
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Missing/lapsed relevant permission: the Central Bank of Iceland required Monerium to cease CASP services from 1 July 2026 because it lacked a MiCA CASP licence; EMI issuance of e-money tokens remains authorised.; Adverse regulatory action: the 2024 AML review found deficient enterprise/customer risk assessment, PEP monitoring and sanctions-list monitoring, with remediation and independent review required.
  - Found via: Central Bank of Iceland supervised entities
  - Check before the call: Obtain the independent remediation report required by the Central Bank of Iceland and confirm closure of every 2024 AML finding.; Clarify which current services are EMI/EMT issuance versus CASP activities and inspect any post-cutover CASP application or licence.

**Penzilla** — Penzilla has credible Seed investors, production enterprise customers, quantified outcomes and recurring SaaS economics. It lacks the mandate's required founder pedigree and any MiCA, tokenisation or regulated-rails angle, while security/developer transparency is weak. The claimed §34d brokerage registration must be independently confirmed before any reliance on regulated distribution.
  - Licences: German insurance intermediary registration D-AE6L-AU6NF-18 via Penzilla Pension Solutions GmbH
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Penzilla claims German broker registration D-AE6L-AU6NF-18, but the official DIHK search result could not be retrieved; human register verification is required.
  - Found via: Munich Startup
  - Check before the call: Query D-AE6L-AU6NF-18 directly in the DIHK Vermittlerregister and confirm current status, permitted category and responsible IHK.; Obtain ISO 27001/SOC 2 evidence, penetration-test summary, DPIA and data-processing controls.

**Dedge Security S.L.** — The prior Randed exit, fresh Seed and LF Decentralized Trust participation are credible positives. However, Dedge lacks the required founder-level financial-infrastructure pedigree and has no public financial-institution production proof, certification or developer assurance. Monitor for a named bank deployment, senior FI hire and security certification before meeting.
  - Found via: Crypto Valley Association corporate directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the complete investor syndicate and cap table.; Verify confidential financial-institution customers and production deployment scope.

**CheckSig S.r.l. Società Benefit (CheckSig Clear B2B unit)** — CheckSig is the most strategically aligned company: an active MiCA CASP with a Tier-1-bank founder, Deloitte SOC attestations, patented custody, proof-of-reserves, Bank of Italy validation and a live Banca Profilo/Tinaba deployment. The strict score remains poor because public developer artefacts, named compliance/analytics infrastructure, multi-country reference customers and institutional investors are not evidenced. Clear also sits inside a mixed B2B/B2C parent, warranting monitoring and targeted diligence rather than a pass.
  - Licences: Italian MiCA CASP authorised by CONSOB, 6 May 2026; SOC 1 and SOC 2 Type II attestations
  - Found via: ESMA interim MiCA CASP register
  - Check before the call: Archive the 24 July 2026 ESMA CASP CSV row and obtain the full CONSOB authorisation decision, including services, conditions and passport status.; Obtain the Deloitte SOC 1/SOC 2 Type II reports, control period, exceptions and scope, plus penetration-test and incident history.

**Nexus FrontierTech** — Nexus has unusually credible compliance deployments, including a live global-bank implementation and regulator collaboration, but none of its founders meets the Tier-1 FI thesis requirement. Its closed, lightly evidenced technical surface and absence of digital-asset/tokenisation infrastructure make it a poor Stratum3 fit despite real commercial traction.
  - Found via: Level39 member directory (Data & AI)
  - Check before the call: Confirm whether the UK company is the group's ultimate parent rather than a regional operating subsidiary.; Reconcile $3.8m versus $7.59m funding totals and identify convertible-note investors.

**Softstack GmbH** — Softstack is a credible, active institutional Web3 security specialist with unusually public audit evidence and real enterprise names. It falls below Stratum3's threshold because the founders lack institutional-finance pedigree, financing is opaque, and the model is dominated by services rather than scalable recurring infrastructure revenue.
  - Found via: Blockchain Bundesverband member directory
  - Check before the call: External capital, cap table and true venture stage are undisclosed.; No public revenue, recurring-revenue mix or customer-retention metrics were found.

**Tangany** — Tangany is one of the strongest operating assets in this set: current BaFin/MiCA permissions, €3bn-plus assets under custody, 700,000 accounts and multiple independently verified production institutions. Exact-threshold scoring is pulled down heavily by a founding team without the thesis-required prior FI/digital-asset-infrastructure pedigree and by incomplete public security assurance. Monitor for a thesis-exception discussion, subject to verifying the ISO certificate and current economics.
  - Licences: BaFin MiCA CASP authorisation, Aug 2025; BaFin crypto custody licence 50085612; BaFin PSD2 payment-services licence for e-money tokens, Mar 2026
  - Found via: ESMA interim MiCA CASP register
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current ISO 27001 certificate, scope, certificate number and accredited certification body.; Verify ARR, gross margin, net revenue retention, customer concentration and how the disclosed €3bn assets under custody monetize.

**Minos Global** — Minos has unusually strong current regulatory positioning for its size: CNMV MiCA CASP authorisation, a Banco de España payment-institution listing and a live MyAndbank deployment. The score is held down by unidentified founders, broken public developer surfaces, no independent security evidence, very limited named-customer proof and undisclosed total funding. Monitor for technical diligence rather than advance directly.
  - Licences: CNMV MiCA CASP authorisation; Banco de España VASP registration D672
  - Found via: CNMV MiCA CASP list
  - Check before the call: Identify and diligence the original 2018 founders and reconcile founder ownership with the current management team.; Obtain the cap table and full financing history to resolve the two unknown capital gates.

**Innopay** — The bank-experienced founders and genuine production role in qvik are thesis-relevant, but the company has only Hungary-verified deployment, sparse public security/API evidence, and two unresolved gates: stage and total capital.
  - Licences: Certified aggregator for Hungary's national instant-payment infrastructure
  - Found via: PayTechShow 2025 programme
  - Check before the call: Obtain the cap table, financing history and current fundraising terms to resolve Gates 3 and 4.; Obtain the GIRO certification or aggregator agreement and clarify whether the designation is transferable outside Hungary.

**AAZZUR** — Experienced founders, fresh capital and a broad regulated-partner ecosystem are attractive, but verified customer traction and technical/security transparency lag the strength of the orchestration narrative.
  - Found via: Mastercard Start Path New Networks 2025
  - Check before the call: Confirm exact group structure and operating substance in Germany.; Obtain paid ARR, provider take rates, named production customers and partner dependency metrics.

**Roaring** — Roaring has the strongest verified commercial evidence in this set—SEB production use, named references, quantified onboarding, SEK 56m 2025 revenue and 1,910% four-year growth—but founder pedigree, security certification, standards participation and financing-stage clarity remain weak against Stratum3's signal contract. No licence veto applies because Roaring is a software/data supplier, not the regulated open-banking provider.
  - Found via: Swedish FinTech Association member directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm formal round stages and obtain a fully reconciled financing history.; Verify founder/co-founder identities and full prior-employer timelines directly.

**Permisso** — Permisso passes all gates and has meaningful positives: a verified Spanish qualified certificate service, live APIs and demos, strong named customer testimonials, quantified workflow outcomes, a JME-led Seed round and active expansion into four European markets. The strict score remains poor because founder fit is weak against Stratum3's Tier-1 infrastructure bias, external bank-origin validation is absent, several security/compliance controls are asserted rather than independently evidenced and the financing is now stale.
  - Licences: Spanish qualified trust service provider for electronic certificates
  - Found via: EBA PSD2 central register
  - Check before the call: Export and archive the live EU/Spanish trusted-list entry for Beyond the Scope S.L., including service status, start date, QSCD scope and any withdrawal history.; Obtain the AENOR ISO 27001 certificate and ENS certification details, with current scope and expiry.

**tell.money** — The product is live and demonstrably embedded with payment providers, but current FCA status remains unverified and the founding team misses the fund's Tier-1-FI criterion. Public security certification and AML-governance evidence also remain thin, keeping a commercially credible regulated-rails vendor below meeting threshold.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Human review required: Tell Money claims regulated payment-institution status, but the current FCA firm card could not be verified and no public AML framework or named MLRO was located.
  - Found via: Open Banking technical service providers directory
  - Check before the call: Pull the full FCA firm card for FRN 924109 and verify current permissions, limitations and disciplinary history directly in a browser.; Confirm whether additional financing occurred after May 2022.

**Omocom** — Omocom has unusually strong regulatory and funding momentum: a verified Swedish insurance concession, 40 platform relationships reported in 2023, production cross-border deployments, public API documentation and fresh 2026 insurer capital. It remains off the core founder thesis and lacks public security assurance, in-house compliance leadership and quantified enterprise proof, so monitor rather than meet.
  - Licences: Swedish insurance undertaking authorization
  - Found via: Inventure portfolio
  - Check before the call: Pull Omocom's exact current Swedish FI company-register record and every active EEA passport notification, including pending classes 10 and 13.; Clarify ownership and governance rights attached to Länsförsäkringar's approximately 39.6% stake.

**Bizcuit** — The active DNB AISP/PISP licence, EEA passports, Rabobank production use and dense integration set are real strengths. However, the founder lacks the required Tier-1 institutional pedigree, customer evidence is overwhelmingly domestic SME-focused, funding and stage remain unverified, and eight confirmed anti-signals reduce the weighted fit from 0.338 to 0.098.
  - Licences: Dutch Central Bank payment institution R161341; AISP and PISP
  - Found via: Dutch Central Bank public register
  - Check before the call: Obtain shareholder/UBO and cumulative capital records to resolve G3, G4 and cheque fit.; Request the current AML governance pack, named MLRO, ISO 27001/SOC 2 evidence and third-party security audit.

**Certifaction** — Certifaction has credible privacy-first technology, a live BX Swiss deployment, more than 500,000 HIN signatures and a valuable Deutsche Telekom channel. However, the founding team lacks the thesis-required Tier-1 financial-institution or digital-asset-infrastructure pedigree, and direct regulatory assurance is limited because qualified certificates come from partner CSPs. The official Swiss CSP list names Swisscom, not Certifaction.
  - Licences: QES delivered under eIDAS and Swiss ZertES - appears to be via a partner QTSP, not held directly
  - Found via: EU Trusted List / Swiss ZertES trust service sweep
  - Check before the call: Request current ARR, financial-services revenue share and customer retention after the 2022 financing.; Obtain the latest cap table, runway and financing plan.

**Trustfull** — Trustfull is investable by gate and shows real production traction with banks plus excellent institutional backing. However, the founder profile misses the fund's Tier-1 FI/digital-asset infrastructure requirement, while public technology assurance is thinner than expected for an identity-risk vendor. Commercial quality does not overcome the core thesis mismatch.
  - Found via: EBAday Fintech Zone 2025
  - Check before the call: Obtain the actual ISO 27001 certificate and verify body, scope and expiry.; Confirm audited ARR, net retention and bank revenue concentration.

**Oblivious** — The company passes all gates and has unusually credible privacy engineering, an independently verified ISO 27001 certificate, public developer materials and a UN pilot. Under the contract's strict evidence thresholds, however, the absence of named financial-institution customers, low external developer adoption, limited financial-services pedigree and a five-round Seed financing history keep the risk-adjusted fit in the poor band.
  - Licences: ISO 27001; SOC 2 Type 2
  - Found via: Central Bank of Ireland Innovation Sandbox report
  - Check before the call: Obtain the SOC 2 Type 2 report and bridge letter under NDA; DQS independently verifies ISO 27001, but SOC 2 is only publicly claimed.; Identify named bank customers, production scope, ARR, retention and quantified privacy/compliance outcomes.

**Percayso Inform** — Percayso has unusually credible insurance traction for its size: £3.0m contracted ARR was reported by its investor, public production deployments include Aviva and Marshmallow, and the founder has a prior LexisNexis exit. It nevertheless sits outside Stratum3's core digital-finance-infrastructure edge: it is UK-only insurance data middleware with no regulated rails, tokenisation, MiCA or cross-border architecture, while API and security assurance remain largely gated.
  - Licences: ICO registration ZA462608
  - Found via: Gallagher Re Global InsurTech Report
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current NQA ISO 27001 certificate, certificate number, scope and expiry date.; Confirm current ARR, gross margin, net revenue retention and the effect of the customer churn disclosed by Praetura.

**Neterium** — Neterium is exceptionally on-thesis at product level and has unusually strong production proof: Erste Group reports a 70% false-positive reduction across a 17-million-customer footprint, while SAS independently documents Orange Bank's live use. The score is pulled down by a founding team without bank/regulator credentials under the library's strict thresholds, absent public certificate identifiers and developer artifacts, and a completely opaque financing history. It merits direct diligence, but stage and ownership must be resolved before a meeting is prioritised.
  - Found via: FinTech Belgium members
  - Check before the call: Confirm whether the company is bootstrapped or externally financed, and obtain the complete cap table, historic capital injections and current valuation expectations.; Obtain the current ISO 27001 certificate, certification body, certificate number and scope, plus the SOC 2 Type II report under NDA.

**Spiko** — Spiko is the clearest tokenisation-rail fit, with a live MiFID wrapper, passporting, open-source contracts, institutional partners and fast AUM growth. It is already a stretch cheque and retains B2C exposure, while public security and AML-governance evidence is thinner than expected for a regulated firm. Move quickly only after validating the compliance-control stack and round economics.
  - Licences: AMF/ACPR-supervised MiFID investment firm; identifier unknown
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Human review: regulated investment firm; public materials did not establish a substantive AML framework or named MLRO.
  - Found via: France FinTech FFT24 speakers
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current AML framework, named MLRO, ISO/SOC evidence and smart-contract audit reports.; Separate infrastructure revenue from fund-management and retail revenue.

**A352 (Financial Navigator)** — A352 has a credible payments-experienced team, a prior Daimler exit, live enterprise references and fresh public-private support. It nevertheless scores poorly because product/security evidence is almost entirely sales-gated, no financial-institution production customer is public, and the regulated-services perimeter is not documented clearly enough for an institutional infrastructure investment.
  - Found via: EBAday Fintech Zone 2025
  - Check before the call: Obtain a written regulatory-perimeter analysis confirming that payment automation never constitutes PSD2 payment initiation or funds handling.; Verify ARR, growth, retention, gross margin and the number and geography of paying customers.

**Pinecone (Pine & Cone Oy)** — Pinecone is a real thematic match: a founder with four years at OP Financial Group, regulated customer-funds operations, production use at Palkkaus.fi, an active EPC SRTP entry and direct EBA Clearing participation. The company is nevertheless extremely small, loss-making, opaque on funding and customers, and has weak public API/security assurance. It merits a licence-confirmation call and traction diligence, not an immediate pass.
  - Licences: Finnish Payment Institution supervised by FIN-FSA; SEPA Request-to-Pay scheme participant; LEI 74370045F1MCE3UWF477
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Payment-institution status is repeatedly claimed by operating partners, but the entity-level FIN-FSA/EBA register record could not be extracted from the public dynamic register; licence number, current authorisation status, permitted services and passporting must be confirmed before reliance.
  - Found via: Helsinki Fintech Guide
  - Check before the call: Extract the exact Pine & Cone Oy entry from FIN-FSA and EBA PIR, including authorisation date, payment services, status and EEA passports.; Obtain current 2025 financials, cash runway, ownership/capital history and the exact amount invested by founders or external backers.

**Quantum Dice** — Quantum Dice has differentiated patented entropy assurance, a shipped Thales HSM integration, credible Oxford research, strong non-dilutive funding and an active enterprise channel. It lacks a finance-native founder, a named financial-institution deployment and public assurance artifacts, while its new ORBIT strategy broadens away from the fund's core digital-finance thesis.
  - Found via: IQT The Hague quantum-cybersecurity programme
  - Check before the call: Identify named paying financial-services customers, production volumes, ACV, recurring QEaaS revenue and the split between pilots and deployments.; Obtain ISO/SOC status, penetration-test and cryptographic-module validation evidence, plus details of entropy certification against NIST/AIS standards.

**DLT Finance** — The MiCAR/BaFin licences, Deutsche Bank founder experience, API product and named live institutional deployments are material positives. The recent final AML enforcement is a veto under the contract, reinforced by weak public compliance governance and security assurance.
  - Licences: DLT Securities GmbH German MiCAR CASP authorisation, granted 18 Dec 2025; DLT Securities GmbH BaFin ID 10159536
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Adverse regulatory action: final BaFin AML fines in February 2026.
  - Found via: ESMA Interim MiCA Register - Germany
  - Check before the call: Evidence of remediation after the BaFin AML findings; Named MLRO, transaction-monitoring and Travel Rule vendors

**ComplyPay** — ComplyPay is unusually strong on regulated programmable-payment infrastructure, regulator engagement and B2B purity, with live EEA distribution. Founder pedigree misses the thesis's Tier-1-FI requirement, security transparency is thin, and financing momentum has gone stale despite compelling product-market alignment.
  - Licences: Danish Payment Institution under PSD2, FTID 22043, passported across the EEA
  - Found via: Danish FSA company register
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current Danish FSA licence extract for FTID 22043 and reconcile it with the lapsed 2021 exempt-PI EBA record.; Request current ARR, payment volume, customer retention and concentration by platform.

**Wultra** — Wultra has unusually strong customer, security, technical and fresh-financing evidence, including bank-originated production confirmation. Strict founder-background and regulatory-policy signals remain weak, and the company is now eleven years old despite only reaching Series A. It merits a meeting only if ARR growth, gross margins and the 2026 round valuation support venture-scale returns.
  - Found via: Czech Fintech Association industry report
  - Check before the call: Verify ARR, 2024-2026 growth, gross margin, net retention and customer concentration.; Inspect the full ISO certificate, penetration-test report and product-specific assurance scope.

**eID Easy** — eID Easy has the best evidenced product and commercial footprint in this set: live APIs, a free test environment, frequent releases, global method coverage and quantified customer cases. It remains a weak Stratum3 fit because the founding team lacks institutional-finance pedigree, named financial-institution production evidence is thin, the cap table is fragmented across numerous rounds, and a registry notice needs explanation. Monitor for a bank-grade case study and corporate-filing resolution.
  - Found via: eID Easy EUDI Wallet member-state rollout status tracker
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current cap table and explain the Estonian deletion notice and remediation history.; Independently validate the current ISO 27001 certificate, scope, issuing body and expiry.

**Roseman Labs** — Roseman combines rare cryptographic depth, current security assurance, public developer documentation, regulator-backed validation and fresh institutional capital. The investment question is commercial conversion: public evidence still does not identify a production bank, contract economics or finance-sector renewal/ROI.
  - Found via: Holland FinTech network directory
  - Check before the call: Name and reference the banks in the Irish sandbox and live German inter-bank network; confirm pilot-to-production dates.; Obtain finance-sector ARR, ACV, gross margin, sales-cycle, renewal and customer-concentration data.

**Credenco** — Credenco is a live Dutch business-wallet vendor with credible standards work, EU grant validation and unusually concrete public-sector use cases. The score is held down by unverified founder provenance, no named production financial-institution customer, and absent published security certification or audit. Monitor for a priced seed round, verified founders and conversion of pilots into production.
  - Found via: EWC LSP consortium / WE BUILD consortium (RFC contributors)
  - Check before the call: Identify the legal founders and verify their complete employment histories, ownership and any Tier-1 financial-institution experience.; Obtain a KVK extract and cap table to confirm independence, current directors, stage and cumulative capital below €30m.

**Lemonero** — Lemonero is live, well inside the cheque ceiling and has credible embedded-lending integrations, bank backing and measurable merchant outcomes. It misses the fund's founder-background requirement, has no public production bank case study, and exposes little developer or security evidence. The capital history also appears stale despite an unspecified 2023 follow-on.
  - Found via: Visa Innovation Program Europe Spain and Portugal 2024
  - Check before the call: Confirm whether the 2023 and March 2025 capital increases included external funding and determine the current fully diluted cap table.; Obtain the current AML governance, MLRO ownership and legal analysis of the B2B-lending licensing perimeter in each operating market.

**Xiphera Oy** — Xiphera has exceptional cryptographic depth, NIST-validated cores, patents, profitable growth and credible hardware partnerships. It only narrowly fits the infrastructure gate through HSM and key-management use cases; no founder, customer or deployment evidence connects it materially to institutional finance. Pass for this fund unless diligence uncovers a substantial confidential financial-institution customer base.
  - Found via: European Quantum Industry Consortium member list
  - Check before the call: Obtain a complete schedule of Business Finland, ESA and EU grants to resolve G4 and cheque fit.; Request financial-services customer references and quantify revenue attributable to banks, custodians, HSMs or institutional wallets.

**AMLYZE** — AMLYZE is alive, independent and squarely on thesis, with unusually strong proof for a seed-stage RegTech: regulator-founder pedigree, an open AML framework, a national-regulator sandbox, public API docs, and quantified production outcomes from Wallter. The strict library still produces a poor fit because it heavily rewards Tier-1 founder credentials, regulatory licences that do not apply to a software vendor, and public security/developer artefacts. Monitor closely and advance only after validating economics, certificate scope and referenceability beyond the Baltics.
  - Licences: Participant, Central Bank of Ireland Innovation Sandbox
  - Found via: EU Digital Finance Platform fintech map
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate, certificate number, scope, expiry, latest penetration-test summary and incident history.; Request current ARR, 2025/2026 growth, gross margin, burn, runway, NRR, customer concentration and contracted pipeline.

**mintBlue** — mintBlue demonstrates genuine enterprise production use, strong developer accessibility and quantified government/accounting deployments. However, no founder meets the required Tier-1 FI threshold on verified evidence, formal security certification is absent, and the investor/funding profile is stale. The three confirmed anti-signals reduce the pre-deduction 0.3253 fit to 0.2353.
  - Found via: Holland FinTech member directory
  - Check before the call: Verify any undisclosed financing after March 2022 directly with management and the Dutch filings.; Obtain founder CVs to conclusively resolve Tier-1 financial-institution tenure.

**Sikoia** — Sikoia has unusually strong institutional investors, an experienced JPMorgan engineering founder, public APIs and credible production traction at Tandem Bank and major lending platforms. However, the directly verifiable regulatory evidence is incomplete, public security certification is weak, international traction is unproven and the product has no digital-asset or tokenisation relevance. Pass for Stratum3's specific thesis, while recognising a credible UK lending-data infrastructure company.
  - Licences: FCA Account Information Service Provider, FRN 942979; FCA credit reference provider, FRN 942616
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Manual FCA register review required: FRNs 942979 and 942616 are repeatedly claimed and customer-corroborated, but the firm-specific regulator record could not be retrieved in this environment.
  - Found via: Earlybird portfolio
  - Check before the call: Manually retrieve FCA firm records for FRNs 942979 and 942616 and confirm current status, permissions, restrictions and trading names.; Obtain the current ISO 27001 certificate, certification body, scope, statement of applicability and latest penetration-test summary.

**Uniify** — Uniify has a relevant former-Nordea founder, broad live integration coverage, public API guidance, national eIDs and a quantified onboarding case. The score is held down by weak production-bank evidence, limited founder depth beyond one bank alumnus, stale financing and an unsubstantiated ISO claim. Monitor for bank references, security evidence and a refreshed round.
  - Found via: Copenhagen Fintech current members
  - Check before the call: Obtain named regulated-bank references, deployment volumes, renewal status and quantified outcomes.; Review the ISO 27001 certificate, audit body, scope, expiry and penetration-test results.

**Montonio** — Montonio is the strongest operating company in this batch on merchant traction, developer usability and product transparency: 8,000 merchants, a current PI licence, ten passports, working APIs, status history, monthly releases and quantified customer outcomes. It nevertheless scores poorly for Stratum3 because the heavily weighted founder signals are absent and the business is ecommerce checkout/logistics rather than institutional digital-asset infrastructure. A good company can still be a poor thesis fit.
  - Licences: Bank of Lithuania payment institution authorisation LB002007
  - Found via: Bank of Lithuania financial market participants
  - Check before the call: Reconcile the nearly €19m current funding claim with the €13.5m publicly announced rounds and date any later financing.; Obtain founder-by-founder employment histories and current ownership after the 2024–26 cap-table changes.

**Performativ** — Strong founder-market fit, production ROI evidence and strategic financial-infrastructure investors make this a credible institutional platform. The largest thesis gap is that it remains conventional wealth software with no tokenisation or regulated digital-asset rail. Verify security certification and whether the Series A has fully closed before advancing.
  - Found via: Copenhagen Fintech current members
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current ISO 27001 certificate and scope, or SOC 2 Type II report.; Confirm whether the conditional Series A closing completed and reconcile total funding including the 2021 round.

**Fraudio** — Fraudio has unusually strong, quantified production traction and current international momentum in payment-fraud infrastructure. It lacks the fund's required Tier-1 FI or digital-asset founder pedigree, has no MiCA/tokenisation angle, and exposes limited security assurance beyond an unverifiable ISO claim. Commercial quality is better than the poor thesis-fit score, but the mismatch is structural.
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: Holland FinTech network directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain exact 2025 and 2026 round sizes and the post-cleanup cap table.; Request the ISO 27001 certificate, certification body, scope and expiry date.

**Aequitec** — Aequitec is sharply relevant infrastructure and has rare independent proof from SIX of a live tokenised-equity/CSD deployment. The fund-specific score remains poor because the team is very small, qualifying founder pedigree is limited, customer geography is narrow, public security/API evidence is weak and the precise 2026 financing status is unclear. The SDX and SCION-SSFN integrations justify monitoring rather than a clean pass.
  - Found via: IFZ FinTech Study Switzerland
  - Check before the call: Reconcile the CHF0.75m disclosed funding with the April 2026 claim conversion, identify investors, valuation and current round stage.; Obtain ARR, net retention, gross margin, active issuer count, assets under administration and country mix.

**CENSE** — Cense combines a prior founder exit, deep Glassnode heritage, a fresh strategic bank-CVC round, an independently confirmed production bank deployment, a quantified 12-to-2-week workflow improvement and more than 10 bank relationships. Its explainable deterministic analytics and TradFi-DeFi evidence layer fit the thesis unusually well. The diligence priority is enterprise security assurance: the site does not substantiate its SOC 2 language with a report or auditor, and the API is not publicly inspectable.
  - Found via: Rabo Investments CENSE portfolio entry
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current SOC 2 report or ISO 27001 certificate, auditor, scope, exceptions, penetration-test summary and subprocessor inventory.; Confirm whether FINMA or an SRO has issued a formal no-action or regulatory perimeter assessment for read-only wallet and exchange data collection.

**ComplyCube** — ComplyCube combines a founder with eleven years of relevant Barclays experience, self-serve developer infrastructure, high reported usage, public pricing and credible production customer evidence. The official UK trust-framework certificate has expired without public renewal evidence, and the ISO claim lacks a verifiable certificate, so diligence should stop short of a meeting until certification status is cured.
  - Licences: UK DIATF; ISO 27001; ISO 30107-3 Level 2 PAD
  - ⚠️ Review flags: VETO — official register shows ComplyCube Platform certificate expiry on 8 April 2026; no renewed certificate was found as of 30 July 2026.
  - Found via: UK DSIT DIATF certified digital identity provider register
  - Check before the call: Renewed UK DIATF conformity certificate after 8 April 2026; Current ISO 27001 certificate number, scope and accredited certification body

**Hoptroff** — Hoptroff has differentiated patented technology, authoritative NIST/NPL evidence and a genuine production global-bank deployment. It nevertheless misses the required FI-founder criterion, has remained early-stage after numerous rounds, and does not publish verifiable ISO certificates or open technical interfaces; no financial-services licence is required for timing-data delivery.
  - Found via: Anthemis portfolio
  - Check before the call: Clarify Purebond Limited's control rights and whether the 2025 transaction changed independence.; Obtain ISO certificates, ARR, renewal rates and named Tier-1 bank references.

**RegGenome (Regulatory Genome Development Ltd)** — RegGenome is a live Cambridge compliance-data spin-out with strong regulatory engagement, credible institutional investors, partner integrations and a valuable multi-year ADB contract. Strict signal scoring is held down by stale financing, weak public proof of named financial-institution production customers and absent public security assurance. Three confirmed anti-signals reduce its already sub-threshold raw fit to poor.
  - Found via: AlbionVC portfolio
  - Check before the call: Clarify the June 2026 Mark Johnston director resignation and current executive leadership.; Request ARR, renewal, gross-margin and concentration data behind the claimed reach to approximately 600 regulated organisations.

**Finexer** — Finexer is alive, independent and demonstrably regulated: Open Banking Limited links the active FCA record, while Sifted reports 250% two-year revenue CAGR, £0.6m latest revenue and only £0.7m of funding. Named customer references, a free sandbox, seven Capterra reviews and government-backed seed capital show efficient early traction. The main gaps are institutional depth and defensibility: no named FI production customer, no public API specification, no ISO 27001/SOC 2 or published audit, a very small team, and UK-only market and licence coverage.
  - Licences: UK open-banking provider listed by Open Banking Limited
  - Found via: Open Banking regulated providers directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm FCA permission details, any limitations and the current status directly from a machine-readable FCA register extract.; Reconcile the 2019 legal incorporation date with Sifted's 2023 product launch year and establish the current financing stage.

**Mitigrate** — Mitigrate has credible insurer deployment, a live Gjensidige agreement and strong structural B2B fit. It lacks the required founder pedigree in Tier-1 finance or digital-asset infrastructure and has little public API/security assurance, while its product does not touch MiCA, tokenisation or regulated rails.
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab Cohort 11
  - Check before the call: Obtain the Horizon Europe grant agreement number and exact non-dilutive funding amounts.; Confirm whether the AXA Climate relationship is a live production integration and request named UK/DACH customer references.

**Centiglobe** — Centiglobe is a strategically relevant, institution-only payment rail with unusual external validation from the IMF, a Mastercard Move integration, production use with Zimpler and a former FI CEO on the board. The fit remains poor because the founder lacks financial-institution experience, disclosed capital is stale and unclear, and core enterprise diligence artefacts—public API documentation, ISO/SOC evidence, audit reports and quantified bank-customer cases—are absent. The no-licence conclusion rests on the disclosed model in which Centiglobe never holds funds and regulated members and custodians do; that perimeter needs legal confirmation.
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: Swedish FinTech Association annual report
  - Check before the call: Confirm the exact cash drawn under Confidus's €5m-plus commitment and reconcile any later undisclosed funding.; Obtain external legal analysis confirming that Centiglobe's tokenised-deposit and settlement design stays outside PI/EMI and MiCAR/CASP authorisation perimeters in every launch market.

**Condukt** — Condukt combines unusually relevant founder operating experience, current FCA sandbox engagement, strong investors and named production users across European fintech. Its evidence gaps are mostly diligence-readiness issues: no customer-originated release, quantified case study, public API surface or verifiable security certificate. It is not itself conducting an evidenced regulated activity, so absence from the FCA register is not a licence veto.
  - Found via: Fintech District RegTech community list
  - Check before the call: Obtain customer reference calls and quantify auto-approval, analyst-time, conversion and false-positive outcomes.; Review ISO 27001 certificate, SOC 2 Type II report, penetration-test summary and data-residency/subprocessor inventory.

**vestr** — vestr is unusually close to the operational core of institutional finance, has a qualifying Tier-1 bank founder and a verified Julius Baer deployment. Its stale financing, anonymous customer concentration and lack of public security assurance prevent a meet recommendation despite impressive company-claimed scale. Revisit after verifying ARR, recent growth and security diligence.
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: Elevator Lab (Raiffeisen Bank International)
  - Check before the call: Reconcile total capital raised, including the undisclosed 2020 Series A and debt/non-dilutive portion of CHF10m financing.; Verify named production customers behind the five top-15 European bank claim and associated ARR concentration.

**Trust Anchor Group (Neuro)** — The company has live revenue, named customers, a standards-active technical founder and useful IBM validation. However, founder Tier-1 FI fit is unproven, institutional production evidence is thin, and the licence/AML gap conflicts with its payment and exchange claims. The low fit score and veto flag make this a pass pending regulatory clarification.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Potentially regulated payment and crypto-asset activities are marketed, but no licence, AML policy or named MLRO was found in the Swedish FI register or company materials.
  - Found via: Swedish FinTech Association member directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the cap table, dated round history and current stage.; Clarify whether regulated payment/token activities are delivered by a licensed partner or group entity.

**Investre** — The active MiFID passport, unique Control Agent positioning, current €8.6m funding total and fund-industry logos make Investre strategically interesting. Strict signal fit is 0.3092 before four confirmed anti-signal deductions, mainly because founder pedigree misses the thesis threshold, technical assurance is opaque, production references are unverified and B2C remains in scope.
  - Licences: CSSF-authorised investment firm; First CSSF-authorised Control Agent under Luxembourg Blockchain Law IV
  - Found via: CSSF Blockchain Law IV Control Agent authorisations
  - Check before the call: Obtain a dated CSSF entity extract showing the precise Control Agent permission and current investment-firm scope.; Confirm which trusted-logo firms are paying production customers and request deployment dates, volumes and reference calls.

**Vereign AG** — Vereign is alive, capital-efficient and has strong open-source standards participation plus a quantified national-scale healthcare deployment. It nonetheless misses the fund's central founder pedigree and lacks a named financial-institution production customer, current security certification and recent financing. The differentiated technology merits tracking, but present thesis fit is poor after confirmed anti-signals.
  - Found via: Analyst web search of Swiss verifiable-credential vendors; UID and incorporation date from imprint
  - Check before the call: Confirm recurring revenue, ARR and commercial contract value with HIN.; Obtain an official cap table and verify whether any post-2020 financing is undisclosed.

**Adclear** — The company has unusually strong early commercial proof: 13 paying customers, mid-five-digit ACVs, multi-year contracts and a quantified production case. It still scores poorly for this specific thesis because the founders lack qualifying bank/regulator pedigree, security assurance is not publicly evidenced, the technical ecosystem is closed, and the product is workflow compliance rather than core transaction or identity infrastructure.
  - Found via: Finextra funding coverage
  - Check before the call: Obtain ARR, net retention, gross margin, contracted backlog and customer concentration.; Review SOC 2/ISO 27001 status, penetration-test reports, model-validation controls and hallucination/error metrics.

**Q*Bird** — Q*Bird has strong, patented quantum-security technology, current EU backing and credible institutional deployments. It lacks the required Tier-1 finance founder DNA, has only a bank demonstration rather than production deployment, and remains primarily broad critical-infrastructure cybersecurity rather than digital-finance infrastructure.
  - Found via: IQT The Hague quantum-cybersecurity programme
  - Check before the call: Confirm whether ABN AMRO or another financial institution has moved from demonstration to paid production.; Obtain cap-table, recurring-revenue and customer-country detail.

**Dydon AI** — Dydon has the strongest founder pedigree and clearest bank production evidence in the set: a prior exit, Deutsche Bank co-founder and named German Landesbank adoption. Strict technology-assurance signals, pure-TradFi positioning and unresolved financing/stage keep it below meeting threshold.
  - Licences: Finance Innovation Finance Durable et Solidaire label
  - Found via: IFZ Swiss FinTech Study
  - Check before the call: Confirm cumulative funding, ownership, profitability, financing appetite and whether Dydon is genuinely Seed/Series A investable.; Obtain the live financial-institution customer list, ARR, contract values, retention, deployment status and quantified ROI.

**TECHT Labs** — The product direction and standards engagement fit the thesis, but the evidence base is pre-commercial: no named bank deployment, public API, security certification or disclosed funding. DSS participation is useful validation but currently authorises only non-live testing. Monitor for Gate-2 approval, a named production customer and institutional security proof.
  - Licences: Bank of England/FCA Digital Securities Sandbox
  - ⚠️ Review flags: DSS approval is Gate 1 only: non-live testing is permitted, but TECHT is not approved for live FMI activity or FCA-authorised for that activity.; Website markets stablecoin and on/off-ramp capabilities; confirm the regulated entity and permissions before treating these as live services.
  - Found via: Level39 member directory (Blockchain) + BoE/FCA Digital Securities Sandbox
  - Check before the call: Verify financing stage, total capital raised and cap table directly with management.; Identify whether either founder has a qualifying Tier-1 financial-institution or digital-asset-infrastructure background.

**CleverChain** — CleverChain has credible FCA Sandbox validation, current awards and a strategically useful Experian partnership. The public record does not establish a Tier-1-FI founder, named production bank customer, completed security certification or clean institutional round structure. The score should remain conservative until diligence replaces those gaps.
  - Licences: FCA Regulatory Sandbox
  - Found via: FCA Regulatory Sandbox - accepted firms
  - Check before the call: Confirm exact stage, total capital, investors and current cap table directly from filings.; Obtain named bank references and production metrics.

**Bankish** — Bankish is an active Estonian enterprise-software company with unusually experienced financial-sector founders, a live Finora deployment and a genuine reseller channel. The investment case is weakened by minimal registered headcount, declining 2025 revenue, continuing losses and related-party debt exposure, plus weak public security assurance and limited independent customer proof. Its current ownership appears mixed rather than wholly controlled by Finora, but the group relationship warrants cap-table diligence.
  - Found via: EU Digital Finance Platform fintech map
  - Check before the call: Reconcile the EUR 1m-plus seed with database estimates ranging from roughly USD 1.37m to USD 4.8m.; Obtain the current cap table, shareholder agreement and details of Finora's related-party loans and provisions.

**Getpaid (getpaid.io)** — Getpaid has the best founder-product fit and developer transparency among the payment middleware candidates, and its Finnish PI licence is independently active and passported. The principal gaps are commercial proof, compliance leadership visibility and stale financing; the Spanish subsidiary closure needs a direct explanation but is not a licence veto.
  - Licences: EU payment-institution licence; number not pinned; PCI DSS Level 1
  - Found via: Picus Capital portfolio
  - Check before the call: Confirm the exact Finnish home-authority licence entry, permitted services, safeguarding banks and passport map.; Explain the March 2026 Spanish subsidiary dissolution and confirm where Madrid engineers are now employed.

**Token City** — An active CNMV investment-firm record, regulator-run DLT sandbox, current API and live regulated issuance are compelling; funding opacity, security gaps, a crowded investor base and retail access reduce fit.
  - Licences: CNMV-authorised investment firm establishment; Completed CNMV financial sandbox testing
  - Found via: CNMV financial sandbox conclusions
  - Check before the call: Confirm total raised and cap table.; Verify DLT Pilot Regime authorization status.

**Coinscrap Finance** — Coinscrap has unusually strong, independently confirmed production bank deployments and a credible recurring B2B model. It lacks the thesis-critical founder pedigree, DACH/Nordic/UK penetration and digital-asset adjacency. The unresolved wording around 'our PSD2 license' is a veto-level diligence issue despite likely reliance on licensed partner Afterbanks.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: PSD2 licence claim is not supported by a Coinscrap register entry; the regulated connectivity appears to rely on third-party provider Afterbanks/Minsait.
  - Found via: AEFI Infraestructura Financiera vertical
  - Check before the call: Obtain the contracting entity, PSD2 permission number and written allocation of AISP/PISP responsibilities with Afterbanks/Minsait.; Verify a current ISO 27001 certificate number, scope, issuing body and expiry.

**Yavrio** — Yavrio has impressive early commercial execution: 250+ customers/sign-ups, strong channel distribution, production references, quantified ROI and multi-rail global coverage. It misses the fund's highest-weight founder criterion and lacks direct FCA authorisation, a named compliance leader and financial-institution customers. Pass under the current thesis despite a commercially promising product.
  - Found via: Tech.eu funding coverage
  - Check before the call: Obtain the live FCA register record for FRN 974097 and confirm principal, permissions, territorial scope and any restrictions.; Verify Cyber Essentials certificate number, level and expiry on the IASME register; request SOC 2 or ISO 27001 roadmap.

**PXL Vision AG** — PXL Vision is alive, independent and commercially real: it has a prior founder exit, an official FINMA consultation submission, current security assurance, production deployment at Swiss Life, several named financial customers and a strong institutional seed syndicate. It remains below Stratum3's bar because the founders lack financial-infrastructure pedigree, developer evidence is closed, the last priced round is old, cross-market traction is not proven, and the product has no explicit digital-asset or DeFi bridge. Monitor for a fresh round, verified ARR and a clearer EUDIW or digital-finance infrastructure wedge.
  - Licences: ISAE 3000 assurance for FINMA-compliant video and online identification controls
  - ⚠️ Review flags: The supplied 'ISAE 3000 assurance for FINMA-compliant controls' is not a regulatory licence and could not be substantiated as stated. Current public evidence instead names KPMG ISAE 3402 Type 2 control assurance and TÜV Rheinland ISO/IEC 27001:2022 certification. FINMA/BaFin compliance language describes customer-process capability, not PXL Vision authorisation.
  - Found via: IFZ FinTech Study Switzerland
  - Check before the call: Obtain current ARR, growth, gross margin, retention, customer concentration and production verification volumes by institution and country.; Reconcile the CHF4.6m announced seed total, any 2019 financing and the CHF2.19m 2025 debt-to-equity conversion; confirm current cash runway and fully diluted ownership.

**Riva Money** — Riva clears all eligibility gates and has a regulator-verified Swedish PI licence, strong founder payments experience, a credible Project A-led round and one quantified production customer. The evidence base is nevertheless too early for this fund's institutional-infrastructure bar: no FI customer, thin public security/compliance controls, no verified passporting, no FCA authorisation and an unverified Swiss VQF claim. The 0.1804 fit includes a 0.12 anti-signal deduction.
  - Licences: UK payment-institution authorisation application; EU payment-institution authorisation application
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Swiss VASP/VQF status is company-claimed but not independently verifiable in an accessible public regulator/SRO register; obtain a current VQF membership extract before underwriting Swiss virtual-asset activity.
  - Found via: Tech.eu funding coverage
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current VQF membership extract and scope for Riva Money Switzerland AG.; Check the Swedish PI's live EEA passport notifications and the status/reference of the FCA application.

**Kord** — Kord has a Tier-1-bank founder, strong recent growth, named ROI evidence and a fresh Series A within core cheque size.
