Its institutional-finance fit is weakened by UK-only traction, residual consumer activity and limited regulatory-market depth. Do not advance before independently confirming both FCA records and exact permissions.
  - Licences: Checkboard Limited FCA FRN 950508; Kord Pay Limited FCA FRN 929773
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Current company terms claim FCA FRNs 950508 and 929773, but the live FCA records and permission scopes were not independently confirmed; an older company page described Checkboard as Yapily's appointed representative.
  - Found via: FinTech Futures funding roundup
  - Check before the call: Retrieve live FCA-register records for Checkboard Limited FRN 950508 and Kord Pay Limited FRN 929773, including status, permissions and principal relationships.; Confirm whether the consumer Checkboard app generates revenue and what share of business is genuinely institutional.

**Diligent AI (godiligent.ai)** — Diligent has unusually strong early production traction, quantified customer ROI, security assurance and Tier-1 VC validation. Fit is constrained by the absence of a Tier-1 bank/regulator founder and limited public evidence of standards engagement, public audit artefacts or mature developer infrastructure. It is worth monitoring toward Series A and meeting if founder-thesis flexibility exists.
  - Found via: Speedinvest portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate, scope, issuer and expiry; review the SOC 2 Type II report under NDA.; Verify ARR, net retention, gross margin, sales cycle, customer concentration and how many named deployments are fully paid production contracts.

**Tesseract (Tesseract Investment Oy)** — Authorization and production evidence are real, but the regulated entity is closer to an asset manager with retail capability than a pure institutional-infrastructure company, and financing is stale.
  - Licences: Finnish MiCA CASP supervised by FIN-FSA
  - Found via: Swedish FI crypto register passporting entries
  - Check before the call: Pull the primary FIN-FSA/ESMA record.; Verify ISO/SOC scope and entity.

**Brickken** — Brickken has real developer artefacts, recent funding, broad reported volume and verified EU Sandbox admission. It misses Stratum3's core founder signal, lacks named regulated-FI production proof, has a crowded financing history and retains material retail/token exposure.
  - Check before the call: Obtain the full cap table and reconcile 13 rounds, 23 investors and cumulative proceeds.; Confirm named regulated-financial-institution customers, recurring revenue, retention and tokenised volume methodology.

**Supercede** — Supercede passes every gate and shows unusually concrete institutional adoption for a Series A: production use by Hiscox, Lockton Re and Convex, customers across four continents, more than $61bn of underlying premium reported on-platform, 500% revenue growth before the Series A, a public OpenAPI surface and regulator-collaborative Lloyd's Lab/BMA participation. The contract score remains poor because its 30% founder category strongly rewards bank, payment-network and regulator pedigrees that this insurance-native team does not have, while several security and regulatory-compliance signals are structurally inapplicable. Monitor rather than pass operationally: a meeting becomes warranted after validating current ARR and retention, the ISO certificate, customer concentration and the post-2024 financing plan.
  - Licences: ISO 27001 certification
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab Cohort 6
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current ISO/IEC 27001 certificate PDF, certificate number, named accredited body, scope, issue date and expiry.; Confirm 2025-2026 ARR, gross margin, net revenue retention, sales cycle, deployment revenue and customer concentration.

**Nuggets** — Nuggets has meaningful FCA-sandbox, OpenID-standards and current product-development evidence. But stage and capital remain unresolved, the team lacks the thesis-required Tier-1 financial-institution founder, and no named institutional production customer is public. Four anti-signals keep the fit in the poor band.
  - Licences: ISO 27001 certified
  - Found via: Tech Nation Fintech 4.0 cohort
  - Check before the call: Obtain a complete cap table, cumulative funding history, current stage, ARR and runway.; Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate, issuing body, scope, issue date and expiry date.

**Straal** — Straal is live, European, B2B and technically credible as a global merchant-payment layer, but the current stage is unverified. It lacks the required founder/FI signal, regulated-rail ownership, public security assurance and institutional-finance customer proof.
  - Found via: Poland fintech map / EU-Startups Poland coverage
  - Check before the call: Identify and verify the legal founders and their full employment histories.; Confirm current financing stage, cap table and any undisclosed rounds since November 2022.

**Apiday** — Apiday has excellent institutional adoption signals, credible quantified case studies, rapid international commercialisation and a founder with a named exit. It passes the broad compliance-and-data infrastructure gate, but its core product is adjacent sustainable-finance SaaS rather than institutional digital-finance rails. The lack of public security certification, API evidence and regulatory engagement makes it a poor Stratum3 fit despite a strong standalone business.
  - Found via: ESGFinTech100 2024
  - Check before the call: Obtain ARR, net revenue retention, sales concentration and the split between recurring platform revenue and advisory services.; Verify ISO 27001 or SOC 2 roadmap, penetration testing, DPIA, data residency, subprocessor controls and incident history.

**Yields.io** — Yields has the strongest verified institutional traction in this set: global HSBC production use, quantified ROI, multiple named banks and international delivery. The score is held back by a thin high-threshold founder/regulatory profile, limited public technical assurance and developer transparency, and a more-than-three-year gap since the last equity round. The company is a software vendor, not a regulated financial-service provider, so absence from EBA/FCA/NBB licence registers is not a veto.
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: FinTech Belgium RegTech mapping
  - Check before the call: Obtain and independently validate the current ISO 27001 certificate number, accredited certification body, scope and expiry.; Confirm ARR, net revenue retention, gross margin, sales-cycle length and customer concentration, especially HSBC and BNP Paribas exposure.

**Kani Payments** — Kani has the strongest institutional production evidence in this cohort so far: a five-year EMI deployment, named references, five-continent use, material processed volume, accelerator validation and repeat Maven backing. The score remains poor because the library heavily rewards Tier-1 founder pedigree, regulatory engagement and public technical transparency, where Kani resolves mostly negative. Monitor pending exact capital disclosure, a Tier-1 bank reference and current security artefacts.
  - Found via: Targeted sector search (Innovate Finance member candidate)
  - Check before the call: Obtain the amounts and valuations for both the 2025 Series A and March 2026 follow-on; G4 and cheque fit remain unresolved.; Request the current ISO 27001 certificate and SOC 2 Type II report, including certificate number, scope, audit period and expiry.

**Triple** — Triple shows credible scale claims, public developer materials, a Nets executive reference and a well-validated institutional cap table. Its investability score is constrained by weak founder-domain verification, no named production-bank case study and incomplete public security substantiation. Monitor pending reference calls and a SOC 2 evidence review.
  - Found via: neosfer Triple investment announcement
  - Check before the call: Obtain two named bank references and verify production volumes and renewal status.; Inspect the SOC 2 Type II report, auditor, scope and exceptions.

**kennek** — kennek has excellent founder-market fit, numerous named specialist-lender references, a verified production deployment and a credible institutional cap table. Its contract-weighted score is nevertheless dragged down by concentrated UK traction, no Tier-1 bank customer, no public security certification/API assurance and stale financing. FCA authorisation is not applicable because kennek is the software supplier and assigns regulated lending/KYC duties to its customers; no licence veto applies.
  - Found via: HV Capital portfolio
  - Check before the call: Verify ARR, net revenue retention, implementation gross margin and revenue concentration across the named lender base.; Obtain the current ISO 27001/SOC 2 roadmap, penetration-test summary and incident history.

**Renew Risk** — Renew Risk has the best verified institutional adoption and investor validation in this cohort: multi-year contracts, McGill licensing, GCube/Nasdaq production use and Molten-led follow-on capital. The team combines bank-risk, catastrophe-modelling and academic engineering depth, but two founding executives left operating roles in January 2026. Its fit remains poor under Stratum3's digital-finance signal library because it is pure insurance data infrastructure and publishes little enterprise-security, API or compliance evidence.
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab Cohort 11
  - Check before the call: Reconcile the GBP 6.7m additive round total with 2026 company pages that state GBP 4.7m and a GBP 16m valuation.; Understand why Ashima Gupta and Gaurav Chawla left executive roles, their continuing ownership and board involvement, and CEO succession stability.

**Kreditz** — Kreditz has a genuinely active Swedish AISP registration, more than 80 reported customers, EUR 10m Series A backing and credible production outcomes at Collector and Skanska Rental. The weighted fit is 0.2905 before four anti-signal deductions; strict founder-pedigree and technical-assurance thresholds are the main score drag, while financing recency and the CEO transition merit monitoring.
  - Licences: Swedish FSA registered AISP, id 59300
  - Found via: Finansinspektionen company register (AISP registrations)
  - Check before the call: Retrieve the EBA PSD2 central-register record and all current passport notifications for institute 59300.; Verify the new Swedish credit-bureau licence in the competent-authority register and obtain its reference and scope.

**Trever GmbH** — Trever has unusually credible production-bank evidence and a tightly on-thesis digital-asset operating system. The score is held down by founders without verified FI alumni credentials and thin public developer/security proof, while lifetime capital remains incomplete. Monitor for a fuller financing history, certificate pack and bank references with quantified outcomes.
  - Found via: Austrian FMA VASP register mirror
  - Check before the call: Obtain a complete lifetime funding and grant schedule.; Review the ISO 27001 certificate, SOC report, penetration-test summary and DORA evidence pack.

**Crediwire** — Crediwire has genuine production evidence at Nordea, a second bank-originated partnership and solid SME-data reach. Its thesis fit is weakened by no verified two-year Tier-1 founder tenure, stale financing, very limited public technical assurance and no digital-asset dimension. Monitor rather than meet until stage, founder pedigree, security assurance and current growth are confirmed.
  - Found via: Copenhagen Fintech current members
  - Check before the call: Confirm the formal stage, complete financing history and current cap table, including whether Nordea reinvested in 2022.; Verify Morten Heick's Saxo Bank title, function and employment dates.

**TrueScreen** — TrueScreen has credible patented provenance technology, a live status page and a sensible compliance use case for financial institutions. It lacks the required founder pedigree and named FI production evidence, while explicit B2C monetisation weakens thesis purity. ISO claims remain unverified, but eIDAS-qualified timestamps are supplied by an external accredited QTSP rather than by TrueScreen itself.
  - Licences: ISO 27001; ISO 9001
  - Found via: Fin+Tech Accelerator
  - Check before the call: Obtain ISO 27001 and ISO 9001 certificates, numbers, scopes, expiry dates and certification-body accreditation.; Identify the external QTSP used for qualified timestamps and confirm its active EU Trusted List service status.

**BlockInvest** — BlockInvest is unusually on-thesis at the product level: institutional tokenisation rails, regulatory-hub engagement, two bank shareholders and a verified live UniCredit issuance. Its signal score is held down by a founder pedigree gap and a lack of public API, audit and certification evidence, not by licence failure; Bank of Italy explicitly treats it as a non-vigilated technology provider. Monitor pending security diligence and proof of repeatable multi-country production revenue.
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: Banca d'Italia Milano Hub selected projects
  - Check before the call: Obtain smart-contract audits, penetration tests, ISO/SOC evidence and architecture documentation.; Confirm the complete cap table and aggregate all undisclosed 2021 and 2024 financing.

**Marble** — Marble has unusually strong open-source proof and credible named production cases for its age, including quantified outcomes at Treezor and Gemba. Its score is constrained by limited regulatory assurance, no verified security certificate, a thin disclosed financing history and little independent institutional press or partner validation.
  - Found via: France FinTech RegTech members
  - Check before the call: Obtain the SOC 2 Type II report or bridge letter and verify auditor, period and scope.; Confirm current ARR, number of paying production customers and customer-country distribution.

**ZealiD** — ZealiD has a genuinely current PTS/QTSP registration, strong API documentation and an unusually good quantified ABN AMRO production case. However, it misses the founder-pedigree thesis, has stale financing, only six group employees, declining 2025 revenue and a persistent B2C surface. Pass for now despite the regulatory asset; revisit only if current growth, enterprise retention and a fresh institutional round materially change the picture.
  - Licences: EU Qualified Trust Service Provider on the EU Trusted List; Supervised by Swedish Post and Telecoms Authority (PTS); eIDAS conformity assessed by SRC Security GmbH
  - Found via: J12 Ventures portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the latest conformity-assessment certificates and reports, reconciling PTS's current listing with TÜV certificate 97225.24 ending 17 May 2026.; Reconcile the supplied $9.6m raised with Caplight's $5.63m estimate and identify the actual most recent round, lead and current cap table.

**Intelligent AI** — Intelligent AI is valid insurance underwriting infrastructure with credible Lloyd's validation, recurring SaaS delivery, multiple rounds and founder ownership. It scores poorly because the founders lack Tier-1 bank or digital-asset pedigree, public security/developer evidence is thin, and production insurer references remain unnamed. The already-profitable Series A process also suggests timing and price may be moving beyond Stratum3's highest-conviction entry point.
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab Cohort 10
  - Check before the call: Reconcile the £3.5m verified financing history with aggregator estimates of $4.4m-$7.4m and inspect the live Series A terms.; Obtain named production insurer references, ARR, gross retention and the proportion of revenue from API versus services.

**Diesta** — Diesta has unusually strong Seed-stage commercial proof: 25 clients, roughly 3,000 connected organisations, mid-single-digit-million revenue and live cross-border payment operations. Julian Schoemig supplies genuine Tier-1 financial-institution pedigree via Munich Re, while open-banking activity is covered through a verified Plaid agent relationship rather than Diesta's own authorisation. The lack of digital-asset/tokenisation exposure and unverifiable certification detail make this a high-quality adjacency, not yet a core Stratum3 meet.
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab Cohort 10
  - Check before the call: Obtain the January 2026 round amount, lead investor and complete post-round cap table.; Request the FCA register extract confirming current Diesta agent permissions under Plaid.

**Monokee** — Monokee has unusually credible identity technology, a granted patent, analyst recognition, and a durable public-sector deployment. It is nevertheless a horizontal IAM vendor with no Tier-1 FI founder, no named bank deployment, and no public ISO/SOC assurance. Its digital-finance thesis connection is therefore too indirect for a meeting today.
  - Found via: OpenID Foundation members
  - Check before the call: Obtain the full founder list, founder employment histories, cap table, lifetime funding, and current ARR.; Identify and reference-check the unnamed banking and insurance customers, including production scope and renewal metrics.

**Wonderful** — Wonderful has a verified FCA permission, genuinely useful public APIs, robust enterprise legal documentation and named merchant ROI. It falls well short of the thesis on founder pedigree, geographic scalability, team scale and institutional-customer evidence.
  - Licences: FCA authorised payment institution FRN 964289
  - Found via: Open Banking regulated providers directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm beneficial owners, external capital and stage directly with management.; Obtain enterprise Asima customer references and recurring-revenue metrics.

**Acuminor** — Acuminor is a live, capital-efficient institutional RegTech with a Nordea alumnus founder, FATF engagement and at least one named production bank deployment. Its claimed 500–750-organisation footprint is compelling but weakly substantiated by named third-party references, while public security and developer evidence is thin. Monitor pending customer calls and security diligence.
  - Found via: Swedish fintech infrastructure landscape
  - Check before the call: Obtain named references behind the anonymous Tier-1 and UK bank case studies, including contract status and ARR contribution.; Verify cumulative equity plus grant funding directly from Acuminor's cap table and Swedish filings.

**Payout** — Current regulatory and product evidence, a government-funded FraudNet project and real production references show an operating regional payments platform. Founder pedigree, recent private funding, security attestations and AML leadership are materially under-evidenced.
  - Licences: National Bank of Slovakia regulated payment institution, decision 100-000-221-585
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G3_seed_or_series_a — confirm before the call
  - ⚠️ Review flags: The PI licence is current in EBA-derived register data, but no publicly verifiable named MLRO or substantive AML framework was found; obtain supervisory and AML evidence before investment.
  - Found via: National Bank of Slovakia payment-services register
  - Check before the call: Archive Payout's current NBS API record and reconcile the decision-number typo on its 2025 licence article.; Resolve the 2016 Dealroom launch date versus the company's stated 2019 founding date.

**Vottun** — The developer platform and sandbox work are real, but institutional traction is unproven, funding is stale and official records contradict any implication that sandbox participation itself authorised regulated activity.

  - ⚠️ Review flags: Banco de España states sandbox participation is not authorisation and that operation outside the sandbox would require PSP authorisation; no current Vottun entry was found in the CNMV CASP list.
  - Found via: Banco de España financial sandbox
  - Check before the call: Clarify which services are currently provided by Vottun versus licensed third parties.; Obtain CNMV/Banco de España counsel memo on MiCAR and payment-services perimeter.

**Quantum Signals** — The product is credible institutional market-data middleware with strong research pedigree, public APIs and early bank engagement. However, neither founder comes from a Tier-1 FI or digital-asset infrastructure company, evidence of production deployment is absent, and the platform currently has no tokenisation, regulated-rail or digital-asset angle. Cumulative funding is also undisclosed, leaving cheque fit unresolved.
  - Found via: Tenity European Accelerator 2025
  - Check before the call: Obtain the full cap table and cumulative amount across the Quantonation and Tenity rounds.; Confirm whether Société Générale or the unnamed US bank is paying and in production rather than research collaboration.

**Venly** — The current stablecoin, wallet and multirail API stack is directly on thesis and has strong developer evidence. However, founder fit and named financial-institution production traction are weak, funding is stale, the company is in the stretch capital tier, and the post-transition MiCA licence perimeter is not yet independently resolved.

Venly says MiCA authorization is in progress and regulated execution uses licensed partners; verify the exact partner-of-record and contractual perimeter before proceeding.
  - Found via: Mastercard Start Path Blockchain and Digital Assets 2024
  - Check before the call: Obtain the MiCA CASP application reference, competent authority, scope and expected decision date.; Identify every licensed partner executing conversion, custody, IBAN and payment services, plus contractual liability allocation.

**ibanXS** — The licence, broad EEA passport, iDEAL founder pedigree and live Rabobank integrations are high-quality regulated-rails signals. Its public technology assurance and customer metrics are thin, and no funding stage or cap table is visible. Monitor pending stage, ownership and recurring-revenue verification.
  - Licences: Dutch Central Bank payment institution R163657; AISP and PISP
  - Found via: Dutch Central Bank public register
  - Check before the call: Confirm current ownership, total outside capital and whether the company is genuinely Seed/Series A investable.; Obtain DNB supervisory correspondence, safeguarding model, AML programme and current audit reports.

**Asteria** — Asteria is a real, registered open-banking data provider with credible Swedbank production history and strong financial-institution investors. However, the team has contracted, the most visible bank deployment ended in 2025, and qualifying founder and fresh-funding signals are weak. The product also remains conventional SME cash-flow SaaS rather than tokenisation or digital-asset rails.

  - Check before the call: Verify exact founder employment dates at SEB and Handelsbanken against the two-year Tier-1 threshold.; Obtain the full cap table and reconcile Swedbank, Svea, Nestholma, and grant funding without overlap.

**TAINA Technology** — TAINA has unusually strong bank-CVC validation, a deeply relevant founder and a useful TradFi-to-digital-asset extension. It nevertheless fails the current sourcing bar on freshness and transparency: no disclosed round since 2022, a crowded/multi-round cap table, anonymised customers and no independently verifiable information-security certificate. The public evidence suggests a credible product, but not a clean new Series A entry.
  - Found via: Anthemis portfolio
  - Check before the call: Reconcile the €18.3m Vestbee aggregate with materially lower database estimates and obtain the fully diluted cap table.; Confirm ARR, 2024–2026 growth, gross retention, named production customers and whether Deutsche Bank/HSBC/SIX are customers, investors or both.

**Ariadne** — Ariadne is eligible and unusually differentiated: a prior successful fintech founder, a product used by 230-plus institutions before exit, direct ISO/TC 68 participation, open ACTUS foundations, an API/Postman surface and an explicit TradFi–DLT bridge. The decisive weakness is commercial opacity. Without a named bank confirming production use, recurring revenue and ROI, the architecture remains more standards-led than venture-validated. Monitor and seek a technical-founder meeting only alongside customer diligence.

**Blockchain Italia** — Blockchain Italia has genuine blockchain delivery history, regulatory-sandbox participation and a named BNL production use case, but its model remains a mixed software-house/consultancy with a consumer product rather than focused institutional infrastructure. Stage and capital are unresolved, founder FI pedigree is absent, and the publicly linked ISO 27001 certificate is expired.
  - Found via: INATBA member directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain official shareholder filings, total capital raised, revenue mix and current financing stage.; Demand a current ISO 27001 certificate or written explanation for the expired certificate still promoted.

**Nivo** — Nivo has strong Barclays founder-market fit, verified DVS orchestration status and quantified production deployments. Digital-asset fit, geographic breadth, current security assurance and financing recency are weak; monitor the AI-agent pivot rather than prioritising a meeting.
  - Licences: UK DVS Trust Framework registered orchestration service
  - Found via: UK statutory digital verification services register
  - Check before the call: Obtain 2025–2026 ARR, retention, gross margin and product-line revenue.; Verify founder ownership and current cap table.

**Salv** — Salv has unusually strong operating evidence: Wise-built founders, named banks, quantified fraud prevention and a cross-border network in production. The strict library penalises the absence of Tier-1-bank founder tenure, public developer/security artefacts and a complex, stale early-stage financing history. Confirm growth and financing plans before deciding whether the network moat warrants an exception.
  - Found via: EU Digital Finance Platform fintech map
  - Check before the call: Verify 2024–2026 ARR, net retention, current headcount and any unannounced financing.; Obtain ISO 27001 certificate details, SOC 2 report and API documentation.

**Inespay** — A live Bank of Spain licence, substantial processed volume, named enterprise customers and EPC standards participation establish a real regulated-rails business. Founder provenance, public technical assurance and target-market FI deployments remain weak against Stratum3's strict signals. Monitor while resolving the ownership discrepancy and whether 2026 growth supports a fresh institutional round.
  - Licences: Bank of Spain Payment Institution no. 6902; PISP
  - Found via: EBA PSD2 central register
  - Check before the call: Resolve Dealroom's unsupported claim that Inetum acquired Inespay in 2022; current regulator, investor and 2026 media evidence indicate independence.; Retrieve the actual ISO 27001 certificate and verify certifier, scope, certificate number and expiry.

**Smart Fintech** — The NBR/EBA licence and live Romanian open-banking integrations are genuine, and partner growth is encouraging. However, the company remains a five-person, bootstrapped, Romania-only operator with no Tier-1-FI founder evidence, no passporting and no public institutional security certificate. It is too locally concentrated and insufficiently finance-institution-led for Stratum3's target profile today.
  - Licences: National Bank of Romania payment institution IP-RO-0011/02.04.2021; AISP and PISP
  - ⚠️ Review flags: The PI licence is active, but no substantive public AML framework or named qualified MLRO was located; human compliance review is required.
  - Found via: EBA PSD2 central register
  - Check before the call: Confirm the current EBA register export fields for authorised services, status and any EEA passport notifications.; Obtain the shareholder register and clarify the ownership/economic relationship with IT Smart Systems.

**Northstake** — Northstake is a live, thesis-aligned institutional staking platform with a regulator-verified MiCA licence and French passport, strong public developer tooling, and credible production integrations. The signal score is held down by a founder profile outside the fund's preferred bank or digital-infrastructure pedigree, limited named customer proof, absent public security certification and stale disclosed funding. Monitor pending closure evidence for the 2024 AML order, a reconciled financing history and stronger production references.
  - Licences: Danish MiCA CASP, FTID 10905
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Adverse regulatory action: obtain formal evidence that the Danish FSA's March 2024 AML order was remediated and closed; the later 2026 MiCA authorisation is positive but not closure proof.
  - Found via: Danish FSA company register
  - Check before the call: Obtain the Danish FSA register extract for FTID 10905 and formal evidence that the March 2024 AML order was remediated and closed.; Reconcile disclosed €2.8m funding against commercial-database totals reportedly reaching roughly $6.4m and confirm the complete round history and cap table.

**Tradevest Digital Assets GmbH** — Tradevest has strong mandate relevance, a confirmed MiCAR CASP entry and a particularly credible live role as 21X's first market maker. The score remains poor because public proof is thin across security controls, regulatory leadership, customer economics and financing, while a consumer DeFi line introduces positioning and conduct-risk questions. Monitor pending full licence-scope, security and cap-table diligence.
  - Licences: German MiCAR CASP authorisation, granted 19 Dec 2025; BaFin crypto custody and crypto registry permissions, BaFin ID 10162132
  - ⚠️ Review flags: MiCAR authorisation is confirmed in the official ESMA CASP file, but the separate BaFin crypto-custody and crypto-register permissions quoted in the imprint were not directly inspectable in the BaFin database.; Two unrelated lookalike domains use the Tradevest name and make implausible retail trading claims; confirm brand-control and customer-confusion measures during diligence.
  - Found via: ESMA Interim MiCA Register - Germany
  - Check before the call: Retrieve BaFin ID 10162132 directly and verify current custody, crypto-register and MiCAR permissions, restrictions and group-entity allocation.; Confirm cumulative capital raised, August 2024 round terms, investors, ownership and whether the stage is Seed or Series A.

**Decentriq** — Decentriq is the strongest technically and institutionally validated company in the set: official Swiss status, an Ethereum Foundation founder, standards-body membership, named global customers, quantified ROI, current G2 traction and a strong investor syndicate. The strict score is reduced by the library's bank-founder bias, no public SOC 2 report, weak named bank-production evidence, stale capital and no digital-asset bridge. At approximately €22.8m total disclosed capital it is a stretch cheque, not a core cheque. It does not claim a FINMA-regulated financial service, so no missing-licence veto applies.
  - Found via: Swiss Cyber-Defence Campus startup challenge
  - Check before the call: Obtain the SOC 2 Type II report, auditor and period, plus penetration-test, enclave-attestation, key-management and incident-response evidence.; Reference-check Swiss Re, Mobiliar and a named bank to separate production deployments from proofs of concept and quantify financial-services ARR.

**inari** — Inari is alive, independent, within cheque size and strongly aligned with institutional insurance infrastructure. The four-founder team has a real named exit, Lloyd's independently confirms operating status and accelerator work, CDTI confirms repeat public co-investment, and the company reports significant scale. It still scores poor under the strict library because the public technical/security surface is weak, the majority of customers remain anonymous, DACH/Nordic evidence is absent and the claimed LEI has lapsed. Renew the LEI and obtain security and customer diligence before reconsidering.
  - Licences: LEI 959800RTG4U7DGQ6TX06
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Entity-identifier veto: Bloomberg's GLEIF-sourced record shows the claimed LEI as LAPSED. This is not a financial-services licence and does not make the active company unauthorised, but renewal and entity-data reconciliation are required before diligence can proceed.; Do not represent LEI 959800RTG4U7DGQ6TX06 as an insurance, software or financial-services licence. Inari's documented pure-software activity does not itself appear to require an FCA, DGSFP, EBA, VASP or CASP licence.
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab Impact Report
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current cap table, reconcile the 2023 Seed with CDTI's 2024 co-investment and 2025 aid, and confirm total dilutive versus non-dilutive capital.; Renew LEI 959800RTG4U7DGQ6TX06 and reconcile the LEI addresses/registration IDs with the current Barcelona address.

**Nopan** — Nopan has an unusually credible early foundation: active EU PI passporting, strong payments pedigree, rich public API material, scheme connectivity and high-quality institutional investors. The score is held back by very early and unnamed customer proof, absent public security/compliance artefacts, and a small single-location team. Monitor closely and meet once a named FI/enterprise production reference and operational-control evidence are available.
  - Licences: DNB-licensed payment service provider (Netherlands)
  - Found via: Seedcamp portfolio
  - Check before the call: Capture the exact DNB/EBA central-register entry, home-state authorisation date and complete permission scope.; Identify the MLRO/CCO, AML framework, safeguarding bank, DORA controls, penetration-test provider and security-certification roadmap.

**Boodil** — Boodil has genuine merchant traction, public APIs and a founder with NatWest Markets experience, but it is merchant acceptance rather than institutional digital-asset infrastructure. The missing direct FCA entry, unnamed regulated principal and absent public AML ownership require resolution before any engagement. Funding and stage visibility are also stale.
  - Licences: UK open-banking payment services provided under FCA-regulated permissions
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Regulatory perimeter unresolved: Boodil initiates open-banking payments but is not found on the FCA register and does not publicly name the authorised PISP/principal or FRN.; No substantive public AML framework or named MLRO was found.
  - Found via: Open Banking regulated providers directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the regulated PISP/acquirer agreements, FCA firm reference numbers and precise allocation of regulatory responsibilities.; Reconcile the 2024 share allotments with the stated £525k disclosed funding total.

**Tokenovate** — Tokenovate is an unusually precise thesis match with a founder exit, deep market-structure legal talent, and verified ISDA/FINOS and Bank of England engagement. The score remains poor because evidence of production FI customers, public technical artefacts and a transparent financing lead is missing; those are diligence priorities, not licence vetoes.
  - Found via: FINOS Common Domain Model community
  - Check before the call: Obtain customer references and distinguish paid production deployments from demonstrations, pilots and ecosystem programmes.; Reconcile CB Insights' $18m total with Companies House allotments and identify the January 2025 lead investor.

**Adnitio (Track A Payment)** — Adnitio is live UK institutional payments middleware and its founder has a relevant prior exit and bank-deployed product history. However, public evidence is exceptionally thin on named customers, security assurance, integrations, team depth and capital history. The two unresolved eligibility gates and multiple diligence gaps outweigh the thesis relevance.
  - Found via: EBAday 2025 exhibitor list
  - Check before the call: Confirm financing stage, all historical capital raised and current cap table directly with management.; Obtain named production-bank references, deployment metrics and renewal/ARR evidence.

**Kapnative** — Kapnative has unusually strong private-markets operating pedigree, a named family-office reference, €11bn of represented client AUM and credible Tenity/First Rate backing. It nevertheless scores poor because there is no public technical assurance surface, no Tier-1-bank deployment, limited multi-country named traction and weak compliance-lead disclosure. More importantly, its regulated tied-agent claim remains unverified in a primary register, which is a veto.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: LICENCE VETO PENDING: Kapnative claims tied-intermediary status under AHP Capital Management, but publishes no BaFin tied-agent registration number and no primary-register entry for Kapnative was independently verified. AHP's own investment-firm authorisation is verifiable; Kapnative's tied-agent notification is not. Do not proceed until BaFin-register proof is supplied.
  - Found via: Tenity (formerly F10)
  - Check before the call: Obtain a dated BaFin tied-agent register extract and registration number linking Kapnative GmbH to AHP Capital Management before any meeting.; Confirm whether the UK establishment serves UK clients and, if so, the applicable FCA permission or exemption.

**The Pension Lab** — Pension Lab has real institutional deployment evidence, including Aegon and Smart Pension, and credible workflow efficiency gains. It nevertheless scores poorly for this mandate because it is UK-only pension administration infrastructure with no digital-asset bridge, thin public security evidence and an FCA record that could not be independently inspected in-session.
  - Licences: FCA firm reference 812251
  - ⚠️ Review flags: FRN 812251 is repeatedly claimed and corroborated by operating counterparties, but the actual current FCA register record and permission set were not retrievable; verify manually before investment reliance.
  - Found via: Money Marketing
  - Check before the call: Inspect FCA FRN 812251 directly and record current status, permissions, trading names and any restrictions.; Reconcile the supplied $1.23m funding figure with the $551k total shown by public company data and identify all investors.

**Rivero** — Rivero has the best institutional proof in this set—live bank deployments, named references, a Visa channel and quantified customer ROI—but evidence is weaker on open technical diligence, independently verifiable assurance and fresh financing.
  - Found via: IFZ FinTech Study 2026 company directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate, PCI DSS attestation, penetration-test summary and subprocessor list.; Confirm 2025/2026 ARR, net retention, gross margin, ACV and customer concentration.

**Ledger Rocket** — The founding team, Revolut ledger background and product architecture are directionally excellent for the thesis. Public evidence is still mostly narrative: no named production customer, inspectable API, certificate, round amount or clear regulatory status. Monitor closely and meet only after the licence-copy inconsistency and security certifications are resolved.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Licence/claims review required: the website says it operates under regulator authorisation and discusses investment services, but no matching FCA firm record was verified.
  - Found via: EBAday Fintech Zone 2026
  - Check before the call: Obtain FCA firm reference or written confirmation that the investment-service language is erroneous template copy and will be removed.; Inspect the ISO 27001 certificate and SOC 2 Type II report, including auditor, scope and validity period.

**Raidiam** — Raidiam passes the non-negotiable gates and is demonstrably independent, live and deeply relevant infrastructure. Its strongest evidence is unusually concrete: founder-led OpenID standards work, FAPI certification, public APIs and Postman assets, active releases, an FCA procurement award and production-scale national ecosystems across multiple continents. The mechanical score is constrained by the library's founder pedigree tests, absent disclosed venture financing, lack of named Tier-1 commercial-bank references and incomplete public certificate metadata. The ICO record is a data-controller fee registration, not a licence; Raidiam does not itself perform a regulated financial activity, so no missing-licence veto applies.
  - Licences: UK ICO registration ZA472236
  - Found via: FCA Smart Data ecosystem
  - Check before the call: Obtain management confirmation of all equity and debt raised, current stage, ARR, growth, gross margin, customer concentration and cash runway.; Export the current ICO entry for ZA472236 and obtain the underlying ISO 27001 certificate, SOC 2 Type II report and 2026 penetration-test report with auditor names, scope and dates.

**FinFlag** — FinFlag has genuine production workflow evidence, recurring SaaS contracts, and excellent senior payments-network experience. Strict scoring is held down by anonymised customers, no disclosed funding stage, limited public technical assurance, and no digital-asset or tokenisation angle. Monitor for a named bank reference and a verifiable security baseline.
  - Found via: EBAday Fintech Zone 2026
  - Check before the call: Confirm whether Geoffroy Dupuis is the legal founder and obtain the full founding-team employment history.; Confirm total capital raised, ownership, and whether FinFlag is bootstrapped or institutionally financed.

**SimplyTokenized GmbH** — Excellent tokenisation-rail thesis alignment and a founder with a real fintech exit are offset by a two-person footprint, no named bank deployment, and no public compliance leader or security certification. Stage and total capital remain undisclosed, and residual investor-facing activity warrants clarification before outreach.
  - Found via: Digital Assets Association Austria (DAAA) member list
  - Check before the call: Obtain cap table, total capital raised, and current financing stage.; Confirm whether SimplyTokenized performs any regulated placement, execution, custody, or transfer activity versus acting solely as software vendor.

**Agio Ratings** — Agio has credible institutional crypto-risk infrastructure, named production users, repeat investors and excellent thesis adjacency. Founder fit and public technical assurance are weak under the strict library. Most importantly, the apparent FCA CRA-registration gap is a legal veto until counsel establishes that the ratings are outside UK CRAR or registration is in process.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Potential unlicensed regulated activity: Agio publicly issues/subscribes credit-style ratings for institutional regulatory reporting and capital calculations, but is absent from the FCA's current registered/certified CRA list. UK regulatory counsel must confirm exemption or remediation.
  - Found via: AlbionVC portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain UK regulatory counsel analysis of whether Agio's public and subscription ratings fall within UK CRAR, plus any FCA correspondence or application reference.; Resolve the full identity and prior employment of co-founder Ben M. and verify each founder's complete financial-institution and crypto-infrastructure history.

**Credi2** — Credi2 has the best institutional validation in this cohort: Volkswagen Bank and RBI products are demonstrably live, Visa and Marqeta are product partners, Speedinvest and Volkswagen are shareholders, and TÜV AUSTRIA confirms current ISO security certification. The score is nevertheless pulled down by a heavily diluted founding team, shrinking public headcount, mature 2015 vintage, limited public engineering transparency and residual B2C group activity. The fit score includes four confirmed anti-signal deductions of 0.03 each.
  - Found via: Speedinvest Credi2 portfolio page
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current ISO 27001/27701 certificates, numbers, scopes and expiry dates directly from management.; Confirm whether the reported $7m total includes all Volkswagen primary investments versus secondary share purchases.

**KEY ESG** — KEY ESG has credible international customers, production private-capital deployments, strong user/review evidence, public APIs, live EDCI/SFDR workflows and an obvious regulatory tailwind. Its fit remains poor under this library because the founders lack regulated-finance pedigree, funding validation is stale and public security assurance is incomplete. Monitor as a commercially credible compliance-data asset, pending security and revenue diligence.
  - Found via: ESGFinTech100 2024
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current SOC 2 Type 2 report, auditor, audit period, exceptions and remediation status.; Confirm ARR, growth, NRR, gross margin, customer concentration, churn and how 10,000 monthly users translate into paid seats and contracts.

**Verifiables** — Verifiables has credible, independently testable EUDIW technology: France Identité hosts its live verifier and documents OpenID4VP, ISO 18013-5, cryptographic session controls and cross-wallet flows. It also has an active French company, a real €1m institutional round, visible founders and useful policy/ecosystem momentum. The underwriting weakness is commercial rather than technical: no named current customers, no FI production deployment, no current security certification and a material pivot from the funded Web3-agency model keep the fit score poor. Monitor for conversion of the France Identité validation into recurring regulated-enterprise revenue.
  - Found via: Web search on EU age verification and agent identity vendors
  - Check before the call: Obtain current ARR, customer count, contract duration, pricing, gross margin and conversion from interoperability demos to paid production.; Reconcile the 2023 Renaissance Web3-agency round and investor rights with the current Verifiables pivot, including remaining agency revenue and IP allocation.

**Coadjute** — Credible infrastructure selection, strategic bank investors and a live property-software integration are outweighed by stretch-stage capital, no verified bank production customer, a single-country footprint and weak public security/API evidence.
  - Licences: Selected technology partner in UK Finance Regulated Liability Network experimentation
  - Found via: Innovate Finance member news: UK RLN experimentation report
  - Check before the call: Confirm the exact post-2024 fully diluted cap table and whether total capital includes debt.; Obtain production references from Lloyds, NatWest, Nationwide or another regulated financial institution.

**Kriptown** — Kriptown/LISE has real strategic signal: an ESMA-verified DLT TSS permission, EUR 2.07m first production IPO, central-bank experimentation, blue-chip bank shareholders and a recommended SEK 199m acquisition offer. The weighted fit is 0.2612 before four anti-signal deductions. The legacy PSAN licence lapse is an explicit veto under the contract, compounded by stale regulatory copy and missing public technical assurance, so the current recommendation is pass pending licence remediation.
  - Licences: Operating under the EU DLT Pilot Regime
  - ⚠️ Review flags: LICENCE LAPSE: AMF registration E2022-030 became automatically void on 2 July 2026 at the end of the MiCA transition. No replacement MiCA CASP authorisation was found, yet Kriptown's site still claims PSAN status and advertises custody, fiat purchase/sale and digital-asset trading. LISE SA's separate DLT TSS authorisation remains active on ESMA's register.
  - Found via: Banque de France wholesale CBDC experiment (LiquidShare consortium) + EU DLT Pilot Regime
  - Check before the call: Obtain Kriptown's MiCA transition plan and either a current CASP authorisation/application reference or proof that all legacy crypto services ceased on 2 July 2026.; Pull the complete ACPR permission decision and exemption schedule for LISE SA and confirm AMF/ACPR supervisory responsibilities.

**ume** — ume has excellent founder-domain fit, genuine global reach and unusually strong reported penetration among large fund managers. Yet customers remain anonymous, current funding/stage are opaque, and technical/security diligence cannot be validated publicly. Pass at present, with potential reconsideration if named references and security artefacts are supplied.
  - Found via: LHoFT Luxembourg Fintech Map 2025
  - Check before the call: Confirm current stage, full capital raised, ownership and profitability.; Obtain named top-50 fund-manager references, ARR and retention.

**Axyon AI** — Axyon is live, institutional, globally oriented and comfortably inside the capital ceiling, with strong strategic investors and a production financial-institution deployment. The central thesis gap is founder pedigree: none of the three founders has the required Tier-1 FI or digital-asset-infrastructure background, while public security/API evidence is thin. The low fit is evidence-backed rather than a coverage artifact.
  - Found via: ING International Innovation Studio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the complete current cap table and clarify whether the 2025 financing is formally an extension of Series A.; Request ISO 27001/SOC 2 evidence, penetration-test reports and enterprise SLA/DPA materials.

**Gataca** — Gataca is an active, independent Spanish identity-infrastructure vendor with public APIs, current GitHub activity, verifiable EU standards engagement, a national privacy award and fresh July 2026 financing. It does not claim or appear to require a financial-services licence; the EU trusted-service and Spanish/UK public records reviewed show no missing required licence, lapsed licence or enforcement veto. The low contract fit reflects no Tier-1 finance founder pedigree, no named production FI customer, an unresolved capital total, a still-live consumer wallet and an ISO 27001 certificate that expired before this assessment.
  - Found via: Signature Ventures Gataca portfolio page
  - Check before the call: Obtain the amount, lead investor, new-investor names and closing documents for the July 2026 financing to resolve the €30m capital ceiling.; Request the renewed ISO/IEC 27001 certificate or remediation evidence; the publicly linked certificate expired on 12 April 2026.

**Verifo** — Verifo is alive, independently owned, passported and has a real EMI licence, public API material, transparent pricing and a named production banking relationship. It is nevertheless a poor mandate fit: founder pedigree and funding are opaque, consumer products persist, digital-asset relevance is weak, filings were late and the official AML enforcement history creates a diligence veto.
  - Licences: Bank of Lithuania EMI licence (Verified Payments UAB)
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Adverse regulatory action: Bank of Lithuania warning and €110,000 fine for AML/CTF internal-control, KYC/EDD and transaction-monitoring deficiencies; remediation and an external audit conclusion were ordered.; The official licence remains active, but management should provide the regulator-accepted remediation evidence and subsequent audit report before any investment process advances.
  - Found via: Bank of Lithuania — EMI/PI licensed institutions register
  - Check before the call: Obtain the 2024 independent audit conclusion submitted after the AML remediation order and any subsequent Bank of Lithuania correspondence.; Identify the founder, current beneficial owner, full board and executive compliance team and verify relevant financial-institution backgrounds.

**Token Terminal** — The institutional on-chain data product is mature, broad and demonstrably in production, but the public founder profile misses the fund's required financial-institution or digital-asset-infrastructure pedigree. Funding remains undisclosed, and enterprise security assurance is thin publicly.
  - Found via: Lifeline Ventures companies
  - Check before the call: Confirm all co-founders and their employment histories directly with the company.; Obtain the 2022 round size, current cap table and any subsequent financing.

**Nexpay** — The regulated rails, API documentation and disclosed €9bn-plus throughput demonstrate a live payments business. However, founder-thesis fit and named institutional customer proof are weak, while recent AML and operational-risk enforcement creates a serious diligence burden. Stage and total external capital remain unverified.
  - Licences: Bank of Lithuania EMI licence
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Adverse regulatory action: Bank of Lithuania fines of €125,000 and €30,000 in 2023; licence remains active.
  - Found via: Bank of Lithuania — EMI/PI licensed institutions register
  - Check before the call: Obtain the full cap table and reconcile all external capital and shareholder injections against the €30m ceiling.; Confirm whether the Bank of Lithuania has closed all 2023 remediation actions and obtain the latest AML audit.

**Crezco** — Crezco has unusually strong SME-scale production evidence through Xero, an active FCA AEMI permission, a public API and quantified customer ROI. It is less aligned with Stratum3's core thesis because no Tier-1-bank founder or regulated-FI customer is public, certification evidence is opaque and the cheque is already stretch. The employment judgment and historical AML disclosures require targeted diligence but are not an FCA enforcement veto.
  - Licences: FCA authorised payment institution FRN 1027543
  - ⚠️ Review flags: FCA authorisation is active, but no substantive public AML policy or currently named MLRO is available; the historical AML disclosures heighten review need.
  - Found via: Open Banking regulated providers directory
  - Check before the call: Reconcile total capital raised and the 2025–2026 share allotments against the cap table, bank statements and subscription agreements.; Download the current FCA permissions and requirements pages for FRN 1027543 and explain the transition from prior API FRN 925173 to AEMI status.

**Signius** — SIGNIUS has credible production deployments, strong APIs/HSM architecture and growing revenue, but it is not itself a QTSP—the qualified service is supplied by registered partners such as EuroCert. No founder meets the fund's Tier-1 FI or digital-asset-infrastructure background requirement, and the cap table is already fragmented ahead of an imminent public-market path. Stage and cumulative funding still require documentary confirmation.
  - Licences: eIDAS trust-service offering (backend via Cryptomathic)
  - Found via: Trusted List of eIDAS providers / Poland fintech map
  - Check before the call: Obtain cumulative cash raised and reconcile all A-F share issuances.; Confirm current ownership and operational relationship of the renamed German entity.

**nBanks** — nBanks is alive, B2B, globally deployed and led by a long-tenured bank alumnus, but it lacks the thesis-critical digital-asset/tokenisation layer and named FI adoption. The undisclosed cumulative funding total needs confirmation, although disclosed amounts remain far inside the core cheque tier.
  - Found via: Portugal Fintech Report 2020 — Capital Markets & Wealth Management
  - Check before the call: Confirm cumulative equity and grant funding directly with management.; Establish whether bank-data access is provided under nBanks' own AISP permission or through a licensed upstream provider.

**norbloc** — norbloc has rare production validation: named banks, FI-originated confirmation, quantified UAE outcomes and a current nationwide CBUAE eKYC mandate. The score remains poor under the contract because strict founder pedigree thresholds, weak public developer/security proof and an old verified equity round resolve negatively. Licensing is not applicable to its software-supplier model, and the official EBA/FCA checks produced no licence veto.

**Nikulipe** — An active EMI licence, 29-country passporting, strong PPRO founder pedigree, public API standards and live Aircash deployment establish a credible payments-infrastructure business. Public funding data, ownership, current revenue quality and target-market customer breadth remain too opaque for a meeting recommendation. Monitor pending cap-table and financial diligence, especially the divergence between regulator-reported 2024 turnover and registry-reported 2025 revenue.

**LISE SA (Lightning Stock Exchange)** — LISE is the clearest thesis match in the set: its DLT TSS permission is confirmed by ESMA, it has executed a real tokenised IPO, and regulated institutions participated in production. The score is constrained by limited public technical/security evidence, a dual retail/institutional model and single-issuer concentration, but the regulatory moat and strategic investor set justify a meeting.

**Citalid** — Relevant cyber-insurance and financial-risk infrastructure with credible institutional deployment, patented IP and strong investors, but the signal base is weak on founder financial-services pedigree, public assurance controls and recent financing; the lapsed ORIAS registration requires human review.

Confirm that Citalid no longer performs insurance intermediation before investment: https://www.orias.fr/home/resultAdvancedSearch?currentInterPage=1136&currentNotifPage=1&step=INTERMEDIAIRE&targetPage=1098

  - Check before the call: Reason and effective date for ORIAS deregistration, and whether any current activity still constitutes insurance intermediation.; Current ARR, retention, gross margin and customer-country mix.

**Eupago** — Eupago is a live, passported and security-certified payment institution with real merchant infrastructure. It misses the founder-background thesis and lacks verifiable venture-stage, funding, institutional-customer and globally scalable architecture evidence. A recent plugin security advisory adds diligence risk.
  - Licences: Banco de Portugal Payment Institution 8709; AISP and PISP permissions
  - Found via: Portugal Fintech Report 2025
  - Check before the call: Confirm total external capital raised and whether the company is institutionally investable at Seed/Series A.; Obtain the current Banco de Portugal service-permission detail for AISP/PISP, not only the PI directory entry.

**AQUATY** — AQUATY is directly on-thesis: it digitises private-market issuance and lifecycle workflows, uses an evidenced German tied-agent regulatory structure, has a founder exit, an experienced scale-up operator and a former SIX Swiss Exchange leader as advisor. However, the current proof set is not yet institution-grade: AQUATY holds no own authorisation, named Tier-1 FI customers are absent, public security/API controls are weak, the product retains a private-investor channel, and the last disclosed round is stale. Re-engage only after a named institutional production case or material financing/licensing milestone.

  - Check before the call: Capture a dated extract from BaFin's official tied-agent register confirming AQUATY, Concedus and the exact permitted activities; verify the umbrella covers every advertised jurisdiction.; Obtain issuer/customer references, live securities volumes, recurring platform revenue, take rate, ARR, retention and customer concentration.

**Onramper** — Onramper is independent, technically credible and commercially deployed across hundreds of Web3 businesses, with strong API documentation and a top-quality seed lead. The score is constrained by thin qualifying founder pedigree, no own licence or public security certification, a four-year funding gap and residual consumer surfaces. Pass for the current institutional-only mandate, but re-open if its identity expansion, security controls or regulated-partner model moves decisively upmarket.
  - Found via: EQT Ventures portfolio
  - Check before the call: Explain EQT's portfolio page showing a 2024 exit and identify the buyer or secondary holder; no company acquisition was found and the Dutch entity remains independent.; Obtain current ARR, transaction volume, take rate, gross margin, customer retention and the reconciled count of 200, 400 and 800 business claims.

**Onyze Digital Assets, S.L.** — There is genuine bank production evidence, two completed CNMV sandbox projects and solid institutional product fit, but the current EU licence gap is a hard veto and the founding team misses the fund's Tier-1-FI pedigree criterion.
  - Licences: Bank of Spain virtual-asset custody and exchange registration, 30 Mar 2022; LEI 529900CEE6XQ4QK5JG20
  - ⚠️ Review flags: LICENCE VETO: Spain's MiCA transition ended 30 June 2026. Onyze is absent from the current CNMV and ESMA authorised-CASP registers; its historical Bank of Spain VASP registration is no longer sufficient for EU crypto-asset services.; Company marketing still describes the Spanish custody solution as regulated while the current CNMV authorisation record is missing; legal/regulatory diligence is required before contact.
  - Found via: ESMA MiCA consultation response directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm directly with CNMV whether a pending MiCA application exists but is not publicly displayed.; Obtain the AFA licence decision and scope directly from Andorra's public register.

**Vega (Vega AltOS)** — Apollo is an unusually strong anchor customer and strategic investor, Motive returned to lead the Series A, and the UK company is active. The score remains poor because Vega is at the €20–30m stretch tier, exposes little technical assurance or developer infrastructure publicly, has no verified founder tenure meeting the Tier-1 bank threshold, and currently lacks the fund's tokenisation or regulated-digital-asset bridge. Fit includes a 0.09 anti-signal deduction.
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: Motive Partners portfolio
  - Check before the call: Verify Alexis Augier's exact Goldman Sachs dates; a tenure of at least two years would change signal #1.; Obtain AltOS API documentation, security certifications, penetration-test results, uptime history, DPA and enterprise SLA.

**Bleckwen** — Bleckwen has credible production deployments, public customer voices and quantified fraud outcomes, but the last disclosed round is seven years old, the active team is small, and the public API/security/certification layer falls well below institutional diligence thresholds. No licence veto applies because the French register classifies it as a software company and its materials describe a scoring vendor, not the regulated principal.
  - Found via: France FinTech founder profile
  - Check before the call: Reconcile the 2016 technology start, 2018 LinkedIn date and 2019 legal spin-out for cohort analysis.; Obtain current ARR, growth, retention, cash runway and details of the 2025 capital increase.

**Synthera AI** — Excellent fintech investors, a relevant Goldman Sachs founder and a technically differentiated risk engine justify monitoring. The investability gap is commercial: no named customer, production deployment, public integration surface or independently verifiable security artefact. The product also lacks Stratum3's preferred TradFi–digital-asset bridge.
  - Found via: Motive Partners portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain named reference customers, paid production status, ARR and retention.; Review the SOC 2 Type II report, auditor, period and exceptions under NDA.

**Facctum** — Facctum has excellent founder-bank pedigree, a credible institutional product, and one named production deployment. However, two eligibility gates remain unknown and the public record is thin on funding, independently verifiable security certification, customer breadth, and open technical proof. The low score includes four confirmed anti-signal deductions.
  - Found via: Targeted sector search (AML/compliance infra)
  - Check before the call: Confirm stage, total external capital, and investor identities directly with management.; Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate and SOC 2 Type II report, including auditor, scope, and validity dates.

**Quantfolio** — Quantfolio has unusually strong real-bank validation: DNB confirms the integration, Storebrand is live, more than €5bn has been advised through the platform, and two major Nordic FIs are shareholders. The score is held down by weak public security assurance, a stale funding date with persistent losses, a crowded/diluted cap table, Nordic concentration and no digital-asset bridge.
  - Found via: Finance Innovation Norway members
  - Check before the call: Obtain the latest ARR, gross margin, net retention, runway and the reason for continuing operating losses after the 2022 financing.; Confirm the exact cumulative equity raised and current post-money valuation; early Skandiabanken and 2020 amounts are incompletely disclosed.

**RequirementONE** — RequirementONE is a genuine, live compliance SaaS with global regulatory coverage and credible Lloyd's/RegTech recognition. It lacks the founder FI pedigree, recent financing, security transparency and named institutional production evidence required for Stratum3's thesis. The €30m capital gate also remains unproven because the Seed amount is undisclosed.
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab alumni
  - Check before the call: Obtain the 2020 Seed amount, investor list and current cap table directly from management.; Request current ARR/customer concentration and named bank or insurer production references.

**Crassula** — Crassula is a live, technically credible banking-software company with named production use, strong public API/release documentation and substantial partner-reported scale. It is nevertheless a mature bootstrapped vendor with ambiguous venture stage, no qualifying founder pedigree, limited top-tier institutional customer proof and weak public security assurance. The fit score includes four confirmed anti-signal deductions of 0.03 each.
  - Found via: Fintech in Baltic — Latvia startups listing
  - Check before the call: Obtain audited consolidated revenue, growth, gross margin and profitability across the UK and Latvian entities.; Confirm cumulative founder/shareholder capital and whether any undisclosed external round occurred.

**Datavillage** — Founder-market fit is credible and the current product targets a real institutional fraud/AML pain point. However, the public evidence shows two FTE, no named production financial-institution deployment, no current ISO certificate and a major B2C-to-B2B pivot. The evidence-weighted score remains poor after four confirmed anti-signals.
  - Licences: ISO 27001-aligned information-security management stated in privacy policy
  - Found via: FinTech Scotland Financial Crime Innovation Call
  - Check before the call: Obtain the actual ISO 27001 certificate, certification body, scope and expiry date.; Verify current ARR, paid financial-institution deployments, renewal rates and whether the anonymous bank testimonial is production.

**Murphy** — Murphy is one of the most thesis-relevant companies in the set: it has a named live financial-services customer across three countries, a NatWest programme selection, strong specialist coverage, rapid international hiring and top-tier investors at core cheque fit. The public evidence is still weak where institutional diligence matters most—founder financial-regulatory pedigree, security certification proof, technical interfaces, customer-level quantified ROI and jurisdiction-by-jurisdiction collections compliance—so the contract score remains poor and the company should be monitored pending certificate and customer diligence.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Murphy claims ISO 27001 compliance, but no public certificate, certification body, certificate number, scope or expiry date was found; treat the certification as unverified until the certificate is obtained and checked.; Debt collection and automated borrower communications are regulated-adjacent and country-specific. Murphy claims operations in more than ten countries, but public evidence does not map local collection permissions, call-recording/consent rules, vulnerable-customer controls or AI Act risk classification by jurisdiction.
  - Found via: Northzone portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain and independently verify the claimed ISO 27001 certificate, certification body, scope, issue/expiry dates and any excluded environments.; Validate the GRENKE production deployment directly: legal entity, countries, go-live dates, portfolio size, recovery uplift, cost reduction, complaints and renewal status.

**Prommt** — Prommt is alive, independently operated, well integrated and has unusually concrete merchant evidence: named customers in several countries, quantified savings, gateway partnerships and current awards. Its regulatory perimeter is sensibly outsourced to licensed partners. However, it is merchant acceptance software rather than institutional financial infrastructure, has no FI customer in production, weak public founder-depth evidence and a notably closed technical surface. The low fit score and four anti-signals place it outside Stratum3's core thesis.
  - Found via: FinTech Ireland map
  - Check before the call: Obtain the complete cap table, round dates, total capital raised and current financing-stage designation.; Request the current PCI DSS Attestation of Compliance, penetration-test summary, SOC 2/ISO roadmap, incident history and DPIA.

**REOR20** — REOR20 has strong founder-market fit through Swiss Re and credible validation in Lloyd's Lab, with globally scalable insurance-risk infrastructure. The evidence still looks pre-commercial: customers are unnamed, the API is sales-gated, and funding is opaque. Monitor the active round and re-score immediately if a named insurer production deployment appears.
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab Cohort 10
  - Check before the call: Confirm cumulative financing and the investor behind the February 2025 debt conversion.; Obtain named insurer references, production status, ACV and renewal data.

**AIS Gateway** — The AISP status is genuine and remains active/passported, with Bank Pocztowy production use and strong founder banking experience. Public product-security evidence, developer transparency and customer breadth are weak, and stage plus total funding remain unknown. The absent public AML framework triggers mandatory human review rather than an automatic pass.
  - Licences: Polish Financial Supervision Authority-registered AISP
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Regulated AISP activity is active and verified, but no public MLRO or substantive AML framework was found; human compliance review required.
  - Found via: KNF payment-services register
  - Check before the call: Pull the primary KNF register extract and obtain the AISP registration reference, insurance and current compliance-officer details.; Confirm total capital raised, current stage and exact post-merger ownership.

**The Upright Project** — Upright has unusually credible named institutional deployments, public API documentation and a defensible scientific-data proposition. It lacks the fund's preferred financial-infrastructure founder pedigree and independently verifiable security assurance, so the opportunity is more interesting commercially than it is a clean team/compliance match.
  - Found via: Helsinki Fintech Guide
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate number, scope, issuing body and expiry date.; Confirm current ARR, net retention and customer concentration.

**CargoX** — CargoX is real, profitable, technically credible and unusually well deployed: the DGC registry confirms its MLETR assessment, IGP&I independently lists the eBL system, and government production use is visible. The score is nevertheless poor for this mandate because founder/FI pedigree, regulated-finance credentials, named production-bank evidence and institutional VC validation are weak, while the legacy CXO/Cyprus structure adds MiCA and governance diligence.
  - Licences: ICC DSI/DGC MLETR Digital Trust verified
  - Found via: EU Digital Finance Platform fintech map
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current ISO certificate schedule and validate its issuer/accreditation and expiry directly with the certification body.; Map the legal and economic relationship among CARGOX d.o.o., CargoX Holding Ltd. (Cyprus), CXO token holders and platform relayers; obtain MiCA counsel analysis.

**Luciditi (Arissian Ltd)** — A prior founder exit, broad official DVS certification, FIDO2 support, public APIs and a privacy-preserving architecture are credible assets. However, the micro-team, absent disclosed venture financing, lack of named production FI traction and weak institutional founder fit leave the opportunity below mandate quality today.
  - Licences: UK DVS Trust Framework identity, orchestration and holder roles
  - Found via: UK statutory digital verification services register
  - Check before the call: Current ISO certificate and independent security audit; Named production customers, ARR, retention and deployment volumes

**DoxyChain** — The Polish trust-service registration is real and the company has a live institutional credential product, but its customer evidence is education-led rather than financial-institution-led. Founder pedigree and developer transparency miss the thesis, and financing appears stale.

  - Found via: INATBA member directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current ISO 27001 certificate and verify the certification body's accreditation and expiry.; Confirm total capital including any unannounced 2023 or 2025 financing shown by private databases.

**CyberUpgrade** — Copla is alive, independent and well within the cheque ceiling, with an exited core-banking founding team, 100+ regulated customers, seven-figure ARR and repeat investors. It still misses the thesis's required Tier-1 institution founder pedigree and regulated digital-asset or tokenisation rails, while public product-security evidence is thin. The commercial profile is investable, but the strict Stratum3 fit is poor.

  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate, certification body, certificate number, scope and expiry date.; Verify seven-figure ARR, gross retention, net retention, ACV, customer concentration and the software-versus-services gross-margin split.

**SKJOLDET** — SKJOLDET has unusually strong early usage, award, grant, and programme signals for a 2024 company, and the pre-transaction data product fits bank fraud infrastructure. However, its founders lack the fund's required FI/digital-asset pedigree and there is no named bank deployment or institutional security evidence yet. Monitor for a bank-originated production announcement and financing disclosure.

  - Check before the call: Obtain current grant amounts, any equity financing, formal stage, and cap table.; Identify bank/PSP customers and distinguish pilots from paid production deployments.

**AID:Tech** — AID:Tech has real international deployments and a credible institutional disbursement use case, but no qualifying Tier-1 founder tenure and weak current technical transparency depress fit. Stablecoin, wallet and card functionality without visible Irish registration requires legal diligence. The consumer layer and five-year financing gap further weaken Seed/Series A investability.

**Corporify** — Corporify has credible enterprise traction, quantified ROI and live use by an investment fund, but it is adjacent ownership/compliance SaaS rather than a direct regulated-finance rail. The founder profile and closed product posture miss the fund's strongest Seed predictors, producing a high-confidence poor fit despite a sound business.

  - Check before the call: Verify the exact duration and function of Olivier Van Borsel's BNP Paribas Fortis role.; Request ISO 27001/SOC 2 certificates, API documentation and ARR/net-retention data.

**InvestSuite** — InvestSuite is firmly within the thesis and has unusually strong founder-bank pedigree, public APIs, a bank-confirmed production deployment and €15m of disclosed capital. The contract score is poor because most high-value library thresholds demand Tier-1 quantified customer cases, public security artefacts, regulatory/standards engagement and top-tier institutional investors that are not evidenced. The 2024 strategic recapitalisation, departure of two original founders and filed headcount contraction also make ownership, runway and execution capacity priority diligence issues.

  - Check before the call: Obtain the 2024 recapitalisation cap table, investor rights, valuation and confirmation of whether the transaction changed control.; Reconcile the website's 50+ team claim with the Belgian filing's decline to 8.5 FTE and identify contractors or employees in foreign entities.

**Complok** — The CEO's rare Tier-1 bank-compliance depth, named multi-country users, founder ownership and recent EU R&D support are positive. They are outweighed by five-year revenue of only €97,904, a three-person team, no production customer case, no public security/API proof, repeated product pivots and an unexplained official deletion notice.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: The website claims continuing-education licence 229603, but an official education-register entry was not located and registry-derived business data reports zero activity licences/notices. This is not a financial-services licence, but the claim must be verified before diligence relies on it.
  - Found via: Change Ventures portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the underlying Official Announcements deletion notice and determine whether Complok is being merged, voluntarily dissolved or merely affected by a procedural filing.; Verify continuing-education licence 229603 directly in Estonia's education register, including holder, scope and validity.

**tokenforge GmbH** — tokenforge is unusually strong on EU tokenisation infrastructure, public APIs, live issuer deployments and regulatory-sandbox engagement. The founder pedigree falls short of the fund's Tier-1 FI requirement, funding is opaque, and its public token issuance introduces governance and retail-exposure risk.

  - Check before the call: Confirm equity stage, total capital raised, ownership of FinToVentures and founder dilution.; Obtain the ISO certificate, full smart-contract remediation evidence and enterprise penetration-test reports.

**Creem** — Creem has unusually good developer tooling, visible integrations, named customer outcomes and fast self-reported growth for its age. It remains a poor Stratum3 fit: the customer base is indie software rather than regulated institutions, no FI deployment is public, security assurance is absent, corporate filings are adverse, and the payment/CASP perimeter cannot be cleared from public registers. Do not meet until the licence chain, safeguarding model, overdue filing and merchant-funds complaints are independently resolved.

  - Check before the call: Obtain counsel's perimeter analysis and contracts with each licensed acquiring, payment, safeguarding, tax and USDC payout principal.; Confirm whether the 2025 report and deletion notice have been cured and reconcile the July 2026 cap-table changes.

**Terrapin** — Terrapin has unusually credible product depth for its size, global fixed-income scope and visible usage growth, plus a founder who managed institutional assets. The main weaknesses are limited named-customer proof, sparse security assurance and no Tier-1-bank founder.
