  - Check before the call: Confirm ARR, customer count, gross retention and the identity of additional institutional clients.; Obtain security audit, penetration-test and SOC 2/ISO 27001 evidence.

**Currency Network** — BIS project credit, a SWIFT technical-challenge runner-up placement and GLEIF finalist status validate unusually relevant technical capability, while the founder brings Tier-1 bank architecture experience. The strict score remains poor because institutional production use, recurring revenue, security assurance and financing disclosure are absent. Monitor for a named deployment or priced round before prioritising a meeting.
  - Found via: EBAday Fintech Zone 2026
  - Check before the call: Obtain the full cap table, funding total, latest share-allotment terms and confirmation that cumulative capital is below EUR 30m.; Obtain named production customers, contract values, deployment stage and recurring revenue versus consulting revenue.

**Payhound** — The MiCA CASP and e-money passport evidence is real, current and unusually strong for a small provider, while disclosed volume, live merchant integrations and a named customer reference show an operating product. The score is held down by opaque ownership/funding and founder history, closed developer infrastructure, thin institutional-customer proof, unverifiable ISO certification details and material adverse-media diligence risk. Monitor until beneficial ownership, capital raised, compliance leadership and the OCCRP-linked transaction history are satisfactorily diligenced.

  - Found via: MFSA VFA / MiCAR CASP licence holders
  - Check before the call: Founders, beneficial owners and current cap table from an official Malta Business Registry extract.; Total external capital raised and current venture stage.

**KeeeX** — KeeeX has real, patented provenance technology, professional-only recurring licences and credible industrial deployments. For Stratum3 it remains underpowered: no named financial institution is in production, the team is sub-10, institutional financing is stale and public security/compliance assurance is thin. Its absence from qualified eIDAS trusted lists is not a veto because KeeeX does not claim to be a qualified trust-service provider.
  - Found via: France FinTech RegTech white paper
  - Check before the call: Confirm current ARR, renewal rate and the trade-finance share of revenue versus industrial traceability.; Obtain customer references for a named bank, insurer or trade-finance platform in non-pilot production.

**FinregE** — The live multi-year FCA Handbook contract, Moody's strategic investment, Innovate UK grant and genuinely global regulatory dataset are excellent enterprise signals; diligence is constrained by unusually anonymous founder employment and customer references, limited developer/security transparency and no explicit digital-assets bridge.

  - Found via: Level39 member directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm Rohini Gupta's named regulator and bank employers, exact roles and tenures.; Obtain the Moody's investment amount, instrument, valuation and governance rights.

**Regpit** — Regpit is live, independent and commercially credible enough to show a production fintech workflow, and Jacob Wende brings unusually relevant AML/legal depth plus visible regulatory engagement. However, public evidence does not show Tier-1-bank adoption, pan-European customer breadth, enterprise security certification, public developer documentation or institutional VC validation. The product also sits at the broad, services-assisted end of AML software rather than Stratum3's digital-asset infrastructure core.
  - Found via: FIBE Berlin startup programme
  - Check before the call: Request ARR, net revenue retention, gross margin and the software-versus-advisory/services revenue split.; Verify the complete cap table, round lead, valuation and founder ownership.

**claimsforce** — claimsforce has credible insurance-domain leadership, a current TÜV Rheinland ISO 27001 certificate, disciplined institutional investors and multiple named German insurer/claims-organisation references, including production use at Zurich. It remains a defensible vertical SaaS candidate, but the public evidence shows little technical openness, no target-region diversification and no disclosed financing since 2020.

  - Check before the call: Obtain current ARR, growth, gross retention/net retention, profitability and runway after six years without disclosed financing.; Confirm the current role and ownership of co-founder Bastian Schweitzer and reconcile the older CTO designation with the present management team.

**AkinovA** — AkinovA has an unusually experienced insurance-market board, a genuine current BMA marketplace licence, a completed Hiscox/Guy Carpenter transaction and a clean active UK company. However, commercial proof is stale and unquantified, public security/AML controls are thin, no current ISO 27001 certificate is visible, and the claimed UK AR relationships need direct-register confirmation.

  - ⚠️ Review flags: The Bermuda Insurance Marketplace Provider licence is current in the BMA register, but both UK appointed-representative claims could not be resolved to current AkinovA entity entries in the FCA register; the FCA confirms an AR is not directly authorised. Obtain current FCA extracts and principal confirmations before investment.
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab InsurTech100 profile
  - Check before the call: Obtain current FCA register extracts for Akinova Limited under Suntera Advisers (UK) Limited and HISL Brokers Ltd, including appointment dates and permitted activities.; Request BMA licence conditions, current AML officer details, AML programme, latest regulatory examination outcome and complaints record.

**Burbank** — Burbank is live, early-stage, institutional payment infrastructure with an exceptional repeat payments founder and strong specialist investors. However, it lacks the thesis-required Tier-1 FI or digital-asset founder pedigree, and public evidence of production FI customers, security assurance and developer maturity is thin. The entity-name confusion adds diligence friction.

  - Check before the call: Confirm paid production deployments and revenue with named issuers, acquirers or PSPs.; Obtain the claimed PCI certificate, scope, assessor and current validity.

**D2X Group** — D2X is unusually on-thesis: a live, AFM-authorised professional-only market venue with named trading participants, EEA passports and credible institutional connectivity. The contract score remains poor because public security assurance, quantified traction, standards engagement and repeatable customer evidence are sparse, while the approximately €20m capital history makes cheque fit a stretch.

  - Found via: AFM trading platforms register
  - Check before the call: Obtain current audited financials, ARR, trading volume, open interest, member count, fee schedule and clearing economics.; Confirm the exact euro-equivalent capital raised, post-money valuation, founder ownership and whether the 2025 extension remained within the Series A.

**BANKSapi** — BANKSapi is an active, genuinely regulated German open-banking platform with an Austrian passport, public API specifications and production deployments at Swiss Life, Gothaer and Stadtsparkasse Wuppertal. However, its funding position is opaque and stale, its public founder/market signals are limited, and it is conventional fiat open-banking rather than Stratum3's institutional digital-asset infrastructure. Public AML leadership and auditable security certification also require diligence.

The evidence still falls short on production deployment, quantified customer outcomes, technical openness and financing transparency; founder Filip Verbeke's 2024 departure also makes current ownership and continuity a priority diligence topic.

The score is nevertheless suppressed by founder-profile gaps, a very small current team, no public security certification or DPIA, stale financing and limited current international traction. **Palm** — Palm has excellent domain-founder credibility, high-quality seed investors and unusually strong quantified enterprise case studies. **Quanted** — Quanted is a live, capital-efficient institutional data platform with founder ownership and unusually good investor follow-on evidence. It falls short on the thesis's founder pedigree and commercial proof: no verified Tier-1-bank founder, no named customer, and only trial-stage deployment evidence. Its current fit is poor after three confirmed anti-signals.

**Nevermined** — Nevermined is a credible frontier payment-infrastructure bet with unusually strong open-source execution, self-serve documentation, public uptime, live Exa commerce and a Mastercard-originated partner announcement. It scores poorly because the library strongly penalises the lack of traditional payments/regulatory founder pedigree, named FI customers and mature institutional controls. The original Web3-to-AI-commerce pivot, shifting founder roster and unresolved Swiss licensing perimeter make it too early for a meeting despite attractive technology and investor validation.

Treat this as a licensing veto until Swiss counsel confirms that Nevermined is purely technical/non-custodial and requires neither FINMA authorisation nor SRO membership.

  - Check before the call: Obtain a Swiss regulatory-perimeter opinion covering card delegation, the 1-2% transaction fee, stablecoin settlement, AMLA/SRO status and any US money-transmission exposure.; Inspect the ISO 27001 certificate, SOC 2 Type II report, PCI SAQ-D attestation and current smart-contract/API penetration tests; the public Omniscia audit predates the present product.

**Näktergal** — Näktergal has real lender deployments, public customer executives and a quantified Bluestep production case, supported by founders with direct bank-operating experience. The signed accounts create material concern: 2024 revenue roughly halved, two customer projects paused and average headcount fell to three, while the last verified financing was in 2021. Licensing is not applicable to the software-supplier model and the Swedish FI register check produced no licence veto.
  - Found via: Swedish FinTech Association annual report
  - Check before the call: 2025 and 2026 revenue recovery, current employees and status of the two paused projects; Current ARR, gross retention, runway and financing plans

**51nodes GmbH** — 51nodes has genuine technical depth, credible industrial partners and government-funded infrastructure work, but it is an engineering consultancy rather than a clearly scalable institutional-finance product. Founder thesis fit and production financial-institution traction are absent, while stage and total funding remain unresolved. Pass for venture investment; consider only as a technical ecosystem contact.
  - Found via: Blockchain Bundesverband member directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm ownership, cumulative external capital and whether management is seeking venture financing.; Separate recurring software revenue from project consulting and grants.

**Looping1.com B.V. (LoopingOne)** — LoopingOne has credible payments founders, a current Dutch small-provider derogation, a national eID integration and a useful Ebury relationship. However, the verified status is an exemption rather than a full PI/EMI licence, current named customer proof is thin, technical and security evidence is closed, and the business still exposes a consumer-facing GreatYouPaid flow. Pass until it demonstrates licence progression and institutional production traction.
  - Licences: DNB-registered exempt payment service provider
  - Found via: DNB public register of payment service providers
  - Check before the call: Retrieve the record-level DNB detail page for NL4022459 and confirm effective date, permitted payment-service categories, transaction-volume ceiling and any restrictions.; Clarify whether the previously announced EMI application is active, withdrawn or replaced by the exempt-provider and emerchantpay partnership model.

**Fundvis** — Fundvis is a live, capital-efficient Luxembourg regtech with a named production user, strong local-program validation and recent financing. Its founder pedigree, open technical proof, security assurance and named multi-country customer base are weak against the contract, while the product has no MiCA, tokenisation or digital-asset angle. The low score reflects thesis mismatch and many confirmed early-stage gaps, not a claim that the product lacks utility.
  - Found via: LHoFT Luxembourg Fintech Map 2025
  - Check before the call: Confirm ARR, contracted institutions, net retention and the geographic customer split.; Request security certifications, pen-test results, DPIA, SLA and DPA.

**WalliD** — WalliD has usable public APIs, standards-aligned credential infrastructure and credible institutional investors, but the team is extremely small and lacks the required financial-institution pedigree. Commercial claims of 50+ implementations, 25 countries and 4m identities are unsupported by a single named customer case study, while security-certification badges lack certificate details. Smart Sign appears to rely on regulator-listed Evrotrust for qualified certificates, avoiding an immediate licence veto, but the integration must be verified.
  - Found via: Portugal Ventures portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the complete financing history and size of the 2022 Portugal Ventures investment.; Verify contractual integration with Evrotrust and correct the public 'EvoTrust' naming discrepancy.

**Mimo** — Mimo is alive, commercially credible and well funded, with named customers, quantified ROI, active hiring and strong European investors. The fit is nevertheless weak for Stratum3: its customers are SMEs and accounting firms rather than financial institutions, it lacks institutional-grade technical evidence and security certification, and its regulated payment stack depends on partners while total disclosed capital is already in the stretch zone.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: OMIM Technology is not itself FCA-authorised for the payment/e-money activity; Mimo relies on Airwallex (claimed FRN 900876) and claims agent status under Plaid (FRN 804718). The underlying partner firms are FCA-authorised, but OMIM's claimed agent scope was not independently retrievable from the public register interface and needs manual register confirmation before investment.; Mimo's security page says 'aligned with ISO 27001' but publishes no certificate, certification body, number or scope.
  - Found via: Northzone portfolio
  - Check before the call: Manually verify OMIM Technology's claimed Plaid agent status and exact permitted scope in the FCA register, including whether the relationship remains current after the 2026 wallet migration.; Reconcile the €18m 2024 headline between equity and the asset-backed facility, and reconcile database reports of four rounds before relying on the approximate €25.8m total.

**Truvity** — Truvity is highly on-theme technically: public EUDI documentation, explicit ARF protocols and a live status page demonstrate credible identity infrastructure. Commercial proof, founder history and certification evidence are weak, and the June 2026 parent-control change makes independence a gating diligence item. Monitor until ownership and customer deployment are confirmed.
  - Found via: EU Digital Finance Platform FinTech Map
  - Check before the call: Determine whether the June 2026 UK-parent control change was an acquisition, recapitalisation or internal reorganisation.; Verify current founders, management roles and beneficial ownership of the Dutch operating company.

**VAKT** — VAKT has exceptional production adoption, named global customers and a defensible shared-network position. It is nevertheless a mature corporate consortium with no individual founder fit, at least 12 strategic investors and seven financings, making it structurally unlike Stratum3's founder-led Seed/Series-A target. Pass despite the strong business.
  - Found via: Level39 member directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm current priced stage, full 2025 round terms and shareholder rights.; Obtain the current ISO 27001/27017 certificates and audit scope.

**SafeCypher** — The An Post deployment is unusually strong seed-stage proof, and the processor integration plus patent are valuable; however, founder departure and voting control, undisclosed financing, thin public assurance evidence and the award-attribution discrepancy materially weaken investability.
  - Found via: EBAday Fintech Zone 2026
  - Check before the call: Exact funding total, round classification, investors, valuation and runway.; Reason Valebond controls more than 75% of voting rights and the founder's current ownership.

**Finovox** — Strong recent financing, revenue growth, named enterprise references and ISO certification demonstrate a live scaling business, but the thesis fit is weakened by no verified institutional-finance founder pedigree, limited developer transparency and no digital-asset bridge.
  - Found via: France FinTech BtoFi infrastructure map
  - Check before the call: Obtain the AFNOR certificate number, validity dates and audit scope.; Confirm recurring ARR, gross retention, net revenue retention and customer concentration.

**Symphopay** — Symphopay is a profitable, fast-growing Romanian payment-orchestration vendor with unusually experienced payments operators and real bank/merchant production use. It remains geographically concentrated, under-resourced, opaque technically and outside the fund's regulated digital-asset/tokenisation thesis.
  - Licences: PCI DSS compliant; Visa and Mastercard certified
  - Found via: GECAD Ventures portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current PCI DSS AOC directly and confirm whether Visa certification remains current.; Confirm contractual allocation of regulated payment activity between Symphopay, banks and acquirers.

**Mistho** — Mistho is live, certified and economically within scope, with credible privacy/security controls and a functioning enterprise model. It lacks the thesis-required founder pedigree and public FI production proof, while its last disclosed financing is stale. The official DVS certificate is current but expires on 15 September 2026, so renewal should be monitored.
  - Licences: UK DVS Trust Framework registered attribute service
  - Found via: UK statutory digital verification services register
  - Check before the call: Verify certificate 243680's current ISO 27001 validity and scope directly with British Assessment Bureau.; Request named lender, insurer or bank production references and quantified outcomes.

**Nitrokey** — NetHSM is credible, open-source and actively maintained key-management infrastructure with public APIs and an independent security audit. The investment fit is weak because no named financial-institution customer, financial-services founder background, institutional round, or FI-specific go-to-market evidence was found, while consumer sales remain material. Licensing is not applicable to its supplier model and no licence veto was triggered.
  - Found via: EuroStack Directory Project
  - Check before the call: Enterprise versus consumer revenue mix; Named bank or financial-market-infrastructure customers

**Handwave** — Handwave has genuine payment-domain founder pedigree, strong regional Seed backing and a third-party-corroborated Visa relationship. It is nevertheless pre-revenue and pre-production, while biometric privacy/security assurance and standards alignment are not yet publicly evidenced. Monitor for certified pilots converting to named acquirer or merchant production before meeting.
  - Found via: Practica Capital investments
  - Check before the call: Verify founder Worldline titles, tenure and specific payments products, plus Jānis Stirna's Luminor tenure.; Obtain the Visa agreement scope and identify the unnamed financial-institution and merchant pilot partners.

**Credibur GmbH** — Credibur is highly relevant structured-credit infrastructure with named customers, €2bn of reported live facility volume and strong specialist investors. The strict score is held down by no Tier-1-bank founder and sparse public security, compliance and developer evidence. Monitor for institutional references and assurance artefacts before meeting.
  - Found via: Digital Lending Summit speaker directory
  - Check before the call: Verify the €2bn live volume and production status directly with at least two customer references.; Obtain SOC 2/ISO 27001 evidence, penetration-test results, DPIA and business-continuity documentation.

**Gubbi AG** — Gubbi has unusually current commercial proof: GDX launched in 2026, works under a clearly disclosed BaFin-regulated liability umbrella and has live third-party integrations. Yet Gubbi itself is not licensed, founder thesis fit is weak, technical/security assurance is largely private, and stage plus capital raised remain unknown. The adjusted fit is poor despite credible DACH distribution momentum.
  - Found via: Blockchain Bundesverband member directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain audited cap table, cumulative financing and current ownership across Gubbi AG, Gubbi Technology, Gubbi Project, Gubbi Protection and MFC relationships.; Verify GDX's exact tied-agent registration entry and contractual allocation of regulated duties between Gubbi and MFC Service GmbH.

**Vidos** — Vidos has credible, certified verification infrastructure, excellent standards breadth and a real self-serve test surface. The score is held down by the thesis's founder pedigree requirements, absent named enterprise customers and stale disclosed financing.
  - Licences: UK Digital Verification Services Trust Framework certified provider
  - Found via: PwC Scale Tokenisation and Digital Assets cohort
  - Check before the call: Obtain customer references and ARR/usage metrics under NDA.; Verify the claimed ISO 27001 certificate and FCA/Central Bank of Ireland sandbox participation against primary records.

**NYALA Digital Asset AG** — NYALA shows real API product depth, EU sandbox selection and strong quantified issuance activity. The missing current BaFin register entry for Smart Registry is a licence veto, and security, funding and enterprise-purity evidence remain below meeting threshold.
  - Licences: Smart Registry GmbH operates a BaFin-supervised crypto-securities register under a provisional German authorisation
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Smart Registry performs crypto-securities registration but is absent from BaFin's current company database; the site claims only preliminary permission under §65 KWG. Obtain direct BaFin confirmation before relying on the licence.
  - Found via: INATBA member directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain a dated BaFin extract confirming Smart Registry's current permission, scope and transitional status.; Verify the current cap table, cumulative funds raised and proceeds from the public offering.

**fidentity** — fidentity is real, independently active and unusually well evidenced, with bank production usage, public API docs and current KPMG identity-proofing certificates. It does not claim or appear to require a FINMA financial-services licence; official FINMA searches found no adverse action, so there is no licence veto. The contract nevertheless yields a poor fit because the founder lacks the required Tier-1 finance pedigree, expansion remains Swiss-centric, fresh capital is stale and consumer signing remains in the product mix.
  - Found via: IFZ FinTech Study 2026 company directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the underlying current ISO/IEC 27001 certificate number, scope and certification body; the public claim alone did not meet signals 50 or 96.; Confirm ARR, net retention, transaction volumes and country-level revenue outside Switzerland.

**xpate** — xpate has genuine regulatory substance in Latvia, two active European entities and a strong developer surface. The public record does not yet show institutional customer validation, material regulated-entity revenue or a thesis-matching founding team.
  - Licences: Latvian electronic money institution licence 27-55/2023/7
  - Found via: Bank of Latvia licensed electronic money institutions
  - Check before the call: Render and archive the FCA register record for claimed FRN 901021 and the EBA passport-country file.; Reconcile the stated 2018 founding year with the 2019 brand/UK incorporation and 2022 Latvian entity.

**Crayonic B.V.** — Strong FIDO-certified hardware and thoughtful public security architecture are outweighed by no Tier-1 FI founder pedigree, minimal named commercial proof and stale financing. FIDO2 is a product certification, not a financial licence, and no regulated activity requiring a financial-services authorisation was identified.
  - Licences: FIDO2 and WebAuthn-compatible products
  - Found via: Decentralized Identity Foundation contributors
  - Check before the call: Verify current recurring revenue, installed enterprise base and named financial-institution deployments.; Obtain current cap table and confirm whether any financing occurred after the disclosed US$1.3m seed.

**Nxchange** — Nxchange has unusually valuable live exchange permissions and credible tokenised-securities infrastructure, but its financing stage and capital base are opaque. The 2024 AFM AML action, retail exposure and limited public technical controls outweigh the regulatory asset until enhanced diligence clears governance and stage.
  - Licences: AFM investment firm authorisation, 24 March 2016; AFM multilateral trading facility authorisation, 28 August 2018
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Adverse regulatory action: AFM AML/Wwft direction imposed 4 November 2022 and published 22 March 2024; AFM states all deficiencies are now remediated.
  - Found via: AFM trading platforms register
  - Check before the call: Obtain the full cap table, total capital raised, current revenue/profitability and investor ownership to resolve G3 and G4.; Review independent evidence of post-remediation AML testing, MLRO qualifications and board risk oversight.

**kicker.cloud** — The BlackRock/Goldman founder background and pure institutional workflow model are unusually thesis-relevant, while the live Savantiq rebrand and EIF profile confirm continued operation. However, the company publishes no named customer, quantified case study or verifiable ISO 27001 certificate, and its last disclosed capital is more than three years old.
  - Found via: Vendep Capital portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain exact founder role titles and employment durations from primary employment records.; Request the ISO 27001 certificate, certification body, scope and expiry.

**Vibrant** — Vibrant has good Nordic investors, 300% reported growth, public developer documentation and credible scheme partnerships. It lacks thesis-grade founder pedigree and digital-asset relevance, while the undisclosed licence/principal structure around direct onboarding and payouts requires regulatory diligence.
  - Licences: PCI DSS compliant
  - ⚠️ Review flags: LICENCE STRUCTURE: no Vibrant entry was found in the Danish FSA register despite KYC, payment processing and payout claims; confirm licensed acquirer/agent responsibility.; SECURITY EVIDENCE: Mastercard/Visa security approval dates to 2022 and no current public PCI DSS AOC was located.
  - Found via: Luminar Ventures portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current PCI DSS AOC and identify the licensed acquirer or payment-institution principal for every EEA country.; Quantify the 2026 Gilion facility so the €30m capital gate and cheque tier can be conclusively resolved.

**Crypto Risk Metrics GmbH** — The contract score is harsh because the bootstrapped company has no investor validation, limited founder pedigree and little public technical assurance. Nevertheless, nearly 100 institutional customers across 23 countries, named bank and market-infrastructure users, customer-issued production evidence and direct MiCAR white-paper responsibility make this one of the strongest thesis-aligned commercial signals in the set. A meeting should focus on retention, margins and assurance artefacts.
  - Found via: Bitkom Blockchain working group ecosystem
  - Check before the call: Obtain audited ARR, gross margin, retention, customer concentration and pricing details behind the reported nearly 100 customers.; Review the IDW PS 951 report, API documentation, penetration-testing evidence, SLA and data-provenance controls.

**Umazi** — Umazi has a credible founder, clean UK filings, government support and a live recurring-revenue product. However, the current proposition is SME-led, no named financial-institution production customer is verifiable, and technical/regulatory standards evidence is weak for an identity vendor. Pass until enterprise traction and security assurance are substantiated.
  - Found via: Decentralized Identity Foundation contributors
  - Check before the call: Obtain named regulated-firm customers, ARR and renewal metrics.; Verify security certification, DPIA and exact ledger/credential standards.

**Ledyer** — Ledyer has a coherent B2B product, experienced payment founders, useful public APIs and a live merchant reference. However, the claimed regulatory status does not clear the register-truth rule, and the company combines a very small revenue base with stale funding and concentrated Swedish exposure.
  - Licences: registered payment institution with Finansinspektionen
  - ⚠️ Review flags: LICENCE UNRESOLVED: company copy claims Finansinspektionen registration, but no exact public-register entry for Ledyer AB / 559311-3714 was independently retrievable. Do not invest until FI confirms current status, category and permissions in writing or via a direct register record.
  - Found via: Visa Innovation Program Europe Nordics and Baltics 2025
  - Check before the call: Obtain the direct Finansinspektionen register URL, institution number, registration category and permitted services for 559311-3714.; Clarify whether Ledyer is a full payment institution or only a registered small payment-service provider and whether any passport exists.

**SIGN8 GmbH** — The German regulator verifies SIGN8's qualified trust-service status, and the product has credible enterprise API, HSM and EUDI-wallet foundations. However, founder pedigree, financial-institution traction, institutional funding and digital-asset relevance are all weak against Stratum3's thesis. Pass for this fund while recognising a legitimate regulated trust-infrastructure business.
  - Licences: eIDAS Qualified Trust Service Provider
  - Found via: EU Trusted List Browser
  - Check before the call: Confirm formal financing stage, revenue, ownership of Teal Capital and the purpose/substance of SIGN8 AG in Switzerland.; Obtain the complete German/EU Trusted List service record, conformity-assessment reports and insurance coverage.

**zkr.** — Live, funded B2B regtech with strong usage claims, public APIs and a national eID integration. It misses the thesis's founder pedigree and publicly evidenced institutional-customer bar, while security-certification evidence is incomplete. The resulting score includes three confirmed anti-signal deductions.
  - Found via: Holland FinTech network directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the actual ISO 27001 certificate and verify certification body, scope and expiry.; Confirm whether any unnamed top-20 accounting customer is a regulated financial institution willing to reference.

**Mopso (Amlet)** — Mopso has strong thesis relevance, a central-bank alumnus founder, real revenue growth and unusually strategic seed investors. Its technical and commercial evidence remains thin: no named production customer, security certification, public API or quantified case study. The Amlet marketability conflict is a human-review veto; monitor until Mopso supplies a current legal basis or regulator clearance and named production references.
  - Licences: Participant in Banca d'Italia Milano Hub innovation programme
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Regulatory go-to-market conflict: Amlet's current SaaS availability claim must be reconciled with Banca d'Italia's public conclusion that it could not be marketed outside the sandbox.
  - Found via: Fintech District RegTech community list
  - Check before the call: Obtain written Italian counsel or Banca d'Italia confirmation explaining what changed after the sandbox conclusion and where Amlet may legally be marketed.; Separate Brain, Orama and Mantis revenue from Amlet; request ARR, 2025-2026 growth, retention, pipeline and customer concentration.

**FinXP** — FinXP has the strongest proven operating scale in this set—an active EMI passport, €2bn annual processing and 12,000 accounts—but it may be too mature for the fund's stage and lacks the required founder pedigree. Public technical and customer-reference transparency is weak, so confirm profitability, ownership and growth before deciding whether it is still venture-investable.
  - Check before the call: Verify current MFSA licence details directly in the MFSA register, including all passport destinations and permitted services.; Confirm profitability, ARR/net revenue, growth and whether the business is still within Seed/Series A economics.

**Stable Mint** — The regulatory substance is real: an active Maltese EMI record, published MiCA EMT whitepapers and an official French AMF passport notice. The product also shows credible API, MPC, analytics and multi-chain infrastructure. However, strict signal resolution finds no named institutional customer, no bank production deployment, no qualifying founder banking pedigree, no public assurance certificate and no disclosed financing amount. The unresolved stage and capital ceiling prevent confident cheque sizing, so the opportunity does not clear Stratum3's evidence bar today.
  - Licences: MFSA Electronic Money Institution; MiCA Article 60(4) crypto-asset custody
  - Found via: MFSA Financial Services Register
  - Check before the call: Obtain an MFSA public-register extract confirming the exact current EMI services and Article 60(4) status directly from the dynamic register.; Confirm total external cash raised, latest priced-round stage, cap table and founder ownership after the 2025 CoinShares/GTSA reorganisation.

**cheqd** — cheqd has unusually strong open-source identity infrastructure, public APIs, live integrations and EUDI alignment, and the founders bring relevant Accenture/R3 digital-infrastructure experience. The investability case is held back by absent named FI production references, weak institutional security assurance, stale disclosed funding and the August 2025 departure of co-founder/CTO Ankur Banerjee from the legal company's board.
  - Found via: Decentralized Identity Foundation members
  - Check before the call: Reconcile CHEQD LABS LIMITED, Cheqd Foundation Limited and the $CHEQ issuer; obtain group structure, intercompany agreements and confirmation of the June 2026 MiCAR whitepaper notification.; Clarify Ankur Banerjee's current operational role and the post-departure CTO/technical leadership succession.

**Siccar** — Relevant verifiable-data infrastructure and FRIL engagement are outweighed by unverified security certificates, founder turnover, stale disclosed funding and no proven financial-institution production customer.
  - Licences: ISO 27001 certified; ISO 22301 certified
  - Found via: FinTech Scotland fintech directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain current ISO 27001 and ISO 22301 certificates with accredited issuer, scope and validity dates.; Confirm whether the July 2025 share allotment represented new external capital and quantify cumulative funding.

**LastingAsset** — The research-heavy cryptography team and recent public investment are credible strengths. However, no Tier-1 founder employment is confirmed, no financial-institution production customer is named, and the live consumer product dilutes institutional focus. Three confirmed anti-signals reduce the score to poor.
  - Licences: FinTech Scotland Financial Crime Innovation Call participant
  - Found via: FinTech Scotland Financial Crime Innovation Call
  - Check before the call: Verify Nanik Ramchandani's named financial-institution employers, roles and tenure.; Reconcile total grants and the £250,000 Scottish Enterprise investment with the cap table and fully diluted ownership.

**Sinpex** — Sinpex has strong Series A investor quality and credible production proof: named regulated customers, quantified ROI, public API documentation and multiple live integrations. It is a regulatory software vendor rather than a regulated entity, so BaFin, EBA, FCA and VASP licences are not applicable and no missing-licence veto is triggered. The strict thesis fit is constrained by absent Tier-1-FI or digital-asset founder pedigree, limited regulatory-body engagement, unverified ISO certification and no demonstrated non-European expansion.
  - Found via: EU-Startups funding coverage - European digital identity rounds
  - Check before the call: Reconcile Tech.eu's €14m two-round total with the separately disclosed €1.5m 2021 seed and any pre-seed; obtain the fully diluted cap table.; Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate, scope, certification body, expiry, latest penetration test and security incident history.

**Diwala** — Diwala is a real, founder-controlled credential utility with standards participation, named institutional claims and impressive contracted reach on company figures. However, the audited registry picture overwhelms those positives: minimal employees and revenue, severe negative equity and liquidity, stale product evidence, no public security assurance and no independently verified production FI deployment. Pass unless a diligence package proves a recapitalisation, current team, active platform usage and customer-backed recurring revenue.
  - Found via: Decentralized Identity Foundation members
  - Check before the call: Reconcile the registry-derived NOK 144.5m public-support total with the much smaller grant/debt schedule in Dealroom and the accounts.; Obtain 2025/2026 management accounts, cash balance, debt maturity schedule, going-concern note and recapitalisation plan.

**sideos GmbH** — sideos has real standards-aware middleware, self-serve API documentation, a thoughtful user-held-data architecture and unusually complete enterprise terms. However, the present signal set looks subscale and commercially unproven: four visible employees, no named customer, no current security assurance, no recent capital and no qualified eIDAS status. The eIDAS 2.0 statement is treated as product compatibility—not a claimed licence—so its absence from the German trusted list is not a licence veto.
  - Found via: Decentralized Identity Foundation contributors
  - Check before the call: Obtain total funding, current cap table, runway and formal financing stage.; Reconcile why the public team fell from 13 people in 2021 to four visible employees now.

**StarLiX** — StarLiX is thematically relevant, FCA-sandbox-validated and structurally B2B, but the evidence base has not progressed from launch and accelerator material to named production customers, technical assurance or a disclosed financing round. The original CEO has left formal control, the current team is extremely small, and the claimed DSS status belongs to unrelated SLIX Finance. Pass unless diligence can establish an active regulated-PSP deployment, current product ownership and financing runway.
  - Licences: FCA Regulatory Sandbox (Cohort 7); Bank of England/FCA Digital Securities Sandbox (as SLIX Finance Ltd)
  - ⚠️ Review flags: FCA Regulatory Sandbox participation is verified but is not FCA authorisation; no StarLiX FRN was found.; The Bank of England/FCA DSS Gate 1 notice belongs to the separate legal entity SLIX Finance Ltd (16140193), not StarLiX Ltd (12872864).; Clarify which regulated entity issues, safeguards and redeems the described 1:1 GBP-backed SLX token and whether StarLiX conducts any regulated payment activity.
  - Found via: FCA Regulatory Sandbox (Cohort 7) + BoE/FCA Digital Securities Sandbox
  - Check before the call: Confirm current operating leadership, founder ownership and the circumstances of Chryssi Chorafa's departure.; Obtain a current cap table, funding history, stage, cash runway and latest management accounts.

**Asterias** — Andrew Walker brings excellent Tier-1 insurance-domain experience and Lloyd's confirms a live operational platform. The investment case remains too early to underwrite because customers are unnamed, reach is UK-only, the team is five people and security assurance is not public.
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab Cohort 13
  - Check before the call: Confirm whether Jacques Germishuys remains CTO after resigning as a Companies House director on 3 March 2026.; Obtain named production references, ARR, contract sizes, retention and pipeline conversion.

**Fennech Financial** — Fennech has a highly relevant Tier-1 banking founder, clear enterprise infrastructure positioning and strategic Lloyds backing. However, public evidence is weak on security certification, current financing momentum, developer transparency and quantified financial-institution deployments. The ICO registration is verified only as a company claim and is not a financial-services licence.
  - Licences: ICO registration ZA547124
  - Found via: UKTN
  - Check before the call: Obtain direct ICO-register confirmation for ZA547124; the public entry was not retrievable during research.; Request current ISO 27001/SOC 2 evidence, penetration-test summary and enterprise security pack.

**Porters** — Porters is exceptionally young but has a credible regulated-fintech operator team, a top-quality European lead investor and an unusually concrete production reference: Finom reports 7,000-plus documents processed within weeks. The hard-signal score remains poor because there is no Tier-1 bank case, public security certificate, open technical surface, regulatory engagement, digital-asset component or documented SaaS economics. The company claims no licence; register checks found none, and its non-custodial vendor model does not appear to require one.
  - Found via: Earlybird Porters portfolio page
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate, certifying body, certificate number, scope and expiry directly from the Vanta Trust Center.; Confirm commercial model, ACV, gross margin and whether fees are recurring per case, per workflow or subscription.

**Depasify** — The API surface, Fidor-experienced founder and TradFi/Web3 orchestration product are genuinely on-thesis. They are outweighed by a lapsed D705 status, no visible MiCA CASP authorisation, a very serious supervisory-cooperation sanction and unresolved regulated-activity perimeter.
  - Licences: Bank of Spain cryptoasset service-provider registration D705
  - ⚠️ Review flags: LICENCE VETO: Bank of Spain D705 is historical and expired with the MiCA transition; Depasify is not present in the 24 July 2026 ESMA authorised-CASP register.; ENFORCEMENT VETO: €240,000 final Bank of Spain fine and public reprimand for a very serious infringement in 2025.; PERIMETER VETO: Current terms describe Depasify directly providing crypto-asset services, while one client now attributes regulated services to another CNMV-authorised CASP and only technology support to Depasify.
  - Found via: AEFI Activos digitales vertical
  - Check before the call: Obtain legal counsel's current MiCA/PSD2 perimeter analysis and confirm which regulated services Depasify itself still performs.; Ask for the CNMV CASP application or authorisation reference, if any, and all correspondence concerning transition from D705.

**Frictionless Markets** — The technical thesis is unusually on-target: audited open-source contracts, live multi-chain issuance, institutional-only distribution and official EU Blockchain Sandbox selection. The score remains poor because founder thresholds, named FI production customers, security certification and public developer maturity are weak. More importantly, the claimed regulatory status does not resolve to an authorisation in CSSF public records; the structure may be lawful through an unregulated securitisation vehicle and licensed third parties, but that must be established by legal diligence before any meeting advances.
  - Licences: Luxembourg-registered securitisation fund O46; Professional-investor issuance under MiFID II
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Regulatory-perimeter veto pending counsel: neither the CSSF entity search nor public registers verified Frictionless Markets Sàrl as a VASP/CASP, investment firm, specialised PFS, fund manager or transfer agent. The company may rely on licensed custodians and an unregulated securitisation vehicle, but that reliance must be documented before investment.; Do not treat RCS O46, RCS B272278, LEI 213800PVGXAUM7KDT872 or 'professional-investor issuance under MiFID II' as a licence.
  - Found via: European Blockchain Sandbox third cohort
  - Check before the call: Obtain a current Luxembourg RCS extract for B272278 and O46, the fund's regulatory classification, and the full group chart.; Request a written regulatory-perimeter opinion identifying which entity performs issuance, transfer-agent, custody, exchange and settlement functions and under which exemptions or partner licences.

**Complytek** — Complytek has unusually credible bootstrapped traction, including named bank deployments and customers across several regions. It nevertheless misses the fund's mandatory founder-pedigree thesis and provides little public technical or security assurance, producing a low evidence-resolved fit rather than an evidence gap. The fit score includes four confirmed anti-signal deductions of 0.03 each.
  - Found via: Visa Innovation Program Europe
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate, scope and certification body directly from management.; Verify audited ARR, retention and customer concentration behind the reported €2.9m-equivalent 2025 revenue.

**ProofSpace** — ProofSpace is a legitimate, active and technically thoughtful credential platform with public APIs, standards-based identity, self-serve deployment, privacy-preserving architecture and credible UNDP/education implementations. It misses Stratum3's core pattern: no Tier-1 FI founder, no FI production customer, no European regulated-identity alignment and very little fresh capital or headcount momentum. Six confirmed anti-signals eliminate the already-low weighted fit.
  - Found via: Decentralized Identity Foundation contributors
  - Check before the call: Request current ARR, paying-customer count, interaction volume, retention and a customer split by sector and geography.; Obtain the full cap table and reconcile the 2019 programme financing with the 2024 Techstars investment.

**FintechX Technologies** — The Aggreg8 licence is real, active and passported, but the confirmed MNB enforcement action is a veto pending remediation proof. Commercial proof, security certification and financial-institution founder pedigree are also weak.
  - Licences: Operates Aggreg8, MNB-supervised payment institution and Hungary's first registered AISP
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Adverse MNB supervisory action requires closure evidence
  - Found via: HUNFINTECH promising companies report
  - Check before the call: Obtain the MNB remediation-closure letter and current internal-control audit evidence.; Confirm whether Aggreg8 remains wholly controlled by FintechX and obtain the consolidated cap table.

**CyberTide** — CyberTide is a well-supported, capital-efficient German compliance-security startup with credible accelerators, government grants, DACH channel validation and a flexible self-hosted product. It lacks the founder pedigree and named production financial-institution evidence central to Stratum3's thesis, and its displayed ISO certification is not publicly verifiable to the signal threshold. Monitor for named FI deployments, certificate disclosure and a finance-specialist commercial hire.
  - Found via: Tenity Incubation Batch 12, Switzerland, March 2024
  - Check before the call: Verify exact founder employment dates and whether Alex Shyvakov held a direct role at any Tier-1 financial institution.; Obtain the TÜV SÜD ISO 27001 certificate number, scope, validity dates and audit report.

**Fume (FumeX AG)** — Fume is live, independent and unusually capital-efficient, with a credible external smart-contract review, clear non-custodial SaaS perimeter and strong strategic fit at the TradFi/on-chain boundary. It does not yet clear Stratum3's evidence bar: the team lacks qualifying institutional pedigree, production customers are not independently verifiable, and security/compliance maturity beyond the contract audit is thin. The licence search is not a veto because Fume explicitly supplies software, never controls assets, and assigns fund licensing/KYC/AML to managers or licensed partners.
  - Found via: Tenity (formerly F10)
  - Check before the call: Obtain customer-originated proof of the claimed live AIF and the claimed CHF400m-AUM on-prem deployment, including paid-production status.; Confirm whether Fume or any affiliate ever controls subscription/redemption smart-contract keys or fiat/stablecoin settlement flows; this could change the FINMA/AMLA perimeter.

**Ralio** — Thesis fit, founder payments-infrastructure experience, security design and investor validation are attractive, but the evidence remains pre-production: no named financial-institution customer, gated documentation, no current security certification and an unresolved regulatory perimeter around wallet/payment-initiation language.
  - Found via: SVV portfolio news
  - Check before the call: Obtain product architecture, API documentation, sandbox access and an independent penetration-test or audit report.; Confirm with UK regulatory counsel whether Ralio Wallet, agent-triggered payment initiation or stablecoin routing requires FCA authorization or an appointed-agent/outsourcing structure.

**pAIscreen** — Alex Noton's prior RegTech leadership and exit plus EBAday selection are credible founder/market signals. However, public evidence shows no named issuing-bank customer, production deployment, API documentation, security certification or disclosed financing, leaving institutional readiness and cheque fit unproven. FCA-register absence is not a veto because the disclosed activity is software analytics rather than a regulated payment or crypto-asset service.
  - Found via: EBAday Fintech Zone 2025
  - Check before the call: Identify all co-founders and verify whether any has qualifying Tier-1 bank, payment-network or large-scale engineering tenure.; Obtain cap table, cumulative capital raised, formal stage and current runway; public sources leave both G3 and G4 unknown.

**Gokind** — Gokind is live institutional data middleware with real production evidence at SAS EuroBonus and several current Nordic integrations. However, it lacks the thesis-critical founder pedigree, has a very small team and weak public security/developer assurance, while cumulative capital remains unknown. The explicit consumer-to-B2B pivot and four confirmed anti-signals reduce the adjusted fit to poor.
  - Found via: Swedish FinTech Association member directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the complete cap table and cumulative capital raised to resolve G4 and cheque fit.; Request the private API documentation, security audit, penetration-test summary, DPIA and any ISO/SOC roadmap.

**CrypDefi** — CrypDefi/Fortkey is unusually strong technically for its age, with public docs, a self-serve sandbox, hardware enclaves and externally corroborated ISO 27001 certification. It remains a weak fit under the contract because founders lack the required institutional pedigree and customers are anonymous. Monitor pending customer references and clarification of the founder transition and regulatory perimeter.
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: Tenity European Accelerator 2025
  - Check before the call: Reconcile the original founders Matteo Janssen/Jochem Hoes with the current Alba Veisllari/Jochem Hoes co-founder presentation and ownership.; Obtain named customer references, production volumes, latency benchmarks and renewal evidence.

**Fluence World** — Fluence has the best external traction in this batch: a named FCA-authorised MGA implementation, an independent Insurance Insider award, Lloyd's Lab selection, and public enterprise references at AbbVie/Amgen. It remains too opaque for a meeting recommendation under the signal contract: founders lack institutional-finance pedigree, security assurance and developer proof are absent, investors are unnamed, team scale is small, the insurance focus is recent, and a statutory filing appears overdue.
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab Cohort 15
  - Check before the call: Confirm the exact 2025-2026 financing consideration, fully diluted cap table, option pool, and whether share allotments represent a new round.; Obtain the Aspect contract, deployment date, ARR, renewal status, loss-ratio or underwriting improvement, and a named customer reference call.

**Verifoxx** — Serious PET research and regulator-backed validation are offset by minimal commercial proof, no public FI production deployment, a tiny team and weak enterprise security evidence.
  - Licences: FCA Digital Sandbox participant; FinTech Scotland Financial Crime Innovation Call participant
  - Found via: FinTech Scotland Financial Crime Innovation Call
  - Check before the call: Verify Bala Subramanyan's J.P. Morgan role, function and tenure.; Obtain cumulative funding by round, grant agreements, private-investor names, runway and current raise plans.

**AQ Forensics GmbH** — AQ demonstrates real case volume, specialist visibility, credible Crystal tooling and court-oriented methodology. It is nevertheless a poor venture and thesis fit today: founder pedigree is intelligence/forensics rather than Tier-1 financial infrastructure, commercial proof lacks named institutional customers, and the delivery model remains founder-led project work with a material consumer line. No CASP licence veto applies because AQ does not perform custody, exchange or transfer.
  - Found via: Blockchain Bundesverband member directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm revenue split across consumers, law firms, authorities, banks and other enterprises, plus repeat-purchase and gross-margin data.; Verify the complete capital history, cap table and formal venture stage.

**Select ID** — The government trust-framework entry and UK Finance mandate validate a real orchestration role. However, stage and capital are undisclosed, no qualifying founder pedigree is identifiable, and the FI deployment remains pilot-stage. The public technical and enterprise-commercial evidence is too thin for the thesis today.
  - Licences: UK DVS Trust Framework registered orchestration service
  - Found via: UK statutory digital verification services register
  - Check before the call: Obtain the cap table, cumulative cash funding and current stage.; Identify the actual founding team and verify prior financial-institution roles and deployments.

**ITSCREDIT** — ITSCREDIT has credible production bank deployments and meaningful international lending-software traction. It misses the founder-background and digital-asset/regulated-rails thesis, while security certification and ownership independence require verification. The business may be sound, but Stratum3 fit is poor.
  - Found via: Portugal Fintech Report 2020 — Lending & Credit
  - Check before the call: Obtain ITSCREDIT's current shareholder register and confirm whether ITSector retains control.; Obtain the ISO 27001:2022 certificate showing body, number, scope and validity.

**AML Factory** — AML Factory has strong domain expertise, named production users and a coherent B2B SaaS model. However, it lacks the required Tier-1 founder tenure, digital-asset rail exposure and public security or API proof. The stale financing record and mostly domestic commercial evidence make it a poor thesis fit despite credible execution.
  - Found via: France FinTech BtoFi infrastructure map
  - Check before the call: Confirm ARR, retention, customer geography and whether the 25+ customer count has grown since 2024.; Request security certifications, penetration-test results, architecture, API documentation and data-processing terms.

**Aptus.AI** — Aptus.AI is eligible, independent and has rare direct validation from both Banca d'Italia and Intesa Sanpaolo, plus a technically credible founder pair and a scalable machine-readable legal-data thesis. It scores poorly under the literal library because most founder-pedigree and public developer-assurance tests are unmet. A meeting becomes attractive if the Intesa deployment is renewed and referenceable, 2025–26 revenue has accelerated, and the ISO and model-governance evidence withstand diligence.
  - Found via: P101 Aptus.AI investment announcement
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current cap table and reconcile the €3m latest round with approximately €4.2m cumulative funding.; Request ISO certificate body, number, scope and expiry; penetration-test executive summary; incident record; RTO/RPO and DORA contractual schedule.

**Hanko GmbH** — Hanko is alive, capital-efficient and technically impressive, with a large active open-source footprint and one production fintech deployment. It misses the founder-background requirement and is a broad authentication platform rather than infrastructure focused on regulated institutional finance. The FIDO product claim has credible official ecosystem support, but the exact current certificate entry should still be obtained.
  - Licences: FIDO2-certified authentication technology
  - Found via: FIDO certified product registry
  - Check before the call: Obtain the exact Hanko product record or certificate identifier from the FIDO Certified Products Directory.; Confirm the precise 2023 round amount and whether any financing occurred after May 2023.

**TrustPro** — The Irish government register confirms a real, live eIDAS qualified moat; the June 2026 Irish trusted list also shows its timestamp service as granted, and Adobe, DocuSign, SPID and European Commission EPREL evidence proves interoperability beyond company copy. However, the investability evidence is weak: founder and team backgrounds are opaque, itAgile controls 79%, external capital is unknown, the developer surface is closed, commercial proof is almost entirely unnamed, and the free consumer line dilutes the institutional focus. Monitor only if the team can disclose ownership, enterprise revenue, customer references and independently verifiable security certification.
  - Licences: eIDAS QTSP (Ireland): qualified e-signature, qualified e-seal, qualified e-timestamp
  - Found via: EU Trusted List / eIDAS QTSP register
  - Check before the call: Confirmed founders and full executive/technical/compliance biographies.; Relationship between TrustPro, itAgile and Havant, including ultimate beneficial ownership and IP ownership.

**CLIMATIG** — CLIMATIG is an active, independent and revenue-generating micro-company with credible climate science, Lloyd's Lab validation, public grant support and a newly confirmed Cytora integration. It remains too early and weakly evidenced for Stratum3: no named FI confirms production use, the team lacks institutional-finance pedigree, API/security assurance is mostly closed, B2C remains in scope, and the claimed ISO certificate cannot yet be independently verified.
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab Cohort 11
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO/IEC 27001 certificate, certification-body accreditation, certificate number, scope and validity dates; reconcile these with the completed implementation project.; Obtain contracts and reference calls for the claimed EY, Allianz and Marsh McLennan engagements and identify any regulated-FI production deployment.

**VSiD Verify** — VSiD has a genuine, current UK DVS certification and a broad production onboarding suite with named professional-services customers. It lacks the Tier-1 financial founder pedigree, open developer surface, FI deployments and cross-border customer evidence central to Stratum3's thesis, while the ISO claim is not independently substantiated to the library threshold. The undisclosed financing total also prevents a core cheque-fit conclusion.
  - Licences: UK DVS Trust Framework registered identity service
  - Found via: UK statutory digital verification services register
  - Check before the call: Obtain the ISO 27001:2022 certificate showing body, number, scope and expiry.; Confirm cumulative capital raised, latest round date and post-money valuation.

**mypensionID** — The UK trust-framework listing and GMPF production evidence are credible, but founder, technology-assurance, funding and digital-asset fit are weak. The product is narrowly useful pension identity infrastructure rather than Stratum3's institutional digital-finance core.
  - Licences: UK DVS Trust Framework registered identity service
  - Found via: UK statutory digital verification services register
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current DVS certificate and resolve the register's historical Target/mypensionID naming inconsistency.; Verify ARR, customer concentration, retention and funding history.

**ONINO GmbH** — ONINO is live, has a real regional-bank deployment, eight stated platforms and quantified tokenization activity, but it lacks the founder pedigree central to the thesis and publishes limited institutional security proof. Its public retail marketplace and native-token history weaken the pure B2B-infrastructure fit. Pass unless diligence can validate an unreported regulated liability umbrella, enterprise security certifications and a substantially stronger institutional customer base.
  - Found via: Blockchain Bundesverband member directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain a complete equity-plus-token funding reconciliation; G4 remains UNKNOWN and cheque_fit assumes the disclosed total is below €20m.; Identify the legal entity holding the disclosed German liability umbrella and map every regulated activity across ONINO, Cashlink and distribution partners.

**Agant** — Agant is live, business-only stablecoin infrastructure with genuine FCA MLR registration and credible distribution/custody partners. It nevertheless misses the thesis-critical founder pedigree, has no disclosed customer production evidence, and publishes little technical or compliance assurance. The registration is not full FCA authorisation.
  - Licences: FCA cryptoasset firm registration FRN 1037671
  - Found via: Innovate Finance UK stablecoin coalition
  - Check before the call: Obtain the full cap table and cumulative cash raised to convert G4 from UNKNOWN.; Verify the ISO 27001 certificate, certification body, scope and expiry.

**Themis** — Themis has the strongest relevant founder background in this set, a credentialed MLRO, a public-sector contract, real research reach and a useful Dow Jones integration. However, undisclosed investors and stage, weak public security assurance, no named production FI case and residual B2C exposure keep the mechanically weighted fit low.
  - Found via: UK Finance associate member directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm authoritative stage, all rounds, lead investors and current cap table.; Obtain ISO 27001/SOC 2 evidence, penetration-test summary and enterprise DPA/SLA.

**OMNIO** — Relevant practitioner pain and unusually capital-efficient reported sales do not overcome absent named production customers, demo-gated technology and unverifiable security certifications.
  - Found via: Vitosha Venture Partners portfolio overview
  - Check before the call: Obtain certificate copies for claimed ISO 27001, ISO 9001 and ISO 20000 certifications.; Verify the claimed Nasdaq and Düsseldorf customers, Alpha Bank pilot conversion, ARR and retention.

**NULA** — NULA shows meaningful local product adoption—10,000+ companies, 10,000 connected accounts, DSK/Yettel distribution and recurring SaaS pricing—but it misses Stratum3's founder archetype and target-market profile. More importantly, the claimed regulated-agent model is too opaque for an investment process: the principal and register entry are not disclosed. Pass unless management supplies regulator-grade evidence and a credible institutional expansion case.
  - Licences: Agent of a licensed financial-services provider for open-banking connectivity
  - ⚠️ Review flags: REGULATORY_VERIFICATION: NULA performs or facilitates account-information and payment-initiation workflows, but its claimed agent relationship could not be tied to a named licensed principal or exact entry in the current official BNB/EBA register.
  - Found via: Vitosha Venture Partners portfolio overview
  - Check before the call: Obtain the licensed principal's legal name, home regulator, licence number, Cloud Tech agent number, permission scope and current BNB/EBA register extract.; Reconcile the 2019 product founding date with the 2017 entity and confirm cumulative equity, grants, debt, latest valuation, runway and all financing dates.

**Commercio.network** — The open API, standards-based identity stack, usage pricing and public-sector contract are credible infrastructure signals. The investment case is outweighed by an unqualified founder profile for this thesis, stale funding, no named FI production customer and the withdrawn CONSOB registration.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: LAPSED REGISTRATION: CONSOB enrolled Commercio.network as a digital-securities register operator in December 2025, then cancelled it on 6 May 2026 at the company's request after it said activity never began.
  - Found via: INATBA member directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm whether any current OAM VASP or MiCAR/CASP application exists under a related Italian entity.; Request the CONSOB withdrawal rationale and determine whether the digital-register activity will restart through a licensed partner.

**Equisafe** — Equisafe has real regulatory substance in crowdfunding, an active Polish VASP registration, more than €400m reported invested, 20 partner institutions, production transactions and a credible TradFi-to-DeFi architecture. The case is weakened by no strict Tier-1 founder signal, minimal public technical assurance, no institutional VC backing, residual B2C exposure and an unresolved CASP authorisation gap for live crypto services.
  - Licences: Equisafe Invest — AMF Crowdfunding Service Provider, approval no. FP-2024-4
  - ⚠️ Review flags: VETO — crypto-asset services are live, but no Equisafe entity was verified as MiCA-authorised CASP; the active Polish record is a legacy VASP registration and a partner says CASP authorisation is still in process.
  - Found via: AMF regulated-entity registers (crowdfunding service providers)
  - Check before the call: MiCA CASP application authority, reference, scope and transition-period legal opinion; Cumulative funding, acquisition consideration, cap table and stage

**Talentir** — The fresh round, reported payout volume, DACH investors and combined bank/stablecoin product are strong sourcing signals. They are outweighed by a fundamental regulatory mismatch: Talentir contractually acts as merchant of record and onward remitter while no exact-name payment or CASP authorisation was found, and it publishes no adequate AML control stack. Until licensed-partner contracts and a defensible legal analysis resolve that issue, this is not investable.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Potential unlicensed regulated activity: the contract makes Talentir merchant of record, moves funds to its settlement account and onward-remits them, but no FMA/EBA PI or EMI record was found.; Potential unlicensed crypto-asset transfer service: Talentir actively transfers USDC/EURC, but no Talentir CASP appears in reviewed FMA/ESMA registers after Austria's MiCA transition ended on 1 July 2026.; No public AML framework, named MLRO, Travel Rule solution or blockchain-analytics partner despite Talentir claiming KYC/AML responsibility.
  - Found via: FinTech Futures funding roundup
  - Check before the call: Obtain the FMA/MiCA legal memorandum mapping every fiat and stablecoin flow, principal/agent status and applicable exemption.; Identify every licensed banking, payment, e-money and CASP partner; verify their register entries, passporting, safeguarding and outsourcing permissions.

**etonec GmbH** — etonec has the strongest payments-policy pedigree in the set and credible proof points in privacy-preserving stablecoin design, Mina delivery, the German EUDI ecosystem and externally cited research. It is nevertheless a small services-led consultancy with undisclosed stage/funding, anonymous clients, no production FI case and no repeatable product economics. Monitor for productisation or a venture spin-out rather than underwriting the consultancy as a conventional Seed/Series A software investment.
  - Found via: Digital Euro Association member list
  - Check before the call: Confirm current shareholders and founder ownership percentages after the December 2025 articles/shareholder changes.; Obtain revenue, EBITDA, backlog, client concentration and services-versus-licence revenue from the 2024/2025 accounts.

**ArBit Technology Limited** — The ESMA/MFSA white-paper notification is genuine, the Maltese entity is live, 2024 revenue is disclosed and the company is well aligned at a product-category level. But the filing is not a licence or approval, Blockestate is still under development, there is no founder institutional pedigree, no named customer, no company-specific audit, and the planned retail token sale conflicts with Stratum3's institution-only thesis.
  - Licences: ABTK MiCA white paper notified to MFSA and registered with ESMA
  - Found via: FinanceMalta business directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm current Malta Business Registry directors, shareholders, accounts and filing compliance directly from the MBR.; Obtain named customer references supporting the disclosed 2024 revenue and separate services revenue from founder-related or consulting revenue.

**Paybyrd** — The merchant product, public API, enterprise pricing and TAP deployment are unusually concrete, but the founding team misses the fund's core FI/scheme pedigree test, funding and group ownership are opaque, and the unresolved regulatory perimeter is a veto.
  - Licences: PCI DSS Level 1
  - ⚠️ Review flags: LICENSING-PERIMETER VETO: Paybyrd is not visible in the EBA/DNB PI/EMI registers. Its terms name licensed acquirers/processors, which may keep it within a technical-service-provider perimeter, but this must be confirmed with contracts and regulatory counsel before investment.; PCI DSS Level 1 cannot be independently verified from a public AOC because Paybyrd makes the attestation available only under NDA.
  - Found via: Portugal Fintech Report 2025
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current PCI DSS AOC/ROC, QSA identity, scope and expiry date.; Map the exact flow of funds and legal roles across Paybyrd, Worldline/Bambora, Rapyd and Mangopay; obtain counsel's PSD2 licensing analysis.

**Filedgr Sàrl** — The product is directly relevant to verifiable fund and tokenised-asset data, and public technical depth is unusually good for a small company. However, funding and stage remain unverified, the required Tier-1 founder signal is absent, and institutional production traction is not public. Monitor pending customer references and completed security certification.
  - Found via: Blockchain Bundesverband member directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm total capital raised, current cap table, and formal financing stage directly with management.; Obtain named financial-institution references and distinguish customers, pilots, integrations, and ecosystem partners.

**Cygnetise** — Cygnetise is a live, highly relevant mandate-control product with named production financial institutions and quantified customer value. The strict score is constrained by thin public assurance and developer evidence, no verifiable qualifying founder signal, repeated pre-Series-A financing and stale disclosed funding. It does not appear to conduct a separately licensed payment or crypto activity, so no missing-licence veto applies.
  - Found via: Innovate Finance member announcement
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current ISO 27001 certificate, scope, certification body and latest penetration-test report.; Verify recurring revenue, net retention, current runway and the purpose of the reported eight financing events.

**Blocksquare** — Blocksquare has a real multinational partner footprint, transparent enterprise pricing and a strong published smart-contract audit. It nevertheless scores poorly on founder institutional pedigree, public developer adoption, current institutional funding and—most importantly—licensing clarity around company-controlled crypto-asset functions. The investment case should not proceed without resolving the MiCA perimeter.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: LICENCE REVIEW REQUIRED: as of ESMA's July 2026 central register, no Blocksquare d.o.o. CASP authorisation, applicant record or notified MiCA white paper was found. Because the company markets token issuance, distribution, peer-to-peer trading, transfers and its own BST/Oceanpoint token, counsel must determine whether Blocksquare is only a technology vendor or itself performs regulated crypto-asset services. A required-but-missing authorisation is a veto.
  - Found via: EU Digital Finance Platform fintech map
  - Check before the call: Obtain a legal memorandum mapping each entity and function to MiCA, MiFID II, crowdfunding and local securities/property law.; Reconcile all financing events, grants, token treasury proceeds and fully diluted founder ownership; total capital remains unknown.

**ToriiPay** — ToriiPay is highly on-thesis payment infrastructure with independently verified EPC homologation and an EBAday finalist slot. Evidence of commercial adoption and funding is still too opaque for a meeting recommendation: no customer, production pilot or investor is named.
  - Found via: EBAday Fintech Zone 2025
  - Check before the call: Obtain the Seed round amount, date, lead and cap table to resolve the capital-ceiling gate and cheque fit.; Request named bank references, paid-pilot contracts, production volumes, retention and revenue.

**Mondo Gate Aktiengesellschaft** — Mondo Gate is eligible Seed-stage German digital-finance infrastructure and has assembled a credible regulated-partner stack. Its licence belongs to PayCenter/Tradevest/Tangany rather than Mondo, while founder pedigree, security assurance, and named FI customer evidence are weak. The live consumer product also dilutes the fund's institutional-only focus.
  - Found via: Blockchain Bundesverband member directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain the cap table, full lifetime funding, and the identity of current investors.; Confirm contractual allocation of AML, outsourcing, DORA, incident, and customer-liability duties across Mondo, Tradevest, PayCenter, and custody providers.

**Tranched** — Tranched is highly relevant to Stratum3's institutional digital-finance thesis and has an excellent seed syndicate, a16z accelerator validation and credible structured-finance founder experience. It does not clear diligence today: no named live financial-institution customer is public, the core smart-contract stack is closed and has no public audit, and no FCA authorisation or named authorised partner is visible even though the product markets investor access and secondary transfers in tokenised securitised positions. Pass unless management can produce a perimeter opinion, the authorised entities and permissions supporting every regulated step, independent security audits and production customer references.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: No FCA Register entry was found for TRANCHED GLOBAL LTD (15371382), despite marketing investor access, tokenised securitised positions and secondary transfers—activities that appear to cross the UK arranging/security-token perimeter unless performed entirely by named authorised partners or within an exemption.
  - Found via: Blockwall portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain UK regulatory counsel's perimeter opinion and map each origination, arranging, issuance, custody, administration, distribution and secondary-transfer activity to the responsible legal entity and FCA permission or exemption.; Identify the appointed administrator, broker, custodian, KYC provider, banking partner and stablecoin issuer, and verify each directly in the applicable public register.

**Data4Deals** — Data4Deals has strong thesis relevance, an FI-originated active partnership with Banco BPI, Visa accelerator validation and credible Portuguese investors. The strict score is held down by founder-profile mismatch, sparse technical and assurance artefacts, one independently verified bank deployment and fragmented pre-Series-A funding. It is a software supplier rather than a payment-service provider, so no missing EBA/Banco de Portugal licence veto applies.
  - Found via: Visa Innovation Program Europe Spain and Portugal 2024
  - Check before the call: Reconcile the $1.1m equity figure, Dealroom's multiple rounds and the €583,333 BPF/PRR loan into a fully diluted cap table.; Obtain BPI production metrics, customer consent architecture, data-controller/processor allocation and bank reference calls.

**NORBr** — NORBr has a credible payments-native team, current institutional funding, broad provider connectivity and unusually good public API/Postman/OpenAPI materials. Strict evidence thresholds still resolve poorly because no Tier-1-bank production case, customer-originated FI announcement, security certification or public pricing was found. It is explicitly a non-regulated orchestration supplier, so the EBA-register absence creates no licence veto.
  - Found via: Holland FinTech network directory
  - Check before the call: Named live production references from banks, acquirers or regulated PSP customers; SOC 2 Type II or ISO 27001 certificate and independent security-audit report

**Node16.ai** — Node16 is extremely early but unusually well aligned to privacy-preserving institutional compliance infrastructure, with credible academic IP and Danish grant validation. It currently has one founder, no Tier-1 FI alumnus, no public prototype or bank customer, and no security assurance, so the score is poor rather than a reflection of ultimate potential. Monitor for a technical co-founder, sandbox admission and first bank pilot.
  - Found via: EBAday Fintech Zone 2026
  - Check before the call: Confirm ownership and IP assignment from CBS/InnoExplorer into Node16 ApS.; Identify the technical co-founder/founding engineers and verify federated-learning implementation depth.

**Ver.iD (Subst.id B.V.)** — Ver.iD has a real eIDAS-shaped wedge, excellent public documentation, a verified ISO 27001 certificate, a completed DPIA and a reported live deployment at Nationale-Nederlanden. The score is held down by an angel-only cap table, no verified QTSP/QEAA status, single-country customer proof, limited open-source adoption and founders without direct financial-institution or regulator tenure.
  - Found via: EU-Startups funding coverage - European digital identity rounds
