  - Check before the call: Confirm whether the company has completed or closed the contemplated EUR 4m follow-on financing.; Obtain named reference calls and production metrics from Nationale-Nederlanden, Verbond van Verzekeraars and municipality Nijmegen.

**Cossack Labs** — Cossack Labs has credible cryptographic engineering, mature open-source assets and strong mission-critical references. However, the founder profile misses the fund's financial-infrastructure pedigree, financial-institution evidence is anonymised, and the current model is bespoke services rather than recurring SaaS. Stage and total funding remain unresolved.
  - Found via: UK Companies House register
  - Check before the call: Obtain cap table, total historical funding and current financing-stage evidence before treating the company as cheque-fit.; Request revenue mix, recurring-product ARR, gross margin and named financial-institution references.

**Theia Insights** — Theia has a strong specialist investor syndicate, recent Series A and credible AI research leadership. It provides almost no named-customer, public API, security-certification or model-evaluation evidence, while the founder lacks financial-institution operating history; under the contract those gaps dominate the otherwise attractive funding story.
  - Found via: Finextra funding coverage
  - Check before the call: Reconcile Theia Insights Ltd with the Delaware Theia Insights Inc affiliate and confirm which entity owns IP and received funding.; Obtain named customer references, production start dates, ARR and retention.

**Notakey** — Notakey is alive and technically credible at product level: it has current documentation, SDKs, a self-serve demo bank, recurring enterprise pricing, cross-data-centre deployment features and an experienced public-identity founding team. Investment evidence is much weaker. The only named banks are attached to a 2016 proof of concept, current customers are anonymous, public repositories are stale, no external audit or ISO/SOC certification is disclosed, and the product does not bridge into digital assets. The eIDAS qualified-signature wording also needs immediate legal clarification against the EU trusted list.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Licence-status review required: Notakey markets 'advanced & qualified electronic signatures', but no Notakey qualified trust-service-provider entry was located in the current EU/EEA Trusted List Browser and no supervisory authority is named. Treat qualified-status language as unverified until management proves it is only enabling software or supplies the responsible listed QTSP.
  - Found via: Fintech in Baltic — Latvia startups listing
  - Check before the call: Ask management to identify the qualified trust-service provider and supervisory authority behind its qualified-signature capability, or confirm in writing that Notakey is only unregulated enabling software.; Obtain two current reference calls from regulated financial institutions and distinguish production deployments from pilots.

**Orchestrating Identity** — The official DVS registration and current ISO 27001 certificate are strong, but the founder misses the core FI-pedigree thesis, funding/stage are unresolved, commercial proof is almost entirely absent and the small B2C product weakens structural fit.
  - Licences: UK DVS Trust Framework registered orchestration service; ISO 27001
  - Found via: UK statutory digital verification services register
  - Check before the call: Confirm whether the company is bootstrapped, seed-funded or financed through related OLR Group entities, and establish total invested capital.; Request named production customers, ARR, renewal data and reference calls.

**Tokenive** — Tokenive has unusually credible cryptography research and relevant digital-asset engineering for its age, plus a clean institutional identity thesis. It is nevertheless pre-production, under-documented against W3C/eIDAS standards, and lacks enterprise security assurance and customer references.
  - Found via: FinTech Scotland fintech directory
  - Check before the call: Verify the exact grant amount, cash runway, ownership and any angel or SAFE financing.; Obtain architecture documentation naming W3C VC/DID, proof system, eIDAS 2.0/EUDIW alignment and revocation design.

**PayByFace** — PayByFace has real merchant deployments, a Mastercard programme relationship and a technically credible processor-integrated model. Its core remains consumer biometric checkout rather than institutional digital-finance infrastructure, with no bank production deployment and weak founder/regulatory fit. Missing public DPIA and security-assurance evidence are material diligence concerns, although its disclosed technical-provider model appears not to require a PI/EMI licence.
  - Found via: Romania Fintech Map
  - Check before the call: Obtain Dutch Chamber of Commerce extracts, shareholder register and complete financing history to resolve stage, ownership and the €30m gate.; Request the legal PSD2 analysis supporting technical-service-provider status in every active market.

**Finkit Solutions** — The company is live, legally active, and has named production users, but the founders, financing, security assurance, technical openness, and customer scale are weakly evidenced. The most material issue is the gap between vendor status and marketing of potentially regulated investment/fund-administration services.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: {'type': 'licence-perimeter', 'evidence': 'Finkit describes itself as a buy-side firm offering suitability assessments, wealth-management/advisory, and fund administration. FINKIT SOLUTIONS LTD was not found on the CySEC investment-firm register. Confirm that every regulated service is delivered solely by named licensed partners before progressing.', 'url': 'https://www.cysec.gov.cy/en-GB/entities/investment-firms/cypriot/'}
  - Found via: Cyprus Registrar company mirror
  - Check before the call: Identify the founders and obtain role-level employment, education, ownership, and board histories.; Confirm cumulative funding, cap table, formal stage, and whether the business is bootstrapped.

**FinterAI** — The national sandbox report is meaningful third-party regulatory and architectural validation, and the government grant confirms continued R&D. However, the company remains subscale: no production bank deployment, no 2024 revenue, negative equity, two visible staff, no public security assurance, and an undisclosed funding total. This is a credible research-led concept but not yet Seed-quality commercial infrastructure for Stratum3.
  - Found via: Tenity Nordics & Baltics Incubation Batch 3, Tallinn, March 2024
  - Check before the call: Confirm all capital raised, including the amount and investors in the private/institutional pre-seed, and obtain the current cap table and runway.; Obtain 2025 management accounts, current cash, burn, liabilities, and evidence that negative equity has been cured.

**Platform-D** — Platform-D is the clearest thesis match in the set—Tier-1 capital-markets founder, tokenisation rails and a verified regulator pathway—but it is pre-live, unaudited and commercially unproven. Monitor through Gate 2, independent audit and the first named institutional production deployment.
  - Licences: UK Digital Securities Sandbox, Gate 1 passed 4 Mar 2025 (updated 16 May 2025)
  - ⚠️ Review flags: REGULATORY SCOPE: Gate 1 is not an operating licence. The official SAN permits non-live testing only and expressly prohibits live FMI activity until Gate 2.; The site describes a 'regulated settlement layer' and a 24/7 marketplace in product language, but separately confirms all displayed data are simulated and live transacting is targeted only after the next sandbox gate. Diligence must ensure marketing never implies current live authorisation.
  - Found via: Bank of England Digital Securities Sandbox Dashboard (Gate 1 SANs)
  - Check before the call: Establish financing stage, total invested capital, group-level funding and the economic ownership links among Platform-D, NYMLAB, Gayadeed, Dchain and D Foundation.; Obtain the Gate 2 application timetable, regulator feedback and permissions sought; do not model Gate 1 as authorisation.

**Monee Financial Technologies** — Monee is unusually thesis-aligned and has genuine FCA, BoE and Central Bank of Ireland testing access, plus relevant Tier-1 banking founder experience. It is not yet authorised for live FMI activity, has no named production customer and publishes no security assurance or developer evidence. Monitor for DSS Gate 2, a clean filing update, a credible lead investor and named institutional production traction.
  - Licences: FCA Regulatory Sandbox (stablecoins cohort); Bank of England/FCA Digital Securities Sandbox (Gate 1); Central Bank of Ireland Innovation Sandbox
  - Found via: FCA Regulatory Sandbox - stablecoins cohort
  - Check before the call: Confirm whether the overdue Companies House confirmation statement has been filed and whether any corporate change is pending.; Obtain the FCA Financial Services Register reference, or confirm explicitly that no operating permission exists beyond sandboxes.

**Sybolid Identity (Sybol)** — Sybol has exceptional early validation through Repsol production usage, a credible public-private pre-seed and direct exposure to eIDAS2-driven infrastructure demand. The score is constrained by a non-financial founding pedigree, one-customer concentration, and a near-total absence of public security, developer and formal trust-service evidence.
  - Found via: SETT portfolio
  - Check before the call: Obtain the current cap table, founder ownership and precise round total.; Request API documentation, architecture, W3C conformance detail and EUDIW ARF version mapping.

**Fynhaus** — The company has genuine bank deployments, channel distribution and unusually relevant ISO 20022/SWIFT gpi product depth. It misses the founder criterion, discloses neither stage nor capital, and provides weak security-assurance transparency; monitor until financing status, reference calls and certifications are resolved.
  - Found via: Holland FinTech network directory
  - Check before the call: Current financing stage, total capital and cap table; Direct customer reference confirming production status and measurable ROI

**31Third** — 31Third is a live, technically credible, Seed-stage infrastructure company with audited contracts, a public API, Enzyme/Valuya production evidence, more than $200m claimed protocol volume and genuine EU Blockchain Sandbox selection. The score is nevertheless poor because founder depth, regulated-FI customer proof, compliance staffing, current financing and market visibility remain weak. Most importantly, the website conflates the European Blockchain Sandbox with the legally distinct DLT Pilot Regime, while no relevant execution/CASP permission is publicly verified. That regulatory-claims mismatch is a veto until counsel and the regulator perimeter are resolved.
  - Licences: EU DLT Pilot Regime participant (per company)
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_capital_below_eur_30m — confirm before the call
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Licence/status mismatch: 31Third's website claims EU DLT Pilot Regime participation, but the current ESMA authorised DLT market-infrastructure register lists CSD Prague, 21X, 360X, Axiology, LISE and Securitize Europe—not 31Third. The European Commission confirms only European Blockchain Sandbox selection.; Potential missing permission: 31Third is absent from the AFM CASP register while marketing order routing/execution and a 10 bps transaction fee. AFM states execution of orders in financial instruments requires MiFID permission and crypto-asset services require authorisation or notification; specialist legal analysis of the non-custodial technical-layer exemption is required.
  - Found via: Outlier Ventures Base Camp
  - Check before the call: Obtain a written regulatory-perimeter opinion covering MiCAR execution/reception-transmission, MiFID II financial instruments, non-custodial Safe modules and the 10 bps fee.; Ask management to correct or substantiate every EU DLT Pilot Regime claim and provide any competent-authority reference number.

**Ecomate** — Ecomate has unusually credible methodology governance for its size: Accredia approved the proprietary scheme and ESMA's official list confirms a timely Article 51 notification, allowing transitional operation while registration is pending. It is nevertheless weak against Stratum3's institutional-finance signals: no named FI production customer, almost no institutional investor validation, a lightweight technical footprint and no final ESMA authorisation yet.
  - Licences: Ecomate ESG Rating scheme accredited by Accredia, agreement dated 11 Apr 2025; ESMA ESG-rating-provider application planned for Q3 2026
  - Found via: Fintech District RegTech community list
  - Check before the call: Track the formal ESMA registration application due in the 2 August–2 November 2026 window and the eventual approval or denial.; Obtain named financial-institution production references, contract values, renewal data and quantified outcomes.

**Mainframe Finance AG** — The unified Swiss institutional stack is unusually close to the product thesis and the founders have relevant banking and crypto operating experience. However, every material traction claim is anonymous, security assurance is absent, and VQF membership could not be independently captured from FINMA's live register.
  - Licences: VQF self-regulatory organisation, membership number 101314 (per company site)
  - ⚠️ Review flags: VQF membership 101314 is stated by the company and links to FINMA's generic member search, but the exact live register row could not be independently captured.; The company performs regulated VASP activity but publishes no named MLRO or substantive AML framework.
  - Found via: Crypto Valley Association corporate directory (Startup Members)
  - Check before the call: Obtain a dated VQF membership certificate and capture the exact FINMA SRO member-search result for Mainframe Finance AG.; Diligence the stated 500M+ client-capital figure, including named clients, assets under custody versus connected assets and revenue.

**Mediatech Limited (BIQUO)** — The BIQUO product is directionally relevant and structurally clean, but evidence indicates a two-person, newly commercialised product with no named FI production customer and no qualifying founder pedigree. The ISO claim is not register-grade evidence because no certificate or certification body is public; no financial-services licence is required for its monitoring-only activity.
  - Licences: ISO 27001
  - Found via: FinanceMalta BIQUO directory profile
  - Check before the call: Verify Malta Business Registry good standing, ownership and all funding.; Obtain the ISO 27001 certificate and audit scope.

**EyesClear** — The founder-market fit is credible and the platform's claimed seven-bank production footprint could be meaningful. Public evidence nevertheless resolves poorly against the strict library: customers are anonymous, security certifications and audits are absent, financing is stale and the product has no digital-asset bridge. Seven confirmed anti-signals reduce the approximately 0.13 weighted base fit to the 0.00 floor. Monitor pending primary diligence rather than dismissing the operational evidence outright.
  - ⚠️ Gate passed without evidence: G4_under_30m_raised — confirm before the call
  - Found via: Level39 member directory
  - Check before the call: Name and reference-check the seven banks claimed to be live in production, including contract dates, countries, deployment scope and renewal history.; Obtain ARR, growth, gross margin, net retention, pipeline, implementation revenue and customer concentration.

**GaiaLens** — GaiaLens has real institutional use, 10 reported clients, strong regulatory-innovation recognition and a core-fit capital history. The founder criterion fails, security assurance is unverified, the original CEO has departed, majority control sits with a non-founder, and named customer/reference evidence remains thin.
  - Found via: FinTech Scotland cluster directory
  - Check before the call: Current financing stage, full cap table and reconciliation of the reported £2m with share-premium filings; Reason for Gordon Tveito-Duncan's May 2025 departure and current leadership responsibilities

**Sygno** — Sygno has credible institutional problem-solution fit, strong explainability, material quantified outcomes and validation from TIN, ROM, FIS and Allianz. The thesis score remains low because founder pedigree misses the contract threshold, named production bank references are absent, and enterprise-grade security and developer evidence is private.
  - Found via: Holland FinTech network directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm cumulative capital raised and obtain the current cap table and founder ownership.; Obtain named bank and processor references, including Allianz go-live status and country rollout.

**Qoobiss** — Qoobiss has real revenue growth, a named production NBFI customer and an ADR-register-confirmed video-identification approval. The founding team is not publicly identifiable, stage is ambiguous after a private-equity event, security assurance is not public, and commercial/operating evidence remains Romania-heavy.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Qoobiss advertises QES-capable e-signature workflows, but Credimatic Network is not identified as a Romanian qualified trust-service provider; verify the third-party QTSP before relying on the claim.

  - Check before the call: Identify the human founders, their prior employers and current ownership/control alongside NCH Capital.; Clarify whether the 2024 NCH transaction is Seed-equivalent growth capital or a controlling private-equity investment.

**Advisory Direct** — The company is live and structurally in scope, but public evidence shows no Tier-1 founder pedigree, product-security proof, or institutional deployment. Government grants and B2B focus are positives, yet commercial and technical validation remain absent after eight years. Total capital raised is still unverified.
  - Found via: FinTech Scotland fintech directory
  - Check before the call: Obtain a complete cap table and cumulative financing history to resolve G4.; Verify Suhail Ahmad's unnamed investment-bank employers and tenure.

**CertiQi** — CertiQi has a differentiated federated-ledger architecture and its FCA Sandbox participation is real. Evidence quality falls sharply beyond that signal: founder biographies are unresolved, the patent family is not identified, security assurance is self-claimed, no production financial-institution customer is named, and the current entity has an overdue confirmation statement. The sandbox listing must not be read as FCA authorisation.
  - Licences: FCA Regulatory Sandbox accepted firm (2021-2025 cohorts)
  - Found via: FCA Regulatory Sandbox accepted firms
  - Check before the call: Obtain the complete founder and management biographies and reconcile the 2021–2025 FCA Sandbox entry with the UK entity incorporated in 2024.; Request the DotLedger patent-family numbers and independently verify the claimed Oracle, DocuSign, Seagate and LedgerDomain citations.

**Sustema** — Sustema is a live, independent and thesis-adjacent insurance data company with seasoned underwriting expertise, a recent second Lloyd's Lab selection and measurable pilot results. It falls short for Stratum3 because disclosed funding is stale, the team remains 2-10 people, customers and production deployments are unnamed, the technical/security footprint is closed, and there is no digital-asset bridge. No licence veto applies: Sustema is a decision-support data vendor, not an insurer, intermediary, payment institution or virtual-asset service provider.
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab Cohort 15
  - Check before the call: Total capital raised and current cap table remain undisclosed.; No named customer or referenceable production deployment was found.

**Javel Groupe** — Javel is narrowly and compellingly thesis-aligned: a founder with deep insurance wording experience, Lloyd’s Lab validation, real-data work with three named carriers, ten market collaborators, a named strategic client and a Lloyd’s strategic investment. The library score remains poor because almost all high-value founder, regulatory, technical-assurance and public-market thresholds are absent. The immediate investment question is whether KEN has crossed from impressive case studies/early adoption into repeatable paid production with defensible accuracy, security and economics.

  - Check before the call: Verify Lloyd’s investment amount, instrument, date, governance rights and whether Lloyd’s or any syndicate has a paid commercial contract.; Obtain signed Inigo/AXIS/Atrium references, distinguish case study, pilot, strategic programme and production, and quantify ARR/ACV/renewal terms.

**Easy Comply** — Easy Comply has detailed current documentation, useful security architecture and a genuinely accessible multi-tenant product. It misses the founder thesis, lacks named FI production proof and institutional backing, and its 2024 accounts showed zero revenue with negative equity. The public evidence supports a pass until paid traction and financial resilience are demonstrated.

  - Check before the call: Verify 2025–2026 revenue, cash balance, shareholder loans, runway and whether the named logos are paying production customers.; Obtain named bank/fintech references and reconcile the site's 40-country claim with contract-level customer evidence.

**FopsAI** — The founder's BNY operating background and institutional reconciliation product are thesis-relevant, with unusually clear B2B pricing and auditability architecture for a new company. Evidence of customer production, certified security, investors and total funding is not yet public, making this a monitor until those points are verified.

**KYC.hr** — The product and active Croatian entity are verifiable, but the business had zero employees and zero revenue in its first reporting period. There is no evidence of a Tier-1 founder, enterprise security posture, API platform, institutional deployment or external financing. Revisit only after a named FI production customer and a credible technical/compliance team emerge.

**ITUS Secure Technologies** — ITUS is a very early but thesis-relevant cyber-insurance data layer with unusually strong validation from Enterprise Ireland, NCSC and Lloyd’s Lab. It is alive, independent and core-cheque compatible. The score remains poor because there is no named insurer production deployment, no quantified loss/underwriting outcome, no public API/integration evidence, and no public security assurance for a platform that asks customers and insurers to trust continuous cyber-control data. Lloyd’s Cohort 16 is the reason to monitor rather than pass.

**Field39** — Field39 is unusually relevant payment plumbing with deep founder-domain experience, HSM/DUKPT capability, PCI DSS Level 1 evidence, multi-cloud deployment and named acquirer integrations. However, stage and total funding remain unknown, no Tier-1 institution founder pedigree is established, and no named production financial-institution customer is public. The strict score is poor despite technically credible infrastructure.

**FundsQ** — FundsQ has real institutional use, a public reference customer and an award-winning Indosuez collaboration, but it misses the founder-bank and digital-finance-rail pillars of the thesis. Current stage, capitalisation and security posture are also unresolved after a decade of operation, making it an unattractive Seed/Series A fit despite a functioning niche business.

**Modena Payments** — The verified entity is alive and has a real, active AIS/PIS licence with two passports, but the investable narrative is not presently diligence-grade. Public registry-derived data reports only €82.8k of 2025 revenue and one employee, with no named institutional customers or financing, while the new site materially overstates the verified licence footprint and retains obvious placeholders. Pass unless the company supplies regulator-cleared service mapping, contractual partner coverage and a verifiable production stack.

The website claims acquiring, money remittance, payment instruments, client-money holding, e-wallets, payouts and all 30 EEA states.; DISCLOSURE INTEGRITY: the website prints 'License No: [TBD]', 'Custodian bank list: TBD', placeholder uptime, an unpublished penetration test and certifications merely 'in progress'; it also calls SRINI OÜ the parent while the official register shows Sakari Ventures OÜ as 100% shareholder.
  - Found via: Estonian FSA payment institutions register
  - Check before the call: Obtain a regulator-reviewed legal opinion mapping every marketed service to permit 4.1-1/99 or to a named licensed partner.; Resolve why the site identifies SRINI OÜ as parent when the official register shows Sakari Ventures OÜ as sole shareholder.

**SLIX Finance** — SLIX is highly thesis-aligned at concept level and its Gate 1 DSS notice is genuine, but that notice permits non-live testing only and is not FCA authorisation. There is no public product proof, customer, API, security artefact, AML lead or financing history, while UK operating substance is not yet demonstrated. Monitor for Gate 2, a staffed compliance function and a named institutional pilot before meeting.

  - Found via: Bank of England Digital Securities Sandbox Dashboard (Gate 1 SANs)
  - Check before the call: Confirm actual UK staff, office substance and Andrii Zamovsky's formal role/equity.; Obtain cap table, financing history, stage and cash runway.

**easyReg** — The product is a live, institution-focused Swiss compliance SaaS, but it is narrowly domestic and founder-led with no Tier-1 FI founder pedigree. Public evidence shows neither named production customers nor institutional security/API maturity, while stage and capital remain unknown. The positive structure is insufficient to offset weak thesis-level founder, technology and traction signals.

  - Check before the call: Confirm current ARR, retention, named bank customers and production usage under NDA.; Obtain the cap table and total external capital raised.

**distriBind** — The domain insight and Lloyd's Lab credential are real, but the evidence set resolves overwhelmingly negative: no named production customer, no public security assurance, no recent financing and repeated filing lapses. **Vercly** — Vercly is an active, founder-controlled Polish RegTech with a verified €0.93m-equivalent PARP grant, a credible industry award and substantive KYB/AML product breadth. It lacks the fund's required founder pedigree, named financial-institution production references and public security assurance; funding stage and total also remain unresolved.

FCA checks found no licence, but no veto applies because Project Heath presents as a non-custodial workflow vendor rather than a fund manager, adviser or asset holder.

**spotixx** — spotixx is highly thesis-relevant, bootstrapped, growing and embedded in serious European AML collaboration. The signal score is held down by the contract's strict named-evidence thresholds: the four German bank participants are not named as customers, the new platform is still in pilot, partner Roseman Labs' certifications cannot be attributed to spotixx, and there is no public API/security artefact. Stage also remains non-standard: a 60+ person break-even services-and-software company is neither demonstrably profitable-at-scale nor demonstrably Seed/Series A.

  - Check before the call: Confirm 2024-2026 revenue, EBITDA and the share of recurring platform revenue versus managed-services revenue to resolve G3 and maturity.; Obtain named bank production references for the new encrypted-computing platform; the current careers page still calls it pilot phase.

**Blockaware** — Blockaware is eligible and thematically relevant, with a capable technical founding team and credible Tenity selection. However, the public product is still 'coming soon', the positioning has shifted from SMB crypto treasury to RWA tokenisation, and there is no public licence, security architecture, customer, integration or production evidence. The official ESMA register does not show a Blockaware CASP authorisation; because no live regulated service is established, this is a pre-launch diligence gap rather than a current licensing veto.
  - Found via: Tenity Switzerland Fall 2023 Incubation cohort
  - Check before the call: Identify and verify the Romanian legal entity, beneficial ownership and current trade-register status.; Clarify whether the product sells infrastructure to asset issuers or directly offers fractional investments to end investors.

**HAELO** — HAELO is finance-specific and highly capital-efficient, but its founder lacks the thesis-required Tier-1 financial-institution or digital-asset-infrastructure background. Public evidence shows a very small team, no security certification or technical transparency, and only a Tesco Bank proof of concept rather than production financial-institution traction. Three confirmed anti-signals reduce the blended score from 0.084 to the 0.000 floor.
  - Found via: FinTech Scotland fintech directory
  - Check before the call: Verify whether Tesco Bank moved beyond the documented proof of concept into a paid production contract.; Obtain the current cap table, grant schedule and any undisclosed angel or SEIS funding.

**Fortstock** — The product is a direct tokenisation-rails fit and has credible XRPL accelerator validation, recent grant capital and an experienced blockchain CTO. Yet the founder-bank criterion is unmet, deployment evidence remains Devnet-only, and the missing CASP/AML framework is a veto-level issue after the MiCA transition ended.
  - ⚠️ Review flags: Potential unlicensed regulated activity: Fortstock enables token issuance/on-chain lending but is absent from the 24 July 2026 ESMA CASP register and CBI register; legal perimeter must be resolved.
  - Found via: XRPL Accelerator cohort 6, run by Tenity, June 2025
  - Check before the call: Obtain Irish counsel's written MiCA, MiFID, lending and crowdfunding perimeter analysis and identify any licensed partners.; Clarify current founders, ownership, governance and the apparent transition from Zahzah/Mikheev to Yurov-led operations.

**Vynex Tech** — The trade-document verification problem is on thesis, and the founder's disclosed Deutsche Bank/LCH/LSE history is directionally relevant. The company is only two months old, however, with no commercial product, named customer, public technical proof or disclosed institutional capital, so it is too early for a meeting.
  - Found via: Level39 member directory
  - Check before the call: Verify the founder's exact roles and dates at Deutsche Bank, LCH and London Stock Exchange.; Resolve total capital raised and whether any investment instruments or founder loans are undisclosed.

**Sotera** — Sotera has an unusually relevant Lloyd's/Tokio investor and accelerator network, a credible senior insurance advisor and a differentiated item-risk data concept. It nevertheless remains a 1-10-person pilot-stage vendor with no named production customer, public security certification, API surface or recurring-revenue evidence. The corporate record is active, but undisclosed funding and conflicting UK/North-America head-office descriptions compound the diligence risk.
  - Found via: Lloyd's Lab Cohort 7
  - Check before the call: Reconcile the UK operating entity and London base with Lloyd's North America head-office label and confirm where management, IP and customer contracts sit.; Obtain the total cash raised, consideration behind 2024-2025 share allotments, latest lead investor and current cap table.

**Pytilia** — Pytilia is alive, founder-controlled, globally revenue-generating and officially verified as an FCA TechSprint participant. However, that programme is not authorisation, neither founder meets the Tier-1-FI requirement, and no named financial institution production deployment is public. Its services-led, multi-sector model is materially outside Stratum3's focused product-infrastructure thesis.
  - Licences: FCA Market Abuse Surveillance TechSprint participant
  - Found via: FinTech Scotland AI compliance challenge
  - Check before the call: Verify the claimed pre-seed stage, all grants/equity raised and current profitability from management accounts.; Obtain named VIGIL/LoopIQ financial-institution references, production status, ARR, gross margin and renewal data.

**Datakeen** — Datakeen is strategically relevant identity/KYC infrastructure with substantial claimed document volume, blue-chip bank logos, France FinTech membership and unusually concrete EUDI-wallet verifier evidence. The strict score is depressed by absent qualifying founder pedigree, private developer and assurance artefacts, weak customer references and an undisclosed funding total. Its non-qualified France Identité test status is not a lapsed licence: Datakeen is a software verifier, not a claimed QTSP, wallet issuer, payment institution or CASP.
  - Found via: France Identité EUDIW Unfold playground
  - Check before the call: Confirm total equity, grants and debt raised and whether the planned Series A closed after the EuroQuity profile.; Obtain production references from BNP Paribas and La Banque Postale with volumes, outcomes and renewal data.

**Haupper** — Haupper is a live, B2B Portuguese RegTech with long operating history, a modest PRR grant and clearly relevant workflows. Its founder history, total capital and customer base are opaque, and public evidence does not show the Tier-1 financial-institution DNA, technical assurance or digital-asset angle central to Stratum3. Pass unless diligence reveals material named FI deployments and an investable financing stage.
  - Found via: Portugal Fintech Report
  - Check before the call: Identify the legal founders, cap table, all equity/debt financing and current financing stage.; Obtain named financial-institution customers, production references, ARR, retention and deployment geographies.

**FeedOracle Technologies** — FeedOracle has an unusually broad and technically detailed live product for its age, including signed outputs, OpenAPI documentation, ISO 20022 validation, free testing and a public trust centre. However, every material claim is self-published: there is no incorporated company, financing evidence, team depth, independent security assurance, named customer, production financial institution or third-party validation. The confirmed anti-signals reduce the already-low weighted score to zero. It is too early and insufficiently institutionally validated for a meeting.
  - Found via: Blockchain Bundesverband member directory
  - Check before the call: Confirm legal incorporation plans, founder identity and ownership, and whether any trade-register or tax registration can be independently verified.; Obtain live customer references, signed contracts, ARR, usage, retention and production API telemetry.

**PROOViD** — PROOViD is a real, active Cyprus RegTech vendor with broad KYC functionality, global document/address coverage, a live enterprise portal and a current FXBO integration. Its licence position is not problematic because it sells compliance software and does not claim regulated payment, e-money or crypto activity. The investment case is nevertheless too opaque and weakly validated: no confirmed founders, institutional customers, funding history, security assurance or DPIA are public, while the product and website show little current momentum. The anti-signal deductions therefore eliminate its already-low weighted fit.
  - Found via: Cyprus Registrar company mirror
  - Check before the call: Confirm the actual founders, ownership, management roles, employee count and relevant bank, regulator, AML and engineering histories.; Request audited or management ARR, paying-customer count, customer concentration, retention, gross margin and verification volumes.
